| AD-66 | *Amended by AD-111 (`kanevas-import`, Monsieur's decisions of 2026-10-11): an attachment is a media library file, referenced by the section; secret denotes the file's visibility.* **What is served, and how**: an attachment's type is **determined by the server on upload, from the first bytes' signature** (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP); the browser's claimed type is ignored. A recognised image is served inline with its type; **everything else** (SVG, HTML, PDF…) is served as `application/octet-stream` with `Content-Disposition: attachment` and the encoded original name (`filename*`). Always: `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. Rejected: serving inline by extension (a .png file that is HTML); embedded PDF preview (outside this slice). |
| AD-67 | *Amended by AD-111 (`kanevas-import`, Monsieur's decisions of 2026-10-11): an attachment is a media library file, referenced by the section; secret denotes the file's visibility.* **No attachment list route**: they travel with the sheet, per section, already filtered by the reader's guard and mode (`{id, nom, taille, image, secrete}`; `secrete` returned only to the GM outside Player mode). A reader learns neither the count nor the existence of what they cannot read. Routes: add (`multipart`, one file per request, `secrete` field then `fichier` field: `secrete` sent after the file is ignored), mark (`secrete`), remove, and read the file (no mode parameter: the account's actual permission; Player mode only changes what the sheet shows). An account that cannot read the section, or a secret attachment for a non-GM: **404**, with the same body as an unknown id; someone who reads without writing receives **403** on add and remove, as does a non-GM setting or clearing secret status (like the first sheet's GM actions). The limit refusal (50 attachments) never states the number in the API; the screen tells only the GM. |
| AD-68 | **A map is read through a single function**, `lireCarte(compte, mode, carteId)`, which returns the map, its elements and, for a graph, its links — already filtered for that reader; the browser filters nothing. A map can be read if the account is a GM outside Player mode, or if it is **visible**; otherwise the address returns 404 like an unknown address (AD-22). An element is returned only if its sheet is readable (AD-38); a link only if its relation is readable (AD-64) and both ends are returned. No count, no marker of what is missing. A GM's Player mode on a non-visible map is not a 404: the screen says a player would not see it (AD-39). |
| AD-69 | **A map background** is an image (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, recognised by the first bytes' signature as in AD-66; any other file is refused) of **at most 25 MB** (clarifies AD-40 and departs from AD-7, which has no attachment limit: a background is displayed as-is by the browser), written through `stockage.ts` (AD-65). Replacing writes the new file, changes the row, then deletes the old one; there is no removal without replacement. Served through `GET /api/univers/:id/cartes/:carteId/fond`, which rechecks map readability on every request (account's actual permission, no mode), with AD-66 headers. Rejected: reusing `deposerPieceJointe` (a map has no section or secret to carry); no size limit (a background of several GB freezes the browser). |
| AD-70 | **Token position**: two numbers from 0 to 100, as percentages of the map frame's width and height, **rounded to two decimals**. The frame takes the background image's aspect ratio (16/10 without a background): a position keeps its meaning at any screen size and if the background is replaced by an image with the same ratio. Rejected: pixels (break on resizing). No version or lock: when two GMs move the same token, last write wins (a position is not content). |
| AD-71 | **Graph layout**: a pure browser function (`disposition.ts`, no dependency), deterministic — starts on a circle in title then id order, 300 iterations of force-directed placement (repulsion between nodes, attraction along links) — applied **only to the graph returned by the server**. Nodes a reader cannot see therefore have no influence on what they see: the layout betrays nothing. The GM does not move a node. Rejected: a graph library (dependency for 40 lines); storing the layout (a GM's positions would betray hidden nodes). |
| AD-72 | **Limits**: at most 100 elements per map, a sheet at most once per map, no map ceiling per universe. Adding an element requires a sheet **in the same universe**; on an illustrated map it carries a position, on a graph it does not (shape fixed at creation). Removing an element is a GM action that does not touch the sheet. Only the GM (outside Player mode) creates, configures, places, removes. |
| AD-95 | **Content is Markdown, rendered on the server** (`kanevas-import`, B-32; replaces AD-58). *Binds*: sections, session reports, scenarios, system entries; E-9, E-7, E-15, E-12; sections service. *Prevents*: two renderings that do not hide the same things (a browser filtering what it received itself); HTML from an author or import executing for a reader; three link syntaxes. *Rule*: store the **Markdown source**; a single server function renders it as HTML for a given reader and mode (AD-96), with `markdown-it` (15.0.2, MIT, checked in the npm index on 2026-10-11) in `html: false`, tables and strikethrough enabled, `breaks: true` (a single newline remains a break, like the previous plain text), links limited to `http`, `https`, `mailto` and sheet links, images and file links limited to the universe's media library files (AD-111), and the product's two extensions: `[[…]]` links (AD-97) and `:::mj` blocks (AD-98, `markdown-it-container` 4.0.0, MIT). The browser receives this HTML and inserts it as-is; the editor's **preview** goes through the same function (a preview route), never a second engine. Source is sent only to someone who can write the section (editing field). Existing plain-text content is read as Markdown, without rewriting. **Handling existing texts** (Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11): read as-is as Markdown, without a format column — the production database is empty. *Rejected*: rendering in the browser — it would require sending what it must not see, or a second server filter that understands Markdown, hence two engines; a rich editor (TipTap, ProseMirror) — a heavy dependency and a document format to maintain, for a GM already writing Markdown in Kanka; HTML allowed then sanitised — the sanitiser becomes the security boundary, `html: false` removes it. |
| AD-96 | **A content view per reader** (`kanevas-import`, B-33, B-34). *Binds*: sheet route, section route, session report route, assistant's `lire_section` and `lire_fiche` tools, search. *Prevents*: one path (assistant, preview, list) delivering a GM block or the title of an unreadable sheet that another hides. *Rule*: every content read goes through a service function `(compte, mode, section)` returning either HTML (screen) or filtered Markdown (assistant): for a non-GM or in Player mode, the source is `contenu_public` (without GM blocks, `docs/donnees.md`); each `[[…]]` link is resolved for that reader (AD-97); each file passes AD-111's double guard (otherwise « Image réservée au MJ. », « Fichier réservé au MJ. » or nothing). The assistant receives `[texte](fiche:42)` for a readable sheet and `texte` otherwise — never an unreadable sheet's id. *Rejected*: filtering at storage time by audience (one copy per audience) — audiences are per account (author), copies multiply. |
| AD-97 | **`[[…]]` links** (`kanevas-import`, B-33). *Binds*: E-9 editor, import API, `liens` table, « Mentionnée dans ». *Prevents*: a link breaking on rename; a link naming an unreadable sheet; an import depending on sheet order. *Rule*: stored form `[[<id>|<texte>]]`, text always present — on write, a `[[<id>]]` without text receives the target's current title, which the author can read (otherwise writing is refused: one cannot link to what one cannot read). The import writes `[[cle:<clé d'origine>|<texte>]]`: if the key is known in the universe, the server rewrites it as `[[<id>|…]]`; otherwise the link remains **pending**, and creating the sheet carrying that key rewrites the waiting content in the same transaction (`liens (cible_cle)` index) — a server rewrite, not a content write: `version`, `version_importee` and `modifie_le` do not move (AD-104). On each section write, the service recomputes its `liens` rows (target, pending, `dans_bloc_mj`). Rendering for a reader: readable target → link; unreadable target → text, without a marker; deleted or pending target → text, and for the GM only the « lien cassé » or « en attente » marker (`docs/ecrans.md`). **Outside a section**: in a **scenario**, links target sheets of the campaign's universe, are resolved on read by the same function and under the same permissions (AD-96), and a pending key is resolved on read through the source-key index; they have no row in `liens` and do not count in « Mentionnée dans »; in a **game system entry**, read by members of several universes whereas a sheet belongs to only one, the `[[…]]` extension is **not active**: the text stays as written. *Rejected*: linking by title (`[[Port-Sel]]`, Obsidian-style) — titles change and are not unique; a slug — an extra column for what the id and source key already do; a phantom sheet created for a pending link — it would appear in lists. |
| AD-98 | **The GM block in text** (`kanevas-import`, B-34). *Binds*: Markdown, search, links, attachments, assistant, section writes. *Prevents*: a secret leaking through a path other than the sheet — full-text search, incoming link, image, assistant, player overwrite. *Rule*: syntax `:::mj` … `:::` (Markdown container; a block within a block is removed through the outermost one, AD-114); on every write, the service computes `contenu_public` (source without GM block lines, based on positions of the block token returned by the engine, never a regular expression) and writes it in the same transaction; the search index indexes both, a non-GM queries only `contenu_public` (AD-101); a link written in a block is marked `dans_bloc_mj` and counts for a non-GM neither in rendering nor in « Mentionnée dans »; a file placed in a block disappears with the block for a non-GM; added from the block, it enters as « Réservé au MJ » (AD-111, Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11); a section containing a block is written only by a GM (403 for a player, even through the assistant), and a non-GM cannot write content containing one (400). Player mode: a player's view, as everywhere (AD-39). **GM block within text, adopted** (Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11): the 111 blocks in 58 Kanka sheets stay in place. Rejected: splitting each block into a GM-only section on import (a single permission model, but fragmented reading). *Rejected*: a block per player or audience — a second who-sees-what within the section (outside scope); an HTML comment `<!-- mj -->` — invisible in preview and rejected by `html: false`. |
| AD-99 | **Sheet hierarchy** (`kanevas-import`, B-35). *Binds*: E-8 (tree), E-9 (breadcrumb, « Contient », « Déplacer sous… »), import API. *Prevents*: a cycle; a breadcrumb naming an unreadable ancestor; two meanings of root. *Rule*: an optional parent, a sheet **in the same universe**, **of any type**; no cycle, checked by the service on every move (walking ancestors, at most 50 levels, refusal beyond that); the breadcrumb shows ancestors the reader sees, others are skipped; a type's tree has as roots sheets of that type with no parent, an unreadable parent or a parent of another type; deleting a sheet lifts its children under its parent. *Rejected*: a same-type parent only — Kanka places locations within worlds; a materialised path (`/1/7/42`) — to rewrite for a whole subtree on every move, for trees of a few hundred sheets; a recursive anti-cycle trigger — unreadable, and the service already checks the same universe. |
| AD-100 | *`kanevas-fiches-liees` (AD-131) : « Mentionnée dans » keeps the links and the free relations ; the natured relations are read in « Fiches liées ».* **Incoming links** (`kanevas-import`, B-36; lifts AD-64's rejection). *Binds*: E-9's « Mentionnée dans » block, sheet route. *Prevents*: an incoming link naming an unreadable source or quoting a GM block. *Rule*: the list of source sheets of relations and links targeting the sheet, each returned if its carrying section is readable by the reader (and, for a link, outside a GM block for a non-GM) and if the source sheet is readable; nothing otherwise, without a count. It travels with the sheet (one response), 50 then a next page. Indexes: `liens (cible_fiche_id)`, `relations (cible_fiche_id)`. *Rejected*: a separately maintained backlinks table — duplicates `liens` and `relations`. |
| AD-101 | **Search across all types, aliases and labels** (`kanevas-import`, B-37; extends AD-63). *Binds*: E-17, E-8, assistant's `chercher` tool. *Prevents*: all-types search becoming a second function with its own permission filter. *Rule*: AD-63 stands — search is the list plus a condition, ordered by title, without relevance or snippets; type becomes an optional filter, label a second filter; a sheet matches if all words are in its title or aliases, or in the same readable section; a non-GM and Player mode query only `titre` and `contenu_public` (FTS5 column filter), a GM `titre` and `contenu`. The assistant's `chercher` tool calls this function instead of looping over seven types (AD-74 amended on this point). |
| AD-102 | **Sheet types: a product list, configured at two levels** (`kanevas-import`, B-42; replaces AD-52; Monsieur's decisions of 2026-10-11) `[ADOPTED]` in principle. *Binds*: sidebar, E-8, E-9, E-14, E-15, E-17, import API, assistant. *Prevents*: an added type costing a migration; eight type lists maintained by hand (verified: `src/services/types.ts`, `services/fiches.ts`, `frontend/src/ecrans/fiche/types-fiche.ts`, `frontend/src/items.ts`, `ecrans/carte/types.ts`, `liste-recherche.tsx` and migration 0001 each copy it); two definitions of enabled type. *Rule*: **a single list**, in a module shared by backend and frontend: for each type, its id, labels (singular, plural, « Nouveau … », « Aucun … »), icon, payload (AD-6); today, in order: `personnage`, `lieu`, `faction`, `objet`, `evenement`, `quete`, `creature`, `race`, `vaisseau`, `savoir`, `recit`, `regle`, `note`, and `compte_rendu` (not configurable). The seven new types have payload `{"v":1}`. The sheets table is **rebuilt once** without a type `CHECK` (`docs/donnees.md`); the service refuses any type outside the list. **Activation**: effective(universe, type) = the universe's forced setting if any, otherwise the attached system's setting if any, otherwise enabled; a single service function computes it, read by the sidebar, E-8, E-17 and API. A disabled type is neither offered at creation nor listed in the sidebar; **it hides no data**: its sheets remain readable, linkable, searchable, and the import API accepts them (the batch warns). **Kanka mapping** (carried by the script, unknown to Kanevas): character → character, location → location, organisation → faction, item → item, event → event, quest → quest, creature → creature, race → race, spaceship → spaceship, world → knowledge (Kanka stores general knowledge there: history, phenomena, resources, flora, chronology, canon; Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11), Kanka's free-text type field → subtype (AD-110), story → story, rule → rule, note → note, journal → session report (within a campaign), scenario → scenario (dedicated table, AD-29), map → an ordinary sheet with its image (maps in the API: another epic, AD-104). **« Savoir »** replaces the proposed « Monde » type (Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11): no « monde » type; chronology as a separate type (a timeline) would belong to a more advanced epic. *Rejected*: user-created types (rejected by Monsieur); per-campaign selection (rejected by Monsieur); keeping the `CHECK` and rebuilding it for every type (one rebuild migration per type); `PRAGMA writable_schema` to rewrite the `CHECK` (possible corruption, unsupported). Vocabulary note: the **« Règle »** type (a universe's rules lore) and **« Créature »** coexist with a system's « Règles » and « Créatures » tabs (E-15, templates); they do not mix — a sheet belongs to the universe, an entry to the system. |
| AD-103 | **The API token** (`kanevas-import`, B-45, B-46). *Binds*: E-18, import API, session guard. *Prevents*: a token opening the interface or another universe; a token surviving loss of role; a token value readable in the database or logs. *Rule*: value `kvs_` + 32 random bytes in base64url, shown **once** at creation; the database stores its SHA-256 (fingerprint comparison, unique `jetons_api (empreinte)`); scope: **one universe**, the actor is the GM who created it; on every request the guard checks known fingerprint, not revoked, not expired, and that this account is **still a GM** of the universe; otherwise **401** with a single body « `{message: "Jeton invalide.", code: "jeton_invalide"}` » (the reason is not stated); a universe other than the token's: 404. The token is accepted **only** under `/api/import/v1/*` (a separate Fastify scope, sibling of the session guard — verified: the guard is an encapsulated scope, `src/routes/session.ts:91-97`) and the session cookie is **not** accepted there; conversely, no other route reads the `Authorization` header. `dernier_usage_le` is written at most once per minute. Lifetime: 7, 30 (default), 90 or 365 days; revocation by any GM of the universe. *Rejected*: a multi-universe token per account — a script error would write to the wrong universe; a token opening the whole API — the interface does not need it and every route would need two guards; a token without expiry — one forgotten on a Mac stays valid forever; OAuth / Authelia token — Authelia does not currently issue a machine access token for this application, and permissions are per universe (AD-9). |
| AD-104 | **The import API, generic and idempotent** (`kanevas-import`, B-47 to B-50; Monsieur's decision: Kanevas does not know Kanka). *Binds*: Monsieur's script, any future source; services for sheets, sections, media library files, relations, campaigns, scenarios. *Prevents*: duplicates on a second pass; an import overwriting what the group has written since; a second implementation of writes (AD-2); a contract drifting from slice to slice. *Rule*: contract under the import API section below; every write **calls the routes' service functions**, with the token's actor (AD-2), plus an ensure function per object that looks up by source key and creates, updates or does nothing; « inchangée » if nothing differs (neither `version` nor `modifie_le` moves); **nothing a human changed in Kanevas since the last import is overwritten** (B-48): field by field, the current value is compared with what the last import wrote — `dernier_import` of the sheet (title, type and PC flag, subtype, parent, aliases, labels), section (title, audience), campaign (name, status), scenario (title) and **file** (name, visibility), with illustration (key) in the sheet's and the set of attached files (keys) in the section's, `version_importee` for section and scenario content —; thus visibility changed in Kanevas (a file reserved for the GM on E-19) **is never restored** on replay, an illustration replaced or an attached file added or removed manually is left alone (the attached set is compared as a whole); a file's **bytes** cannot be edited in Kanevas: a different fingerprint for the same key replaces the bytes and counts as updated; a field the import could not write is not compared as modified: a **pending parent** (missing target, or one that would create a cycle) keeps the requested parent and written parent in `dernier_import`, and every replay retries setting it once the target exists, like a pending link; an equal field takes the new value, a different field is left alone and the batch warns (« titre modifié dans Kanevas : non écrasé »); an assistant edit counts as human. Explicit limitation: a relation **removed** in Kanevas is recreated by a replay that still sends it (the batch counts it as « créée »); a file **deleted** from the media library likewise returns — deleting a sheet falls under AD-105; a section created in Kanevas, without a key, is never touched; an imported section absent from a new submission is left alone (nothing is deleted by omission; deletion uses `DELETE`). Every write belongs to a **batch** opened by the script; the batch **counts by object kind** — sheets, files, campaigns, scenarios, relations: created, updated, unchanged, refused — plus warnings and the number of **pending links and references** at closure; it keeps the report (1,000 entries). One request is one transaction: a refused sheet writes nothing of itself. Unknown parent or relation target: the sheet is written without it, with a warning (the script sends them in order, or replays). **An unknown campaign** for a session report (its campaign is mandatory, AD-61): the sheet is **refused** (400 `invalide`, `champ: campagne`), nothing of it is written, the batch counts a refusal — the script pushes campaigns first, or replays. **Pending link and « inchangée »**: a section is compared using the source **as sent by the script, after resolving known keys** (each `[[cle:k|t]]` whose key identifies a sheet is compared as `[[<id>|t]]`, each `(fichier:cle:K)` whose key identifies a file as `(fichier:<id>)`; a reference still pending is compared as sent, since it is stored as-is); likewise a sheet's `illustration` and a section's `fichiers`, compared after resolving known keys; rewriting a pending link or **file reference** when its target arrives (AD-97, AD-111) **is not a human modification**: it changes neither `version`, `version_importee` nor `modifie_le`, and does not count for B-48. An identical replay therefore returns « 0 créée, N inchangées ». **Maps are not in the import API** (Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11): a more advanced solution is needed, in another epic; a Kanka map is imported as an ordinary sheet, its image in the media library. *Rejected*: delete everything then recreate everything on every pass — loses what the group wrote and changes all identifiers (session report links break); an archive format sent as a whole (Kanka or custom zip) — Kanevas would learn a format, and a mid-import failure is all or nothing; direct database writes by the script — bypasses permissions (AD-2) and the volume is unreachable from the Mac. |
| AD-105 | **Deleting a sheet, deleting a universe** (`kanevas-import`, B-40, B-41; partly lifts the nothing-can-be-deleted exclusion). *Binds*: E-9, E-14, import API (sheet `DELETE`), storage. *Prevents*: an orphan row; a file served after deletion; universe deletion through a stray click. *Rule*: one service function per action, one transaction, GM only (outside Player mode); sheet: lift its children under its parent, then remove relations and map elements targeting it, its sections (and by cascade their relations, file references, links, proposals; media library files stay, AD-111), the sheet; links targeting it become broken (`ON DELETE SET NULL`). Universe: everything hanging off it, tokens and batches included; the game system remains. `RESTRICT` and no-action foreign keys stay as a safety net (`docs/donnees.md`). Disk files are removed **after** commit. Universe: confirmation by entering the exact name (E-14). **No tombstone** (Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11): a sheet deleted in Kanevas returns on replay if the script still sends it; to keep it from returning, delete it in the export. *Rejected*: a trash bin or soft deletion — an extra column and filter in every read, for an undo need Monsieur has not expressed; an empty-universe action reserved for a universe marked as a test — one more flag; deleting the test universe suffices for journey P-10; changing `RESTRICT` to `CASCADE` — rebuilding three more tables, and losing the safety net. |
| AD-106 | **Ceiling for all Markdown text: 100,000 characters** (`kanevas-import`, B-43; amends AD-91, AD-62, AD-85). *Binds*: sections service, `limites.contenuSection`, editor, proposals (AD-80 uses the same constant). *Prevents*: an import truncating 6 Kanka sheets (the longest has 61,000 characters). *Rule*: the single constant rises to 100,000 and applies to **all Markdown text** (AD-95): section and session report text, scenario, system entry, proposed content (AD-80) — the same text has only one ceiling, and a Kanka scenario is not refused where a section passes; nothing else changes. The import API's JSON request body is raised to 8 MiB (Fastify default: 1 MiB, `src/app.ts:23-25`). *Rejected*: splitting on import — six sheets read in fragments; no ceiling — content of several MB would block the editor and index. |
| AD-107 | **The original date** (`kanevas-import`, B-50). *Binds*: import API, E-13, E-9. *Prevents*: 36 imported session reports dated on import day, losing their order. *Rule*: the API may supply `creeLe` (and `modifieLe`) for a sheet on creation; it is the sheet's date, without an extra column; it is never in the future (refusal); the interface does not set it. *Rejected*: an original-date column alongside `cree_le` — two dates, only one determines order, and which one to display. |
| AD-108 | *Replaced by AD-111 (media library, Monsieur's decisions of 2026-10-11): every file, image or document, is a referenced media library file.* **An image within text is an attachment of its section** (`kanevas-import`, B-44). *Binds*: Markdown rendering, E-9's « Pièces jointes » block, import API. *Prevents*: an image leaking outside its section's audience; a second bytes route. *Rule*: `![légende](piece:<id>)` (the import writes `piece:cle:<clé>`, rewritten when the attachment arrives); an image targets only an attachment **of the same section**; it is served through AD-67's route, under its guard; an attachment referenced from a GM block is marked secret on write; an attachment referenced by text also stays in the « Pièces jointes » block. The limit of 50 attachments per section stands (`[TO VERIFY]`: image count per section in the export, which the script can count; above 50, raise the limit for imported sections). No media library (`docs/parcours.md`, outside scope). *Rejected*: a universe media library — an extra entity and audience, for 385 MB of images no sheet displays; images from an external address — leaks the visit to a third party (AD-92). |
| AD-109 | **The assistant token set through administration** (`kanevas-import`, B-51) — included, final independent slice (Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11). *Binds*: E-5, assistant availability (AD-77), volume. *Prevents*: a token readable in a response; two contradictory token sources. *Rule*: reused from Antre-du-maitre (`backend/src/services/claude-token.ts`, `routes/claude-token.ts`) — the server starts `claude setup-token` (the binary bundled by the Agent SDK) under `script` (pseudo-terminal, without `node-pty`), shows the authorisation address to the admin, writes the received code, retrieves the token; one session at a time, at most 10 minutes; the token goes into a 0600 file on the volume (`/data/claude/oauth-token`) and **takes precedence** over `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`; it is never returned (the screen says only whether it is set and since when). Routes under `/api/instance/` (AD-86 guard: 404 outside the `parents` group). In stub mode: a fake `setup-token` returning a fixed address and accepting code `bouchon`. `script`: verified by Antre-du-maitre in the `node:20-bookworm-slim` image (claude-token.ts:12-14, 2026-08-06), which is Kanevas's runtime base (`Dockerfile:26`); `script` comes from `bsdutils`, an essential Debian package: confirmed, no installation. *Rejected*: keeping OpenBao alone — every setup or renewal requires Monsieur to access the vault and restart the pod. |
| AD-110 | **Subtype, free text and specific to the type** (`kanevas-import`, B-52; Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11). *Binds*: E-8 (card, filter, creation), E-9 (header, « Changer le type »), E-17, import API. *Prevents*: a closed subtype list to maintain; one type's subtypes suggested for another; a filter naming a subtype carried only by unreadable sheets. *Rule*: an optional text column on the sheet (1 to 60 characters), free input, never an entity; **type-specific** — cleared when the type changes; completion suggests distinct subtypes of the same type in the universe (case-insensitive; the GM sees all, reading every sheet); E-8's filter suggests only those of sheets the reader sees, with their count; never secret (at title level, like aliases and labels); searched by E-17 with the title; protected by B-48; carried by the API (`sousType`, Kanka's free-text type field). Labels remain cross-cutting. *Rejected*: a subtype table per type (an entity and management screen for free text); subtypes as labels (labels are cross-cutting, a subtype is specific to its type). |
| AD-111 | **The media library: all the universe's files, with their own permissions, referenced, under a double guard** (`kanevas-import`, B-24, B-44, B-53 to B-56; Monsieur's decisions of 2026-10-11 — a media library, against the recommendation not to build one; **all** files, images and documents, against the proposal to split by kind; replaces AD-108). *Binds*: E-19, E-9 (text, « Pièces jointes » block, illustration), E-8 (thumbnails), E-12, rendering (AD-96), storage (AD-65), import API. *Prevents*: a player seeing a file through a sheet when it is closed to them, or the reverse; two copies of the same file with diverging permissions; two homes for files; a deletion leaving sheets pointing into the void without the GM knowing. *Rule*: a **file** belongs to a universe and carries its **visibility** — `joueurs` (default) or `mj`; two levels (a per-player level would duplicate the section author, AD-19: rejected). Its **kind** (image or document) is deduced from its type, determined by the server from the signature (AD-66): PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP are images, everything else a document. It is **referenced**: in text, `![légende](fichier:<id>)` (an image) or `[texte](fichier:<id>)` (any file, a download link) — the import writes `fichier:cle:<clé>`; an already-known key is rewritten as `fichier:<id>` on write, an unknown key stays as-is, **pending**, and a `fichiers_attente` row (universe, key, section or sheet, manner: text, attached, illustration) locates it; the file's arrival (`PUT /fichiers/:cle`) rewrites these texts, sets the waiting attached references and illustration, in the same transaction, without changing `version`, `version_importee` or `modifie_le` (AD-104), like a pending link (AD-97); an imported sheet's `illustration` or `fichiers` naming an unknown key wait likewise, with a batch warning (never a refusal); a pending reference renders as « Fichier en attente d'import : « K » » for a GM, nothing for a player; **attached** to a section (a reference row); a sheet's **illustration** (a column, images only). **Two add paths** (B-55): from a sheet, adding a file creates it in the media library **and** sets its reference **in a single transaction** (one service function, called by E-9's upload route and the assistant's image tool, AD-44); or choose an existing file (reference only). **Double read guard**, in the per-reader view (AD-96): shown if the container is readable (section, and GM block for a non-GM; sheet for an illustration) **and** the file is readable (GM outside Player mode: every file; otherwise: `joueurs`, **or added by this account** — a third read rule, not a visibility level: whoever added a file always sees it, in the library, through its route and in the sheet, double guard included, Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11); readable container and closed file → « Image réservée au MJ. » / « Fichier réservé au MJ. » (it says a file is there, not what it contains: Monsieur's decision); closed container → nothing. The file is served through its own route (`GET /api/univers/:id/fichiers/:fid`, `?v=<jeton>`) guarded by **its** visibility alone, with AD-66's rules (image inline, everything else downloaded under its name, `nosniff`, `sandbox`) and AD-93's cache (token = disk name); the media library (E-19) lists what this guard opens; « Utilisé dans » and E-19's « Usage » filter consider only usages the reader can read — the illustration of a sheet they see, a reference in a section they read and, for a non-GM, outside a GM block —: neither a closed section's title, presence within a GM block, nor the existence of a hidden usage is exposed (D1). An **existing** file placed in a GM block does not change visibility: the block guard suffices; a file **added** from a sheet takes visibility from its add location (below). Writes: a GM adds, renames, changes visibility, deletes; **whoever writes a section** adds a file there or references a file they see, and removes a reference there; **the visibility of a file added from a sheet follows the add location**: inside a GM block, or in a section not all players read (« Les joueurs la lisent » off, even if its author reads it), it enters as « Réservé au MJ »; elsewhere, « Visible des joueurs »; an illustration added from the header enters as « Visible des joueurs » if the sheet has at least one section read by players, « Réservé au MJ » otherwise (Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11); choosing an existing file never changes its visibility (B-55's warning applies); the `joueurs` default applies only to direct addition in the media library (E-19) and import (the script sends `mj` for what the source uses only in GM content); only the GM deletes a file. Deletion: the row, its attached references, illustrations targeting it (set to null), then the disk file after commit; `(fichier:<id>)` references remain in texts and render as « supprimé » (GM) or nothing. **At most 100 attached files** per section (instead of 50 attachments); no limit on in-text references or file count in a media library; no size ceiling (AD-7). **Migration**: each attachment becomes a media library file (`mj` if `secrete`, `joueurs` otherwise) attached to its section, each illustration a `joueurs` file set as illustration — bytes stay where they are, under the same name; the emptied attachments table is no longer read and is dropped in a later slice (expand, migrate, contract). A map background stays specific to its map (AD-69; maps are another epic). *Amends* AD-7, AD-35 to AD-37, AD-65 to AD-67 (an attachment is a reference; secret denotes file visibility; the list travels with the sheet and in E-19). *Rejected*: copying the file into each sheet (two permissions for one file, Monsieur's decision); splitting by kind, images in the library and documents as attachments (two homes, rejected by Monsieur); a GM-only default (rejected by Monsieur: the script carries responsibility for marking GM files, API contract); deriving visibility from usages (a file would change permissions when placed elsewhere); an embedded PDF preview (AD-66). |
| AD-122 | **An adventure is a sheet** (`kanevas-scenarios`, decision of Monsieur, 2026-10-25 ; replaces AD-29, retires AD-30 and AD-62). *Binds* : the type list (AD-102), E-8, E-9, E-3, E-13, the import API, the assistant, the migration. *Prevents* : a second content-bearing entity that must re-implement links, relations, files, illustration, search, tree and import — the four gaps the import of a real campaign showed (no incoming link to a scenario, no image, no relation towards it, no tree). *Rule* : (1) campaigns, scenarios and one-shots are rows of `fiches` with `type = aventure`, a `reglable` type of the product list, icon `flag`, labels « Aventure(s) », « Nouvelle aventure », « Aucune aventure » ; (2) the sub-type is free (B-52) — the product proposes « Campagne » and « Scénario », nothing depends on the exact string except the E-3 block « Campagnes en cours », which reads `sous_type = 'Campagne'` without case ; (3) a scenario belongs to its campaign by `parent_id` (AD-115), a one-shot stands at the root ; the tree is the default view of the type's list ; (4) no rule of the sheets is bent for the type : audience by section (every adventure's sections are born closed, campaign included — the GM decides what the players see, decision of Monsieur 2026-10-25 ; the creation form may open « Présentation »), deletion, type change (`changerTypeFiche` reads `reglable`, not a hard-coded type), search, links, relations, files, illustration ; (5) deactivated (E-14/E-15), the type behaves exactly as any deactivated type, nothing more : the bar entry gone, creation refused (E-8, `creer_fiche`, the import warns `type_desactive`), existing adventures readable and writable with their blocks and their status, the E-3 blocks unchanged — no data is hidden. *Rejected* : **keep scenarios apart and give them links, images, relations and a tree** — every function of the sheets would exist twice, and each new one would have to be added twice, for ever ; **two types « Campagne » and « Scénario »** — a one-shot fits neither, and the tree needs no type to say « belongs to » ; the sub-type carries the distinction and a third kind costs nothing ; **a type the GM cannot deactivate** — nothing requires it, `reglable` keeps E-14/E-15 uniform. |
| AD-123 | **The play status is a charge of the sheet** (`kanevas-scenarios`). *Binds* : `fiches.charge` of the type, the `PATCH` route, E-8, E-9, E-17, E-3, the import, B-59. *Prevents* : a status column on `fiches` that every other type would carry empty ; a status per sub-type that the import and the filters would have to know. *Rule* : (1) `charge = {v:1, statut}` with `statut ∈ en_preparation | prete | en_cours | terminee`, validated by the type's schema at creation (default `en_preparation`), at change and at import ; the base checks JSON only ; (2) one route changes it : `PATCH /api/univers/:id/fiches/:fid {statut}`, GM out of player mode, 400 on another type or another value, 403 for a player who reads the sheet or the GM in player mode, 404 for one who does not (the services' usual guards) ; written whole, no version — the last write wins ; (3) read by whoever reads the sheet (the badge is public, like PJ/PNJ) ; `GET .../fiches?statut=` filters by `json_extract` (400 « Le statut nécessite le type aventure et une valeur valide. » without `type=aventure` or with an unknown value; only the screen's address ignores an unknown value) ; (4) one vocabulary of **four** statuses for every adventure — « En préparation », « Prête », « En cours », « Terminée » (the former `active` reads `en_cours`) — decided by Monsieur on 2026-10-25 over the three first asked (« préparation », « prête », « jouée ») : E-3 and the players need « En cours » to tell the campaign being played from the ones done. *Rejected* : **a status per sub-type** (« Jouée » for a scenario, « Active » for a campaign) — two enumerations to validate, filter and import for one notion ; a campaign that is « En cours » for months and a scenario « En cours » for one evening are the same state at a different scale ; **three statuses only** (« préparation », « prête », « jouée », as first asked) — E-3 and the players need « En cours » to tell the campaign being played from the ones done ; reopened if a campaign's « En cours » proves useless ; **a status on the sections** — the status is the adventure's. |
| AD-124 | **Blocks of the sheet, by type** (`kanevas-scenarios` ; amends AD-46). *Binds* : E-9, `frontend/src/ecrans/fiche/`, the preparation and comptes-rendus services, B-60. *Prevents* : a special screen for the adventure (E-6 rebuilt under another name) ; blocks of the suivi glued into a section. *Rule* : (1) beside the section blocks (registry `fiche/registre.ts`, per section, every type), E-9 has a registry of **sheet blocks**, each declaring the types it serves, the roles, a rank ; they render between the header lines and the sections ; (2) the adventure has two : « Préparation » (GM, hidden in player mode) and « Comptes-rendus » (every member, with « Nouveau compte-rendu ») ; the « Contient » block already lists the children ; (3) the preparation tasks hang on the sheet (`taches_preparation.fiche_id`, `ON DELETE CASCADE`), routes under `/api/univers/:id/fiches/:fid/taches`, guard AD-47 on the adventure's universe ; (4) a compte-rendu names an adventure in `charge.campagne_id` (name kept, AD-61 unchanged otherwise) — campaign or scenario, the one it was created from ; it shows that adventure (E-13, the « Campagne » line) only to a reader who reads it, never its title otherwise ; (5) the deletion bilan of an adventure (AD-119) counts its tasks, its scenarios sent to the root and the CR left without adventure. *Rejected* : **a section block** (per section) for the suivi — a campaign's tasks are not a section's ; **an « E-6 » page for adventures** — one sheet screen, or the type is not a sheet ; **a foreign key from the CR's charge** — JSON ; the dangling CR is wanted (« Aventure supprimée »), as a CR survives its author. |
| AD-125 | **The import pushes adventures through the sheets route** (`kanevas-scenarios` ; amends AD-121 / B-48). *Binds* : `/api/import/v1`, the Kanka/Notion script (outside the product), E-18, B-61. *Prevents* : two import contracts for one entity ; a status overwritten by a reimport. *Rule* : (1) `PUT …/fiches/:cle` accepts `type: aventure` with `sousType`, `parent`, sections ; `statut` (optional, default `en_preparation`, validated: 400 `invalide` otherwise or on another type), `parent` by origin key (already supported), sections ; `statut`, like `sousType` and `parent`, is marked in `dernier_import` and never overwrites a value changed in Kanevas (B-48) ; (2) a compte-rendu keeps `campagne: <clé>` — the key of the adventure it names ; (3) the routes `PUT …/campagnes/:cle` and `…/campagnes/:cle/scenarios/:scle` and their counters disappear from the contract (`GET` of the base says the types and limits) ; the lot counts adventures among the sheets. *Rejected* : **aliases of the old routes** — a second path to write the same row, to maintain for a script that has one author (Monsieur's) who adapts it in one evening ; **a generic `charge` field in the import** — opens the whole JSON to the script ; `pj` and `statut` are named fields, validated. |
| AD-126 | **The assistant creates a sheet, not a campaign** (`kanevas-scenarios` ; replaces AD-31 ; built by `kanevas-scenarios-suivi` (2) and `kanevas-scenarios-import-assistant` (1, 3)). *Binds* : `services/assistant/catalogue.ts`, the stub, E-12, B-62. *Prevents* : two tools that die with the tables ; a tool per type. *Rule* : (1) one GM tool `creer_fiche(type, titre, sousType?, parent?)`, type among the activated ones, that calls the sheets' creation function (AD-2) ; the event in the conversation leads to the sheet ; (2) `creer_campagne`, `creer_scenario` and `lister_campagnes` disappear ; « list the campaigns » is `chercher` with the type ; (3) the stub answers the same two sentences (« crée une campagne « X » », « crée un scénario « X » dans « Y » ») through `creer_fiche`, so the assistant's recipes stay playable. *Rejected* : **keep `creer_campagne` as an alias** — a second tool that does a subset of the first ; **a tool per type** — thirteen tools for one gesture. |
| AD-127 | **One migration converts the suivi, then drops its tables** (`kanevas-scenarios`). *Binds* : the migration mechanism (AD-51), `docs/donnees.md` « Cadrage `kanevas-scenarios` », the stub seed. *Prevents* : two sources for one campaign during a transition ; a product that reads both tables « for a while ». *Rule* : one script, played at start-up after backup : campaigns and scenarios become sheets (status mapped, `active → en_cours`, « Présentation » open for a campaign that was `active` or `terminee` and closed for one in preparation, the scenario's text in a closed section), tasks rehung, CR charges rewritten, `campagnes` and `scenarios` dropped, origin keys kept (prefixed on collision) ; checks and the example base as `docs/donnees.md` says. The old addresses answer « Page introuvable. ». *Rejected* : **a transition period with both models as a steady state** — nothing in production to protect (no campaign imported), and two readers of one notion is the defect this epic removes ; the plan's first tranche does coexist with the old suivi until the second lands, by construction of vertical slices (the type is recetted alone, the pivot comes next), and that is the only transition ; **a redirection table from the old addresses** — nobody has them bookmarked. |
| AD-128 | **A sheet is created whole: parent and first sections in the same call** (`kanevas-scenarios-type`). *Binds* : `POST /api/univers/:id/fiches`, `creerFiche`, the « Nouvelle aventure » window (E-8), later `creer_fiche` (AD-126). *Prevents* : a creation window that chains three calls (create, add the section, move) and leaves a half-made sheet when the second fails ; a type-specific creation route. *Rule* : (1) `POST .../fiches` accepts, besides `type`, `titre`, `charge`, `sousType`, an optional `parent` (a sheet id, readable by the GM, put through `deplacerFiche`, AD-115) and an optional `sections` — at most 10 `{titre, joueursLisent?}` created **empty** through `ajouterSection`, the first switch of the audience set by `changerAudience` when `joueursLisent` is true (default false: born closed, B-8) ; (2) everything runs in one transaction (a refusal of the parent, a section or the audience writes nothing) ; (3) the status of an adventure is not a parameter here : it is born `en_preparation` (AD-123) ; (4) `PATCH .../fiches/:fid` takes `statut` (alone, or with the other fields in the same transaction), through `changerStatutFiche` in `services/fiches.ts` : GM out of player mode, `invalide` (400) for a sheet whose type has no status or a value out of the four, `modifie_le` untouched (the status is play state, not text) ; (5) `GET .../fiches?statut=` is a condition of `listerFiches` (`json_extract(f.charge, '$.statut')`), 400 on an unknown value, and 400 without `type=aventure` (the only type with a status). *Rejected* : **the window composes the calls** — a failure between two leaves a sheet nobody asked for, and the assistant would copy the sequence ; **a `presentation: true` flag** — it names a section of one type in a generic route ; **`statut` in the creation body** — the product creates in preparation, the import (AD-125) is the one that sets it at birth. |
| AD-129 | **« Déplacer sous… » walks the hierarchy of the universe, and never offers the sheet's own subtree** (`kanevas-deplacer-arborescence`, B-35, decided by Monsieur on 2026-10-29 ; within AD-99 and AD-115, which are unchanged). *Binds* : E-9 « Déplacer sous… », `GET .../fiches/racines`, `GET .../fiches/:fid/enfants`, the `horsDe` filter of `GET .../fiches`. *Prevents* : a tree view that cannot tell where it may go down ; a « root » of the box that differs from what « Aucun parent » does ; a search that offers a descendant the move will then refuse ; an exclusion that a player could use to probe the hierarchy above sheets he does not read. *Rule* : (1) the box's root level is the sheets with `parent_id` null — exactly where « Aucun parent » puts a sheet ; it is read by `GET .../fiches/racines`, for the GM outside player mode only, who reads every sheet, so it is the AD-99 root without the type criterion and not a second sense of it ; a type's tree (AD-115) keeps its own roots. (2) A lower level is « Contient » (`enfants`), whose rows gain `aDesEnfants`, computed under the caller's reading (AD-115 (3)) and over **every** type — unlike the tree of a type, where it means a child of the same type. (3) `horsDe=<fid>` on the search leaves out the sheet and its descendants inside the query (so pages stay full and the cursor right) ; the descendants are gathered by `parent_id`, one level per read, at most 50 levels as AD-115 (2), never by recursive SQL ; GM outside player mode only. (4) The tree view leaves out the sheet's descendants by not going down into the sheet (shown, not choosable) ; the server still refuses a cycle (AD-115 (1)), the last word when the hierarchy moved meanwhile. *Rejects* : `parent=racine` without `type` on the list — the list's `parent` is the tree of a type, with its own root rule and page of 100 ; one route with two root rules is what AD-99 forbids ; filtering the descendants in the page — a page of 100 could come back empty while results remain, and the page would need the subtree anyway ; the root open to players — no screen needs it, and for a reader who does not read everything « no parent » and « no readable parent » part ways, the second sense of root AD-99 forbids. |
| AD-130 | **The search knows the links and the tree** (`kanevas-fiches-liees` ; extends AD-63, AD-101, AD-118 ; precises AD-115 for the descent). *Lie* : `listerFiches`, E-17, the block « Fiches liées », B-65. *Empêche* : a second search function for the linked sheets ; an unbounded recursion on the tree ; a count that reveals a relation the reader cannot read. *Règle* : (1) AD-63 holds — the search stays the list plus conditions : `lie=<fid>` adds `EXISTS` a relation whose target is that sheet (or, with `descendants=1`, one of its descendants in the tree), whose section is readable by the reader and whose source is the candidate sheet — for a symmetric nature, whichever of the two sides is that sheet ; `nature=<key>` restricts to it ; `etat=la|disparu` reads `charge.disparu` ; (2) **the descent of the tree is a recursive CTE bounded to 50 levels** (`PROFONDEUR_MAX`), on `fiches_par_parent` — AD-115 forbade recursive SQL for the ascent (which traverses unreadable ancestors one by one) ; the descent only needs the ids, the readability is applied to the candidate sheet, not to the intermediate places, which are never named by the search ; (3) `lie` on a sheet the reader does not read answers as if the sheet did not exist (empty, 200). *Rejets* : **a loop per level in the application** for the descent — fifty round trips for one filter ; **naming the intermediate places** in the result — a path through a hidden place ; the block « Fiches liées » names the direct place of each line only (`via`), under the same guard. |
| AD-131 | **« Fiches liées » is a block of the sheet, read backwards from the natures** (`kanevas-fiches-liees` ; uses the sheet blocks of AD-124). *Lie* : E-9, `GET …/fiches/:fid/liees`, « Mentionnée dans » (AD-100), B-64. *Empêche* : a second « Mentionnée dans » ; a panel the phone cannot show ; counts computed in the screen. *Règle* : (1) one route builds the groups (nature read backwards × type — and, for a symmetric nature, from both sides) with their totals **with and without descendants** and the first lines, under the two guards, `descendants` on a place ; the screen only draws, and decides from the totals whether the block, the toggle or the line « Rien à ce niveau » shows ; (2) the block sits before « Mentionnée dans », which keeps the links and the free relations ; (3) a block of the sheet, not a side panel (decision of Monsieur, 2026-10-25 : « on commence par un bloc et on verra à l'usage ») — the panel stays a drawn evolution, `#etat-volet` of the mockup. *Rejets* : **extending « Mentionnée dans » with groups** — it lists sources of links, the new block lists sheets by meaning ; mixing them hides both ; **computing the counts in the browser** from the full list — 300 characters of a city. |
| AD-132 | **« Gone » is a charge of the sheet, one key, a word per type** (`kanevas-fiches-liees`, B-67, decision of Monsieur 2026-10-25 ; like `pj`, AD-17, and `statut`, AD-123). *Lie* : the charge schemas of personnage, créature, faction, famille, lieu, objet, vaisseau ; the type list (word, menu entries, filter words, glyph per type) ; one route ; E-8, E-9, E-17 ; the import. *Empêche* : a column every other type carries empty ; seven booleans with seven names for one question ; a mark that hides the sheet. *Règle* : (1) `charge.disparu` boolean, absent = there, on the seven types above ; the words live in the type list (`disparu: {oui, non, menuOui, menuNon, filtre, icone}`) — « Mort/Morte », « Détruite », « Éteinte », « Détruit » — and nowhere else ; (2) written by `PATCH /api/univers/:id/fiches/:fid {disparu}` (GM out of player mode, 400 on a type without the mark) and by the import (`disparu`, protected by B-48) ; (3) read by whoever reads the sheet ; `?etat=la|disparu` filters, the same two values for every type ; (4) the mark on the image is a rendering rule of the charte (desaturation, the type's glyph in a chip), never a change of the image file. *Rejets* : **a status with more values** (disparu, inconnu, en ruine) — the text says it, the mark answers one question ; **one key per type** (`mort`, `detruit`, `eteinte`) — three filters, three import fields, three routes for one notion ; **hiding gone sheets from players** — a death or a ruin is a fact of the world, the audience of the sections decides what is read. |
| AD-133 | **« Famille » is a type of the list** (`kanevas-fiches-liees`, B-66 ; AD-102). *Règle* : `famille` / `familles`, « Famille », « Nouvelle famille », « Aucune famille », icon `users`, reglable, charge `{v:1}` ; natures : membre, allié, ennemi (target) ; se situe, détient, allié, ennemi (source) — « dirige » is a qualifier, never a nature (decision of Monsieur, 2026-10-25). *Rejets* : **a family as a sub-type of faction** — a family and a faction answer different questions at the table, and a character belongs to both. |
| AD-134 | **The import carries natures, qualifiers, families and the « gone » mark** (`kanevas-fiches-liees` ; amends AD-121 (4), AD-104). *Règle* : `POST /fiches/:cle/relations` entries read `{section, cible, nature?, qualificatif?}` (`type` accepted as `qualificatif` for one version, documented deprecated) ; an unknown or incompatible nature is a refusal counted (`nature_incompatible`), not a free relation ; uniqueness on (section, cible, nature, qualificatif) ; `disparu` on a sheet of a type that carries the mark and `type: famille` go through `PUT /fiches/:cle` ; no migration of the existing relations (decision of Monsieur, 2026-10-25 : the import is redone). *Rejets* : **mapping free words to natures at import** — Kanevas does not know Kanka (AD-104) ; the script says the nature. |

| AD-121 | *Amended by AD-125 (`kanevas-scenarios`) : campaigns and scenarios go through the sheets route ; `PUT /campagnes/:cle` and `PUT /campagnes/:cle/scenarios/:scle` are removed ; the compte-rendu and relations parts hold.* **Campaigns, scenarios, session reports and relations through the import API** (`kanevas-import-reprise`, B-47, B-48, B-50; details AD-104, AD-107, AD-61). *Binds*: `PUT /campagnes/:cle`, `PUT /campagnes/:cle/scenarios/:scle`, `PUT /fiches/:cle` of type `compte_rendu`, `POST /fiches/:cle/relations`, E-13, batch report. *Prevents*: a session report without a campaign; a second session-report write path; a relation bypassing the limit or guard; a replay undoing a campaign status. *Rule*: **(1) Campaign**: `{nom, statut}` (`en_preparation` by default), ensured by `cle_origine` through `services/campagnes.ts`; `dernier_import` keeps the written name and status; a status changed in Kanevas is never restored (B-48, warning « statut modifié dans Kanevas : non écrasé »). **(2) Scenario**: `{titre, contenu}` under the campaign identified by the address key (`cle_origine` unique per campaign), calls `services/scenarios.ts` with the 100,000-character ceiling (AD-106); title and content protected by `dernier_import` and `version_importee`; unknown campaign: 404 `campagne_inconnue`, the batch counts a refusal. **(3) Session report**: `type: compte_rendu` is accepted by `PUT /fiches/:cle` from this slice; `campagne` (source key of a campaign in the universe) is **required**: absent or unknown, the sheet is refused (400 `invalide`, `champ: campagne`, reason « campagne inconnue : <clé> »), nothing written. Creation goes through AD-61's single function (actor: the token's GM, hence no author); the **first** section in the list becomes the « Compte-rendu » section and subsequent ones are ordinary sections. An existing session report's campaign does not change: a different key on replay is ignored with warning « campagne non modifiable, ignorée ». `creeLe` acts only on creation (AD-107); on replay it is ignored without warning if equal, with a warning otherwise. **(4) Relations**: `POST /fiches/:cle/relations` ensures each entry through the existing relation function (`creerRelation`, its refusals `auto_relation`, `relation_existante` → « inchangée », `limite_relations`); an entry with an unknown section or target is not written and yields a warning (the script replays); `auto_relation` and `limite_relations` are counted refusals (`relations.refusees`) with their code in the report; the response returns per-entry details. One request = one transaction per entry, never per batch of entries: a refusal does not cancel its neighbours. *Rejected*: an original-date column (AD-107 stands); a campaign implicitly created by a session report — the script pushes campaigns first, AD-104's rule; changing a session report's campaign by replay — Kanevas has no such action, the import does not invent one. |
| AD-119 | *`kanevas-scenarios` : the bilan of an adventure also counts its preparation tasks (deleted), its scenarios (to the root) and the comptes-rendus left without adventure (AD-124).* **Deletion in practice: summary read beforehand, one transaction, disk afterwards** (`kanevas-import-tenir`, B-39 to B-41, delegate's decision of 2026-10-12; clarifies AD-105) `[ADOPTED]`. *Binds*: E-9 (title, deletion), E-14, E-13, E-19, import API, `services/fiches.ts`, `services/univers.ts`, `stockage.ts`. *Prevents*: a dialog announcing numbers it has not read; an orphan row; a file removed from disk for a rolled-back deletion; name confirmation handled only by the browser; a player or Player mode deleting. *Rule* — **(1) Rename**: `PATCH …/fiches/:fid` accepts `titre` (1 to 120 characters after trimming, `invalide` otherwise), GM outside Player mode; the service function `renommerFiche` is the sole title write path from the screen and does not touch `dernier_import` (a title differing from what the import wrote is protected on replay, B-48); a session report is renamed likewise. **(2) Summary**: `GET …/fiches/:fid/suppression` (GM outside Player mode; 404 otherwise) returns `{sections, relations, places, fichiers, enfants, parent: {id, titre} \| null}` — relations targeting the sheet, placements on maps, files referenced by its sections or illustration, children all read (the GM reads everything); E-9's dialog calls it on opening. **(3) Delete a sheet**: `supprimerFiche(db, acteur, universId, ficheId)`, one transaction, in order: children attached to the sheet's parent (or root); relations and map elements targeting it; its sections (cascades remove their relations, links, file references, proposals, pending references); the sheet; links targeting it become `cible_fiche_id NULL` (broken). Only after commit, disk files this deletion detached from a row that owned them directly (legacy attachments) are removed through `stockage.ts`; media library files are never removed here. No trace of the sheet is kept. Unreadable or unknown to the actor: `introuvable`. **(4) Through the import API**: `DELETE /api/import/v1/univers/:id/fiches/:cle?lot=` calls the same function with the token's actor, by `cle_origine`; unknown key: 204, nothing written; counted as « supprimée » in the batch's « Fiches » kind. **(5) Delete a universe**: `supprimerUnivers(db, acteur, universId, nom)`; GM outside Player mode (`introuvable` for someone without a role, `refuse` for a player); the name is compared exactly **by the server** (`invalide`, `detail: 'nom_different'`). A transaction deletes in order: tokens, batches, preparation tasks, scenarios, campaigns, elements then maps, file connections (references, pending references), relations, links, proposals, sections, sheets (their `parent_id` first set to `NULL` in one `UPDATE`), aliases, labels, type activations, media library files, members, the universe; the game system stays. File and map background paths are **collected beforehand** and deleted from disk **after** commit; a deletion failure is logged without rollback. The session cache carries no universe permission (AD-9): members lose access on their next request. *Rejected*: reading the summary only at confirmation (the GM would confirm stale numbers) — the dialog recounts on opening, and deletion does not depend on it; blanket `ON DELETE CASCADE` (AD-105: `RESTRICT` remains the safety net); a name confirmed only by the browser field; universe deletion restricted to its creator — GMs are equal (accepted risk, AD-105). |
| AD-115 | **The tree in practice: a parent through `PATCH`, a bounded ancestor walk, two reads counting only readable data** (`kanevas-import-arbre`, B-35, delegate's decision of 2026-10-12; clarifies AD-99) `[ADOPTED]`. *Binds*: E-8 (tree), E-9 (breadcrumb, « Contient », « Déplacer sous… »), import API (`parent`). *Prevents*: a cycle; an ancestor, child or counter betraying an unreadable sheet; a second parent write path. *Rule*: (1) `services/fiches.ts` carries `deplacerFiche(db, acteur, universId, ficheId, parentId \| null)` — GM outside Player mode (`exigerMJ`), `introuvable` for an unknown parent or one in another universe, `invalide` with `detail: 'cycle'` (« Une fiche ne peut pas être rangée sous l'un de ses descendants. ») when the sheet is the requested parent or one of its ancestors, and `detail: 'trop_profond'` (« La hiérarchie est trop profonde. ») if the walk exceeds 50 levels; the route is `PATCH .../fiches/:fid` with `{parent: <id> \| null}` (the key joins `sousType`; an unknown key, `titre` has been accepted since `kanevas-import-tenir`, AD-119), and it is the sole parent write outside import and lifting children on deletion (AD-119), which calls the same function (AD-2). (2) Ancestors are walked through `parent_id`, one level per read, never recursive SQL; a sheet's view (`GET .../fiches/:fid`) returns `ancetres: [{id, titre, type}]`, from highest to nearest, **restricted to those the reader reads** (`sqlFicheLisible` for the same actor, Player mode included); the walk continues beyond an unreadable ancestor. (3) « Contient » and the tree return only readable sheets and no child count: `aDesEnfants` is a boolean computed under the same read filter. (4) Import: `parent` is a source key; if unknown, or if it would create a cycle, the sheet is written without a parent (or keeps its own) with warning « parent <clé> inconnu : fiche écrite sans parent » or « parent <clé> ferait un cycle : non posé », the batch counts it as pending at closure, and `dernier_import.parent` keeps `{demande, ecrit}`; on replay, if `ecrit` is null and the current parent is still null, retry; if the current parent is no longer what the import wrote, it is left alone with warning « parent modifié dans Kanevas : non écrasé » (B-48). *Rejected*: a `parent_id` field in `POST .../fiches` — creating then filing are two actions, the first exists; a single `GET` of the whole tree — 201 locations and 12 levels, the response grows with the universe whereas a page per expanded node stays at 100; a child count — it would count only readable data and invite guessing the rest. |
| AD-117 | **The media library: its functions and routes** (`kanevas-import-mediatheque`, details AD-111; decisions taken by the delegate, the agent who decides in Monsieur's place under mandate). *Binds*: `src/services/fichiers.ts`, `src/routes/fichiers.ts`, E-19, E-9. *Prevents*: two upload functions that each compute a visibility; a read right written in two places. *Rule*: (1) **one upload function**, `ajouterFichier(db, acteur, universId, flux, nom, cible)`, replaces `deposerPieceJointe` and `poserIllustration`; `cible` is `{ou:'mediatheque'}` (GM only, visibility `joueurs`), `{ou:'section', sectionId, maniere:'joint'\|'texte', dansBlocMj?}` or `{ou:'illustration', ficheId}` (GM only). The visibility is **never** a client parameter: the service deduces it from where the file is added (AD-111); `dansBlocMj` is accepted only from a GM, outside Player mode. The right is checked before the stream is read and again inside the writing transaction (AD-65); a failure leaves no row, no byte, no reference. `maniere:'texte'` creates the file and returns the `fichier:<id>` reference that the editor inserts: `fichiers_sections` is filled only when the section is saved, which recomputes its rows in the same transaction as the content (like `liens`, AD-116); a file inserted then abandoned stays in the library, « unused » (the GM tidies it; the non-GM who added it still sees it). (2) **One read function**, `fichierLisible(db, acteur, fichier)`: GM outside Player mode → everything; otherwise `visibilite = 'joueurs'` or `ajoute_par = compte`; the bytes route, the list, the panel, « Utilisé dans », the reader's view and the illustration all call it; a second hand-written `WHERE` is a defect. The usages shown additionally pass through the container guard (section read, outside a GM block for a non-GM, fiche seen for the illustration), query by query, never filtered in the browser (D1). (2 bis) **Refusal of the 101st attachment**: the `limite_pieces` code is kept (409 `conflit` for a GM, « Cette section porte déjà 100 fichiers joints. »; 403 `refuse` for a non-GM, « Cette section ne peut pas recevoir d'autre fichier. »). (3) **Routes** (session, under `/api/univers/:id`): `GET /fichiers?nature=&visibilite=&usage=&q=&tri=&apres=` (100 per page, cursor; `total` = the files the reader reads, without filters, not counting closed ones), `POST /fichiers` (multipart; fields `section`, `maniere`, `blocMj`, `fiche`), `GET /fichiers/:fid` (bytes, `?v=`), `GET /fichiers/:fid/detail` (+ `?apresUsage=`: 10 usages then the rest), `GET /fichiers/:fid/impact?action=reserver\|supprimer` (the usages the **GM** loses; the GM reads everything), `PATCH /fichiers/:fid` (name, visibility; GM), `DELETE /fichiers/:fid` (GM), `POST /fiches/:fid/sections/:sid/fichiers` `{fichierId}` (attach an existing file), `DELETE /fiches/:fid/sections/:sid/fichiers/:fichierId` (remove the reference), `PUT /fiches/:fid/illustration` `{fichierId}` (choose) or multipart (add), `DELETE …/illustration`. The `…/pieces-jointes…` routes and the old byte-serving illustration routes **are removed** in the same task as the client that called them (their table is emptied: they could no longer return anything). (4) **Migration** (expand only): creates `fichiers`, `fichiers_sections`, `fichiers_attente`, adds `fiches.illustration_fichier_id`, copies each attachment and each illustration into a `fichiers` row (same name on disk, no byte copied), `ajoute_par` = the GM with the smallest `compte_id` of the universe (failing that, the member with the smallest `compte_id`; with no member the row is not migrated and its bytes stay), `ajoute_le` = the attachment's `cree_le` (illustration: the fiche's `modifie_le`), illustration name = the fiche's title trimmed to 200; `empreinte` and `cle_origine` are **null** for these files (SQL does not read the disk): the import never finds them (a `PUT /fichiers/:cle` creates its own file); they remain in the media library. (5) **Deletion**: one transaction (row, references cascaded, illustrations set to null), the bytes afterwards; the `(fichier:<id>)` in texts stay and render « supprimé » (GM) or nothing. (6) **The assistant's generated image**: `ajouterFichier` is callable with just `{compteId}`, and the `generer_image` tool of the GM's catalogue (`kanevas-images`, AD-89) calls it with no other guard (`verifierDepot` first, to refuse before spending the engine): the image is joined to the section, its visibility following the section. *Rejected*: a visibility chosen by the client (a lying client would open a file); a per-file, per-account rights table (AD-111: two levels); filtering « Utilisé dans » in the browser; keeping the attachment routes alongside (two write paths for one file); computing the fingerprint of earlier files in the migration (disk read outside SQL). |
| AD-112 | **The assistant token: file, connection session, routes** (`kanevas-import-jeton-assistant`, clarifies AD-109). *Binds*: `services/assistant/jeton.ts` (file), `services/assistant/connexion-claude.ts` (session), `routes/instance.ts`, `disponibilite.ts`, `repondre.ts`. *Rule*: (1) the file is `<data root>/claude/oauth-token` (root = database directory, `CLAUDE_DIR` to override), mode 0600 in a 0700 directory, written through a temporary file in the same directory then `rename`; its setup date is its `mtime`. (2) **A single resolver**: `jetonEffectif()` reads the file on each call then, failing that, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`; `disponibilite()` and `repondre()` use it, nobody else reads the variable or file; the token leaves this module only for the call's `env` (AD-73). (3) **One connection session at a time** (in-process lock, AD-5): starting stops the previous one; it lives at most 10 minutes (injectable clock), its process is killed on expiry, cancellation, refused code, token setup and server shutdown. The process is launched **without a shell**, `script -qec` with the Agent SDK's `claude` binary, `env` reduced to `PATH`, `HOME` (a temporary directory specific to the session) and `TERM`; output is read to extract the address (`https://` and a host under `claude.com`, `claude.ai` or `anthropic.com` — or one of their subdomains —, otherwise refused; `claude.com` since `kanevas-connexion-claude-com`: Claude Code 2.1.287 prints `https://claude.com/cai/oauth/authorize?…`, and the filter that ignored it answered 502 in production) and token (`sk-ant-oat…`), never logged or relayed. (4) Routes (`parents` guard already set in `preHandler` on `/api/instance`, AD-86): `GET /api/instance/assistant/jeton` → `{pose, poseLe, source, assistant}` (`source`: `instance`, `deploiement` or `null`; `assistant`: `bouchon`, `claude-agent` or `aucune`, the result of `disponibilite()`, so the panel knows whether to say « indisponible »); `POST …/jeton/connexion` (201 `{connexionId, adresse, expireLe}`, 502 `connexion_impossible`); `POST …/jeton/code` (`{connexionId, code}`, code of at most 500 characters; 200 state; 400 `connexion_echouee` (refused code) or 400 invalid input without this code (empty, too long, control character); 410 `connexion_expiree`; never the cause); `DELETE …/jeton/connexion` (204, cancels). None returns, logs or includes the token or code value in an error. (4 ter) **The code and Enter go out in two writes** (`kanevas-connexion-claude-entree`): `soumettreCode` writes the code, waits `DELAI_ENTREE_MS` (500 ms), then writes `\r` alone; written together, a pasted 87-character code is taken by Claude Code 2.1.287 for a paste that swallows the carriage return, and the connection failed after 60 s in production. (4 bis) The launched program and its address deadline are session parameters (`script` by default, 30 s): a test replaces the program to exercise `connexion_impossible`. `connexionId` is an opaque id drawn randomly on each opening; `POST …/jeton/code` without a session (never opened, expired, replaced, cancelled or ended by a failure) **or whose `connexionId` is not the current session's** (a second tab left on a replaced connection) returns 410 `connexion_expiree`, without touching the current session or token; absent or empty `connexionId` returns 400 invalid input, like an absent code. (5) **Stub**: same path, program replaced by `scripts/faux-setup-token.mjs` (fixed address `https://claude.ai/oauth/authorize?code=true&client_id=bouchon`, accepts code `bouchon` or a synthetic 87-character code (43 + `#` + 43) and returns `sk-ant-oat01-bouchon`, any other code fails; like the real CLI, it does not validate a code longer than 40 characters whose carriage return arrives in the same chunk — it displays it masked and waits); in stub mode, the assistant stays on `bouchon` transport (AD-55, AD-77): availability is proven with injected configuration without `KANEVAS_STUB`. *Rejected*: a field where the admin pastes a token (the `setup-token` flow is the only path, avoiding a secret pasted into a web field); storing the token in the database (a database backup must not carry a secret); `node-pty` (native dependency, `script` suffices, AD-109); rereading the variable on every request without the file (the file is what can be set without redeployment). |
| AD-113 | **Markdown rendering, preview and reading** (`kanevas-import-markdown`; clarifies AD-95, AD-96). *Binds*: `src/services/markdown.ts`, preview route, reads of sections, session reports, scenarios, system entries and proposals, assistant. *Prevents*: a read rendering its own HTML, a preview diverging from reading, source served to someone who cannot write it. *Rule*: `rendreMarkdown(source)` is a pure service function, without database access. Subheadings are shifted down one level (`#` and `##` become `h3`, `###` becomes `h4`, `####` becomes `h5`, beyond that `h6`) so they never rise above the section title. An image remains text (its alt text) until the media library (AD-111) is delivered; only an `http`, `https` or `mailto` URL is recognised as an image, another (relative) remains the literal source `![alt](x.png)`. `http`, `https` and `mailto` links carry `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`. Every read returns `html`; the `contenu` field (source) appears only if the caller can write the text. Preview is `POST /api/apercu` `{texte}` → `{html}`, behind the session, refused above 100,000 characters (400): it renders what the function would render, nothing is read or written. The link, file and GM block slices add context (universe) without changing a read route. The assistant's read tools go through the same view function as screens and return the caller's view: **Markdown** source if they can write the section, otherwise the rendered view, without source. *Rejected*: a preview computed in the browser (second engine); a `format` field per text (previous texts are read as-is); returning source to everyone and hiding it in the browser. |

The file follows SQLite's rebuild procedure: table `fiches_nouvelle`, `INSERT … SELECT` of individually named columns, `DROP TABLE fiches`, `ALTER TABLE fiches_nouvelle RENAME TO fiches`, then `recherche_fiches` indexes and triggers recreated (0005; `legacy_alter_table` set if SQLite refuses renaming because of another table's trigger, and cleared afterwards). Current type and subtype differing from what the last import wrote (`dernier_import`, B-48) mark the sheet as modified in Kanevas: a replay leaves them alone. Distinct subtypes are grouped case-insensitively (`lower()`), displaying the most-used spelling, oldest on a tie. *Rejected*: a shared module between `src/` and `frontend/` (see above); type `PATCH` with title `PATCH` in the same route (renaming belongs to `kanevas-import-tenir`, which extends the same `PATCH`); rebuilding the table in TypeScript code rather than an SQL file (AD-14: migrations are numbered files); a catch-up migration disabling `foreign_keys` for all migrations (the key is disabled only for the file declaring it). |
| AD-114 | **The GM block, clarified** (`kanevas-import-secrets`; clarifies AD-98, AD-96, AD-101). *Binds*: `src/services/markdown.ts`, `sections.ts`, `droits.ts`, `comptes_rendus.ts`, `fiches.ts` (search), assistant, import API. *Prevents*: a secret leaking because a write site forgot to compute public content; an improperly closed block letting subsequent content through; two notions of containing a block. *Rule*: (1) **One function** `contenuPublic(source)` in the Markdown service, pure, returns source without lines covered by top-level `mj` blocks: the start comes from the engine's `mj` tokens, the end is the `:::` line balancing `:::mj` openings encountered inside (the container itself does not nest), counting only lines at the same quote level (`>`) as the opening, and not counting a closing line indented by 4 or more columns (the engine does not close it either), nor a `:::` line surrounded by whitespace other than spaces or tabs (non-breaking space, U+2003…: the engine does not honour them); an unbalanced block runs to the end of the source, even in a list or quote; a `:::` line in a code block within a GM block closes it, as on screen; a block is recognised only if its opening line is `:::mj` (exact case, nothing after it), and never in a code block. It removes whole lines: it may remove more than the block; what the GM sees hatched is always removed. The container is registered only for the section nature (sections and session reports): `rendreMarkdown(source, {nature})` for a scenario or system entry leaves `:::mj` as text (docs/ecrans.md, B-32), and those texts have no `contenu_public`. (2) **A single write point**: every write of a section's `contenu` (screen, assistant, import, session report, applied proposal, creation) goes through a function in `sections.ts` writing `contenu` and `contenu_public` in the same UPDATE or INSERT; a test scans the code's `INSERT INTO sections` and `UPDATE sections SET contenu` and fails for a site bypassing it. (3) **`contenu_public` is `NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`**: a forgotten site shows an empty, unsearchable section to a non-GM — a visible defect, not a leak. (4) **Contains a block = `contenu_public ≠ contenu`**, without an extra column; `peutEcrireSection` (`droits.ts`) adds for a non-GM: false. `vueSection` returns `peutEcrire` false and omits `contenu`; the 403 is that of a readable but unwritable section (« Vous ne pouvez pas modifier cette section. »). A non-GM writing content whose `contenuPublic` differs from the content receives 400 « Un bloc MJ ne peut être écrit que par un MJ. » (after permissions, before version). Player mode restricts only reads (AD-39): writes consider the actual role. (5) **The reader's view** (AD-96): for a non-GM or in Player mode, the source read is `contenu_public`; for a GM outside Player mode, `contenu`, rendered with the `mj` container (`markdown-it-container` 4.0.0) as « `<div class="bloc-mj" role="note" aria-label="Bloc MJ seul">` »; the « MJ seul » badge and its padlock are drawn by the stylesheet (`::before`, token colours), the server emits no icon. Rendering source without a block never produces this container. (6) **The editor's preview** (AD-113) receives optional context `{nature, univers_id, mode}`: without `nature: 'section'`, the text belongs to a scenario or system entry and `:::mj` stays as text; with it, the (hatched) block is rendered only if the caller is a GM of that universe outside Player mode, otherwise it does not exist. Only editing a section in E-9 sends the nature. (7) **The index**: `recherche_sections (titre, contenu_public, contenu)`, triggers rewritten (insertion, update of `titre`, `contenu` or `contenu_public`, deletion); search (AD-63) prefixes the query with an FTS5 column filter `{titre contenu_public} :` for a non-GM or Player mode, `{titre contenu} :` for a GM — the whole query under the filter, in parentheses; a test checks that a word found only in the block returns nothing to a non-GM.
