(8) **The assistant**: `lire_fiche` and `lire_section` go through the service's view function (never raw `contenu`); the assistant's actor is the account without mode (AD-74), so the GM receives the full source, a player the public source. *Rejected*: a has-block boolean column (a second truth to keep in sync); filtering only on rendering (search and assistant read the source); denying text access to a player whose section carries a block (they read it, do not write it); recognising `:::MJ` or `::: mj x` (more forms, more ways to get it wrong in either direction).  |
| AD-116 | **`[[…]]` links: grammar, rendering by target, rewriting** (`kanevas-import-liens`, B-33, B-36; clarifies AD-97 and AD-100). *Binds*: sections service, `rendreMarkdown`, import API, assistant, E-7 and E-9. *Prevents*: a link changing meaning depending on the read path; a query per link; an import rewrite making content appear modified in Kanevas. *Rule*: grammar set in `docs/ecrans.md` (the `[[…]]` links section) and recognised through the Markdown engine's tokens, outside code; `rendreMarkdown` receives a **resolver** (`(cible) → lien \| texte \| cassé \| attente`) built by the service for the reader, in one query for all links in the text; rendering is decided by the target (readable, unreadable, absent sheet, pending key), `liens` serves only « Mentionnée dans » and rewriting; rewriting a pending key is a sections service function, in the sheet-creation transaction, incrementing neither `version`, `version_importee` nor `modifie_le`; replay compares after resolving known keys (AD-104). No catch-up for previous texts: `[[…]]` was not a syntax. *Rejected*: deciding rendering from `liens` — a stale row decides instead of the target; replaying existing sections in the migration — a migration rendering Markdown; a resolution route called by the browser for each link — the server already renders for the reader (AD-96). |
| AD-118 | **The contract for all-types search, aliases and labels** (`kanevas-import-recherche`; details AD-101, AD-63, AD-74). *Binds*: `services/fiches.ts` (`listerFiches`), `services/etiquettes.ts` (new, sole reader/writer of `fiche_alias`, `etiquettes`, `fiche_etiquettes`, AD-2), E-17, E-9, `chercher` tool, import API's `PUT /fiches/:cle`. *Prevents*: a second search function; an alias or label revealing an unreadable sheet; two label lists (screen and service). *Rule*: (1) **a single function**: `listerFiches(db, acteur, universId, {type?, etiquette?, recherche?, curseur?, limite?})`; `type` and `etiquette` are two optional conditions added to the read filter, never replacing it; Player mode is `acteur.modeJoueur` (already carried by the maps' actor), removing GM-only text from the `recherche_sections` column filter (columns `{titre contenu_public}` for a non-GM or GM in Player mode, `{titre contenu}` for a GM, AD-101; the column-selection function is provided by `kanevas-import-secrets`, search calls rather than copies it). (2) `recherche_fiches (titre, alias, sous_type)` is queried without a column filter (none of the three is secret); input is neutralised as in AD-63 (`motsRecherche`); labels are filtered through `EXISTS (… fiche_etiquettes … nom = ? COLLATE NOCASE)`, never FTS. (3) **Matched alias**: the `Fiche` returned by the list carries `alias`, `etiquettes` (names, alphabetical order) and, only if `recherche` is supplied, `aliasTrouve` (the first alphabetical alias whose words all match word prefixes, when the title does not match; computed in TypeScript on the already-read sheet's aliases, never through a second index). (4) **Suggested labels**: `GET /api/univers/:id/etiquettes` returns `[{nom}]` for labels carried by at least one sheet **the actor reads** (same read filter, Player mode included); 404 « Introuvable. » without a role; a label without a readable sheet is neither suggested nor counted. (5) **Writing**: `PUT /api/univers/:id/fiches/:fid/etiquetage?mode=joueur` (Player mode is an address parameter on every request in the slice, as with maps, AD-68; `GET /etiquettes` too) body `{alias: string[], etiquettes: string[]}` replaces both sets at once (GM outside Player mode; otherwise `introuvable` if the sheet is unreadable, `refuse` otherwise): trimming, empty values discarded, case-insensitive duplicates merged, aliases 1 to 120 characters and at most 20, labels 1 to 40 and at most 20 (`invalide`, `detail` `alias_trop_nombreux`, `etiquettes_trop_nombreuses`, `alias_trop_long`, `etiquette_trop_longue`); an unknown label is created in the universe, a label with no remaining sheet is deleted in the same transaction (a label without a sheet does not exist); a sheet's `GET` returns `alias` and `etiquettes` to whoever reads it. (6) **Import API**: `PUT /fiches/:cle` accepts `alias` and `etiquettes` (same bounds, field refusal without refusing the sheet: the field is left alone and the batch warns) through the same service function as (5); B-48 comparison is **by set**, ignoring case and order, against `dernier_import`; a set equal to what was written is replaced, a set modified in Kanevas is left alone and reported (« alias modifiés dans Kanevas : non écrasés »); sending the same values returns « inchangée ». (7) **The `chercher` tool** without `type` calls `listerFiches` without a type condition and returns sheets by title, with their type; with `type`, the same function; it has no `etiquette` parameter (B-26 does not promise one). (8) **Migration** `00NN-alias-etiquettes.sql` (number assigned at merge, AD-51): the three tables, their indexes, `recherche_fiches` rebuilt with `alias`, triggers concatenating aliases (ordered `group_concat`) on every insertion, deletion or change to `fiche_alias` and `fiches.titre`/`sous_type`, then rebuilding the existing sheets' index (`INSERT INTO recherche_fiches(recherche_fiches) VALUES('rebuild')`). *Rejected*: an alias as the secondary title of a second sheet row — two rows per sheet to deduplicate; searching the label in the FTS index — a label is compared exactly; a per-sheet label table (free text) — no list to suggest; a label badge counting sheets — a count reveals the existence of hidden data if computed without the filter, and its usefulness does not justify the risk. |
| AD-135 | **The brand files are public static files of the server, copied from the pack** (`kanevas-icones`). *Binds* : AD-15 (session guard), AD-57 (frontend served by the server), `assets/brand/kanevas-marge/` (the source of the identity). *Prevents* : a favicon or manifest that answers a redirect to the sign-in (a browser fetches them without the cookie, the manifest even without credentials) ; a second drawing of the icon ; a dev server and a production image that serve different files. *Rule* : the seven files `favicon.ico`, `favicon.svg`, `apple-touch-icon.png`, `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png`, `icon-maskable-512.png`, `site.webmanifest` live in `frontend/public/` (Vite serves it in dev and copies it to the root of `dist/public` in the build) ; `routes/session.ts` serves exactly these names with `@fastify/static` **before** the guard scope, without a session, `cache-control: public, max-age=86400` and `site.webmanifest` as `application/manifest+json` ; any other root address keeps the guard. The images are byte copies of `assets/brand/kanevas-marge/web/` (a test compares them) ; the manifest is the pack's, completed (`start_url`, `scope`, `display: standalone`, `lang`, `background_color` and `theme_color` `#121317`, the charte's dark `--surface`). `index.html` declares the four links of the pack's « Exemple d'intégration web » and `theme-color`. No service worker, no offline cache (docs/parcours.md, not triaged). *Rejected* : **icons behind the guard like `/assets/`** — the login redirect would answer the browser's request ; **a generic `frontend/public` allowance for every file** — a file dropped there would silently become public ; **a route reading `assets/brand/` at run time** — the image (`Dockerfile`) does not carry `assets/` ; **a build step copying the pack** — a script to keep for seven files that change once. |


## Import API (`kanevas-import`, target)

> Implemented: context and limits (`GET /`), batches, keyed sheets of every product type,
> file uploads and references, sheet deletion, campaigns, scenarios and relations. Sheet imports
> accept title, PJ, origin dates, keyed sections, aliases, labels, parent, subtype and illustration.
> `compte_rendu` additionally requires a known campaign key. Without `?lot=`, writes leave no batch trace.

**File import** (`kanevas-fichiers-put`): `assurerFichier` in `services/fichiers.ts` implements `PUT /fichiers/:cle` (same `{id, etat: creee|mise_a_jour|inchangee, avertissements}` result as sheets). Send `nom` (1–200 characters) and optional `visibilite` (`joueurs` by default, or `mj`) before the multipart `fichier`. SHA-256 is computed while `stockage.ts` streams the upload; unchanged bytes keep their disk token, changed bytes replace it. `dernier_import` protects the name and visibility independently; each protected field warns in the response and batch. An empty file is a 400 `invalide`, `champ: fichier`, counted as refused with its key and reason, without a stored file. `GET /` adds `limites.fichiersJointsParSection` (100). Files are counted under the batch’s `fichiers` nature.

**File references** (`kanevas-fichiers-references`): `PUT /fiches/:cle` takes `illustration` (image key; a document is a 400 `pas_une_image`), `fichiers` per section (at most 100 keys, else 400 `invalide`, `champ: fichiers`, counted as refused) and `fichier:cle:K` in a text. A known key is rewritten to `fichier:<id>`; an unknown one stays as sent, waits in `fichiers_attente` and warns the batch (never a refusal). When the file arrives (`PUT /fichiers/:cle`), one transaction rewrites the texts and sets the joins and the illustration, then deletes the waiting rows, without touching `version`, `version_importee` or `modifie_le`; a 101st join stays waiting with a warning. The replay compares after resolving known keys; the illustration and the joined files are protected by B-48 (keys in `dernier_import`). The batch closing counts the waiting references with the waiting links (E-18); the GM sees « Fichier en attente d’import » on E-9 (server-side view, AD-96: a reader never receives the key).

**Report and relation imports** (`kanevas-reprise-cr-relations`, AD-121):
`assurerFiche` creates reports through `creerCompteRendu`, without an author, reusing its initial
« Compte-rendu » section for the first imported section. Additional sections use ordinary section
services. A missing or unknown campaign is a counted 400 `invalide`, `champ: campagne`, before any
sheet write. Replay keeps the campaign and creation date, warning when either differs. E-13 uses
that creation date for both display and ordering, under the existing section visibility guard.
`assurerRelations` in `services/import-relations.ts` handles each array entry in its own transaction
through `relierSection`. The response is an array of `{section, cible, type, etat, avertissements}`
with `id` on creation and `code`/`raison` on refusal. States are `creee`, `inchangee`, `ignoree`
(unknown section or target, warning only) and `refusee`. Domain refusals `auto_relation` and
`limite_relations` also appear as `code` in the batch report. E-18 already displays the `relations`
counters; `GET /` exposes `relationsParSection: 100`.

Contract consumed by Monsieur's script, and any other (AD-103, AD-104). Base:
`https://kanevas.berard.me/api/import/v1/univers/:id`, header `Authorization: Bearer kvs_…`. JSON,
camelCase names, ISO 8601 dates. An **origin key** (`:cle`): 1 to 200 characters, without `|`, `]`
or a newline, encoded in the address; chosen freely by the script (`lieu:port-sel`, a Kanka identifier…).
A write carries `?lot=<id>` to be counted; without a batch it succeeds but nothing is counted. Errors: the routes' format (`{message, code}`); 401
`jeton_invalide` (single body); 404 for another universe or an unknown key (except `DELETE /fiches/:cle`, 204 even if unknown); 400 `invalide` with `champ`;
413 for a body over 8 MiB.

| Route | Role |
|---|---|
| `GET /` | The universe, the token’s account, `types` with their activation, `statuts` (`en_preparation`, `prete`, `en_cours`, `terminee`), and `limites` (section content: 100,000 characters, aliases and labels per sheet, report entries). |
| `POST /lots`, `GET /lots/:lid`, `POST /lots/:lid/clore` | open a batch (201, or 200 on resumption, with the batch object: `lotId`, `etat`, counters, report, `avertissements`; if the same token has an open batch, that one is returned: resumption); read it; close it (returns the report: counters, refusals, warnings) |
| `PUT /fiches/:cle` | ensure a sheet: `{type, sousType?, statut? (adventure only; defaults to en_preparation at creation, protected on replay), titre, illustration? (image file key), pj?, campagne? (key, for a session report), parent? (key or null), alias?, etiquettes?, creeLe?, modifieLe?, sections: [{cle, titre, contenu, joueursLisent, joueursEcrivent, fichiers? (attached file keys)}]}`; `contenu` in Markdown, with `[[cle:<clé>\|<texte>]]`, `:::mj` … `:::`, `![légende](fichier:cle:<clé>)` (image) or `[texte](fichier:cle:<clé>)` (any file). Imported sections follow the list's order, before those created in Kanevas. Returns `{id, etat: creee\|mise_a_jour\|inchangee, avertissements: [...]}` |
| `PUT /fichiers/:cle` | ensure a media library file, image or document (multipart: `visibilite` (`joueurs` by default \| `mj`), `nom`, then `fichier`); unchanged if fingerprint, name and visibility are the same; B-48 protects name and visibility. **The script must send `mj` for a file that the source uses only in GM content** (Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-11); a file that the source attaches to no sheet is also imported here. A sheet then references it by its key (text, a section's `fichiers`, `illustration`) |
| *(removed)* `PUT …/pieces/:pcle` | no separate attachment anymore: a file is sent to the media library (`PUT /fichiers/:cle`), then the section references it (AD-111) |
| *(removed)* `PUT …/illustration` | the illustration is the `illustration` field (image file key; a document: 400 `pas_une_image`) of `PUT /fiches/:cle` (AD-111) |
| `POST /fiches/:cle/relations` | `[{section: <section key>, cible: <key>, nature?, qualificatif?}]` (`type` alone is read as `qualificatif`, deprecated, with a warning): ensure each relation (uniqueness on section, target, nature, qualificatif); an unknown nature, or one that does not fit the pair of types, is refused and counted (`nature_incompatible`); an absent relation is not removed |
| `DELETE /fiches/:cle` | delete the sheet (AD-105, AD-119 (4)); 204, even if the key is unknown; carries `?lot=` like any write |

Suggested script order, not required: batch; files (media library); sheets, parents first (campaigns and scenarios are `aventure` sheets); relations; close. Replaying everything is safe (B-47).

## Deployment and operations

| Repository | What it carries | Merge order |
|---|---|---|
| `AntorFr/kanevas` (reference) | backend, frontend, migrations, image CI, all product documentation | 1 |
| `AntorFr/smart-home-charts` | `charts/kanevas`, on `common` | 2 |
| `AntorFr/k8s-home-lab` | HelmChart (`clusters/tantive/games/`), OIDC client in Authelia | 3 — merging there deploys |

- **Image** `ghcr.io/antorfr/kanevas:<x.y.z>`, public, built by CI on a semver tag;
  the version has a single source, the tag.
- **URL** `https://kanevas.berard.me` (wildcard, nothing to create).
- **Volume** `hostPath /mnt/data/kanevas/data` mounted at `/data`: the database, attachments,
  `CODEX_HOME`. The node's replicated dataset is the backup; restoring means
  putting this directory back.
- **Secrets** (OpenBao, `openbao-tantive`): the OIDC client secret; the Claude subscription
  token (AD-54, optional: the admin can set it from the screen, AD-109) and the Codex token, which Monsieur sets himself.
- *(kanevas-import)* **No new deployment secret or setting**: API tokens live
  in the database (their fingerprint). **Volume**: the import adds ~1.1 GB of files (all of them, including the 385 MB
  not attached to anything, which go into the media library), plus one copy per trial universe until it is deleted
  (`[TO VERIFY]`: free space under `/mnt/data` on `tantive`). **Ingress**: Traefik, without
  authentication middleware in front of Kanevas (verified: `k8s-home-lab/clusters/tantive/games/kanevas-helm-config.yml`),
  so the `Authorization` header reaches the pod and Traefik does not cap the body (no
  `buffering` middleware). **Backup**: unchanged; before the first real import, Monsieur copies
  `/mnt/data/kanevas/data` (the recreation of `fiches`, `docs/donnees.md`, runs at startup of
  the image carrying it).
- *(kanevas-import)* **Target repositories**: `AntorFr/kanevas` only (order 1). `smart-home-charts`: nothing.
  `k8s-home-lab`: the image version bump for production deployment (order 3, Monsieur merges),
  nothing else. The **import script** is in no repository of the chain: it lives on Monsieur's
  Mac (Monsieur's decision); if it were to be versioned, it would have its own repository, outside
  the epic.

## Stubs

| Dependency | What the stub returns | Who builds it |
|---|---|---|
| Authelia (OIDC) | a login screen listing the test accounts (Antor, Léa, Teo, Mira, Admin; identifiers `antor`, `lea`, `teo`, `mira`, `admin`); the session is the same as after Authelia, including groups (Admin carries `parents`, which `kanevas-recours-admin` reads like those from Authelia) | `kanevas-premiere-fiche` |
| Assistant model (AD-54) | `bouchon` transport: scripted responses, selected by keywords, that call the real tools with the person's permissions | `kanevas-assistant-membre`; the proposal rule (AD-80): `kanevas-monde` |
| Seed data (acceptance, criteria) | on startup in stub mode, on a database without universes: demonstration universes, members, systems, sheets and illustrations, seeded through service functions (`docs/ecrans.md`, « Données de départ du bouchon »); never outside stub mode, never in tests that do not call it | `kanevas-illustrations`: `kanevas-il-systemes-serveur` (mechanism, world without illustrations), then `kanevas-il-illustration-ecrans` (illustrations, failure files) |
| Image engine (AD-50) | `bouchon` adapter: a fixed image, attached through the real path (AD-44); a request containing « échec » fails, to test this case | `kanevas-images` |
| *(kanevas-import)* The import script (outside the product) | no secret is involved: the token is created on E-18 in stub mode as in production. For acceptance, a **sample** — about ten sheets representative of Monsieur's database (Markdown, table, links, GM block, parent, aliases, labels, image, dated session report), requested from Monsieur (discussion of 2026-10-11), converted into API calls by a small test client in the repository, replayed twice (second batch: everything « inchangée ») | `kanevas-import-reprise` (see “Import sample”) |
| *(kanevas-import, B-51)* `claude setup-token` | `scripts/faux-setup-token.mjs`, run instead of `script` when `KANEVAS_STUB=1`: a fixed address, the code `bouchon` or a synthetic 87-character code accepted (another code fails), a fake token displayed (the server writes it, `jeton.ts`); the assistant stays on `bouchon` transport (AD-55) | `jeton-assistant-connexion` |

**Import sample** (`kanevas-import-reprise`). The sample is drawn from Monsieur's real database (private repository `AntorFr/eneria`, clarification of 2026-10-15): excerpts, not a Kanka converter. It lives in `src/bouchon/echantillon/` (`echantillon.json` in a neutral format, `provenance` and mappings in its `README.md`, two images and a document). The **test client** `src/bouchon/client-import.ts` (`npm run import:echantillon -- <url> <universId> <jeton> [json]`) reads it and calls the import API in AD-104's order (batch; files; campaigns; sheets, parents first; relations; scenarios; close); it knows nothing of Kanka, never writes the token and resumes an open batch after an interruption. Contents: two campaigns; eight sheets (Eneria → Éneria Prime → Base Éneria-1 in a tree, a character with aliases, labels and an illustration, a faction and an event entirely GM-only, a quest, an item with an attached document, a Markdown table and a GM block, a link to a key sent later and one to a key that never arrives, « Nyx »); three dated session reports (2021-03-03, 2021-02-10, 2021-04-18, pushed out of order); two scenarios, one of 60,000 characters carrying a link to Éneria Prime; four relations, one to an unknown target; three files. First batch total: 11 sheets (8 + 3 session reports), 3 files, 2 campaigns, 2 scenarios, 3 relations created, 1 warning, 0 refusals. The stub token is tied to « Essai Kanka »; to replay on a new universe, create the universe then a token on E-18 and pass them to the client. `refus.json`, pushed separately, carries a session report for an absent campaign: refused, nonzero exit. The client serves acceptance in stub mode only; neither run at startup nor imported by the server.


Activation: `KANEVAS_STUB=1` (AD-55), never set in `k8s-home-lab`. Monsieur's acceptance on
the production URL remains real.

### Detail of `kanevas-outils-format-vaisseaux-fiches` (B-82)

The format says what the book's tables say about a hull, an element, a weapon and an example (decision of Monsieur, 2026-11-08). **Every field below is optional**: absent = as before; nothing is guessed, nothing is invented (AD-131, « Nothing from Éneria enters the public repository »; the table also numbers « Fiches liées » AD-131, not meant here). The `F-` identifiers belong to this slice. It amends V-2, V-6, V-10 and R-3; the modifications' effects and the system engine belong to `kanevas-outils-format-vaisseaux-effets`, not to this slice.

**F-1 — The fields (amends V-10: « all fields required » stays true of the existing fields; these are optional).**

| Nature | New optional fields |
|---|---|
| `coques` | `vitesse`, `armure` integers 0–99; `equipage_min`, `equipage_max` integers 0–100 000 (when both are given, `equipage_min` ≤ `equipage_max`, else `invalide`, `champ: 'equipage_max'`) |
| `moteurs`, `amenagements`, `defenses` | `classe_min`, `classe_max` integers 1–4 (both given: `classe_min` ≤ `classe_max`, else `champ: 'classe_max'`); `condition` text 1–200 characters (cost or technology condition, free text, **never evaluated**) |
| `defenses` | `bonus_vitesse` integer 0–9 *(signed −9 to 9 since `kanevas-outils-format-vaisseaux-ecarts`, Y-1)* |
| `armes` | `degats` text 1–40 characters (a dice expression such as `1d8+1` is text, not parsed); `perforation` integer 0–99; `qualites` text 1–120 characters; `niveau_tech` integer 0–9. `classe_min` of a weapon keeps its meaning (munitions by hull class, V-2), and `classe_max`, `condition` do not exist on a weapon: a weapon fits every hull |
| `exemples` | `equipage` integer 0–100 000 |
| `modifications` | nothing here (effects: `-effets`) |

A field outside its nature is refused like any unknown field (strict schema, `champ` = the field). A mis-typed value (a string for an integer, 100 for `vitesse`, an empty or too long text, a `null`) is `invalide` with `champ` = the field, the other rows pass (K-3). An absent field is `null` in the row; **on replay a file that drops a field is a modification** (the element goes back to `null`, the catalogue version moves, K-3) — the file is the truth, as for every column. Text is stored as written (trimmed), rendered as plain text everywhere (React text, Markdown escaping in R-3: `|`, line breaks and backslashes are escaped or flattened), never as Markdown. *Rejected*: one JSON column `regles` carrying them (the import and the checks per field would be written twice, and K-1's compare-by-column would not see them); a free-form `notes` text (nothing for the calculation to read).

There are **two migrations**, one per task that needs them (next numbers at merge, AD-51; `0030` and `0031` at the time of writing), each **only `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN`**: the first adds the catalogue columns, the second adds `swn_conceptions.equipage`. The columns are, nullable, each with its `CHECK` (`c IS NULL OR (typeof(c) = 'integer' AND c BETWEEN a AND b)`, written out because `typeof(NULL)` is not `'integer'`; the text columns `typeof(c) = 'text' AND length(c) BETWEEN 1 AND n`). The second is `ALTER TABLE swn_conceptions ADD COLUMN equipage INTEGER CHECK (equipage IS NULL OR (typeof(equipage) = 'integer' AND equipage BETWEEN 0 AND 100000))`. No table is rebuilt, no row is rewritten, the catalogue version does not move.

**F-2 — The crew is an input of the design, not of the catalogue.** `swn_conceptions.equipage` (nullable): the save and `POST calcul` bodies gain `equipage?` (integer 0–100 000, else `invalide`, code `equipage_invalide`, `champ: 'equipage'`; `null` reads as absent). **Default**: absent in the request = the hull's `equipage_min` when it states one, else `null` (the design has no stated crew; no crew line, no crew alert). The number is **resolved at the save and stored** (`equipage`, and `resultat.equipage`), so a later change of the hull's minimum by a catalogue import does not move a saved crew (the design is stale, S-4, and « Recalculer » keeps the stored number: `produire` reads it from the row; a design saved before the slice has `equipage` `NULL`, so « Recalculer » leaves it without a crew, the hull's minimum is not applied to it). An example's `equipage` is imported, stored and read by the API like any column of the catalogue; this slice does not use it elsewhere (no design is created from an example, and the examples sheet is unchanged). `equipage` of a design on a hull that states neither bound is accepted and stored (the player chose it; nothing checks it). *Rejected*: crew as a `choix` line (it is not an element of the catalogue); crew stored as « default » (a flag every reader would resolve again, and the stored result would change under the player's feet).

**F-3 — The calculation (amends V-2).** `coque` handed to `calculer` gains `vitesse`, `armure`, `equipage_min`, `equipage_max` (each an optional number: absent = not stated), an `element` gains the optional `classe_max`, `condition`, `bonus_vitesse`, `degats`, `perforation`, `qualites`, `niveau_tech` (its `classe_min` already existed), and `reglages` is unchanged; the call gains `equipage: number | null`. All integers, so the sums are exact.
```
vitesse = coque.vitesse + Σ e.bonus_vitesse × q   (defences)      null when coque.vitesse is null (a bonus alone gives nothing)
armure  = coque.armure                                              null when absent
equipage = the design's number (F-2)
```
`Resultat` gains `vitesse`, `armure` (numbers or `null`) and `equipage: { choisi, min, max } | null` (`null` when the design has no crew; `min`, `max` are the hull's, `null` when not stated). A stored result of before this slice has none of the three keys: every reader (E-22, E-9's component, the assistant, V-6) treats an absent key as `null`. **Alerts, added to V-2's lists**: *blocking* — `classe_inadaptee` after `moteur_multiple` and before `element_retire`, one per line whose element states `classe_min` above, or `classe_max` below, the hull's class (`ligne` = the index; texts « « Hangar d'essai » : classe de coque 3 au moins. », « « Salle d'essai » : classe de coque 3 au plus. », both bounds equal: « … : classe de coque 2 seulement. »); *watch* — `equipage_insuffisant` (« Équipage insuffisant : 4 sur 6 au moins. », when `equipage` < `equipage_min`) and `equipage_excessif` (« Équipage excessif : 22 sur 20 au plus. », when `equipage` > `equipage_max`), after `reserve_nulle`, **exactly at the bound** (equal passes). The class is *blocking* because the element cannot be fitted on that hull (the same standing as `limite_depassee`); the crew is *watch* because a skeleton crew is a legal play choice the table decides and no stored design must become unsaveable when a catalogue states a minimum later (cheapest to undo; the GM can still read the alert). An element's `condition` raises no alert (text only), a weapon never raises `classe_inadaptee`.

**F-4 — The text version (amends V-6) and the rule sheets (amends R-3).** V-6's measures table gains the rows `Vitesse`, `Armure`, `Équipage` **after `Portée de saut` and only when the value is not `null`**, so a design with nothing stated prints byte for byte as before; values print as integers. **The examples sheet (R-3) does not print these rows** (`tableMesures(…, false)`: its crew is the example's, not a design's). The alerts list needs no change. R-3's tables gain columns **only when at least one row of the table states the value** (a column nobody states never appears; a row that does not state it prints `—`): coques — `Vitesse`, `Armure`, `Équipage min`, `Équipage max`; moteurs, aménagements, défenses — `Classe min`, `Classe max`, `Condition` (and `Bonus de vitesse` for défenses); armes — `Dégâts`, `Perforation`, `Qualités`, `Niveau tech.`. They come after the existing columns, in the order above. A table of a catalogue that says none of it is therefore identical to V-6/R-3 as delivered (a test compares). R-4's version needs no change: `c=` is the catalogue version, which moves when an import adds or modifies an element (K-3) — a new or changed field is a modified element — so the update of the sheets is proposed (R-7); `m=` (masks and withdrawals) is untouched. A catalogue that states none of the fields leaves both, and the sheets, as they were.

**F-5 — The assistant and the export.** `swn_vaisseau_calculer` (V-9) returns the new keys of `Resultat` as they are (no tool parameter changes except the optional `equipage`, an integer); nothing else reads them.

**F-6 — The browser.** `frontend/src/outils/swn/vaisseaux/` gains: the **crew control** of the workshop (E-22), the results rows `Vitesse`, `Armure`, `Équipage`, the **choice-row captions** (class range, condition, damage line) and the **ineligible mark** on an element whose class range excludes the chosen hull. No rule is computed in the browser (V-5 stays): the screen displays what `POST calcul` returns; the mark of an ineligible element is the **server's** data (`GET catalogue` rows gain `classe_min`, `classe_max`, `condition`, `degats`, `perforation`, `qualites`, `niveau_tech`, `bonus_vitesse`, `vitesse`, `armure`, `equipage_min`, `equipage_max` when stated, `null` omitted from the JSON), and the alert is always the server's (the mark only says « classe 3 au moins »).

**F-7 — Rights and security.** Nothing here widens a reading (one generic change of shape: `lireCatalogue` rows read by a GM now carry `donnees`, as the rows of a player already did, so the workshop can show the captions): the new fields are columns of the same catalogue rows, read under the same guards (K-2); a masked element keeps being resolvable for the design that carries it (V-3). The `condition` and `qualites` texts are catalogue content the GM imported and are public to the tool's readers like the name. **No book text is in the repository**: the trial catalogue's additions are invented (F-8).

**F-8 — Trial data.** The trial files are **not changed** (V-11: the delivered tables and counts of `retrait` stay true). The tests write their catalogue in memory (a `catalogue-essai-regles.json` fixture under `src/outils/swn/exemples/` is *not* added to the seed): hull `frégate-r` classe 2, vitesse 3, armure 5, équipage 6 à 20; `salle` classe 2 à 3; `hangar` classe_min 3; `voiles` defence with `bonus_vitesse` 1; `canon-r` with `degats` `1d8`, `perforation` 3, `qualites` `Bruyant`, `niveau_tech` 3 — all invented. Hand-calculated expectations: speed 3 + 1 = 4; crew 6 passes, 5 raises `equipage_insuffisant`, 20 passes, 21 raises `equipage_excessif`; `hangar` on the class-2 hull raises `classe_inadaptee`, on a class-3 hull not; `salle` on a class-3 hull passes, on a class-4 hull raises it. **Nothing of Éneria** (AD-131, the one on Éneria, below).

*Rejected overall*: evaluating `condition` (text only: the book's conditions are prose); a speed or armour bonus on other natures (no such rule is asked, `-effets` carries the modifications); making the crew bounds blocking.

### Detail of `kanevas-outils-format-vaisseaux-effets` (B-83)

The format says what the book's modifications and system engine do (decision of Monsieur, 2026-11-08). The effects are **a closed list the module knows**: the data pick one and give its numbers, they never carry a formula or code (AD-130, the letter's principle 4). **Everything below is optional**: absent = as before; nothing is guessed (AD-131). The `X-` identifiers belong to this slice. It amends V-2, V-6, V-8, V-9, V-10 and R-3 **on top of the fiches slice's F-1 to F-8** (this slice depends on it: speed, armour, the optional-field rule, the second `swn_conceptions` column). It adds no table.

**X-1 — The fields (amends V-10 and F-1; all optional, strict).**

| Nature | New optional fields |
|---|---|
| `modifications` | `cout_pct` integer 1–1 000 (percentage of the **hull's** `cout`, added to `cout`, never multiplied by a factor); `effet` a code of X-2; the parameters of that effect, below |
| `amenagements` | `effet` (only `moteur_systeme`) and its parameters; `soute` integer 0–1 000 000 (cargo capacity, X-3) |

Parameters, each **required with its effect, refused without it** (`invalide`, `champ` = the parameter): `valeur` — `vitesse`, `armure`: integer −9 to 9 except 0 (the column's `CHECK` allows −9 to 20 because `reparation_min` shares it, 1–20); `reparation_min`: integer 1–20; `points_max` (1–99), `sens` (`puissance_vers_masse` or `masse_vers_puissance`), `echange_puissance` (integer 1–99, power per point) and `echange_masse` (multiple of 0.5, 0.5–99, mass per point) — `echange`; `rend_puissance` and `rend_masse` — `moteur_systeme`: **four numbers each, one per hull class 1 to 4** (integers ≥ 0; the mass a multiple of 0.5 ≥ 0). A parameter that does not belong to the chosen effect is refused with `champ` = the parameter. `soutes_doublees`, `magasins_doubles` and `echange` need `limite` 1 in the file (`champ: 'limite'`, reason « limite 1 attendue »): one line per design, so a target or a number of points has one meaning. An unknown `effet` is `invalide`, `champ: 'effet'`, reason « effet inconnu : <code> » (`moteur_systeme` on a modification, or another effect on a fitting, is also unknown there); the other rows pass (K-3). On replay a file that drops the effect is a modification (K-3, as F-1). Storage: nullable columns of `swn_catalogue_modifications` and `swn_catalogue_amenagements` (`cout_pct`, `effet`, `valeur`, `points_max`, `sens`, `echange_puissance`, `echange_masse`, `rend_puissance`, `rend_masse` — the last two JSON text of four numbers —, `soute`), each with the `CHECK` it can carry alone (range, `effet IN (…)`, `sens IN (…)`); the rules between columns (parameter required with its effect) are the import's, as K-3 already is for the other cross rules. Two migrations, `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN` only, numbers at merge (AD-51; `0032` and `0033` at the time of writing, after the fiches slice's `0030`, `0031`): the catalogue columns, then `swn_conceptions.reparation`.

**X-2 — The closed list (the slice's architecture decision).** Derived from the effects the book's modifications ask, with no value of Éneria. Seven codes (six modification effects and `moteur_systeme`) in a module constant `EFFETS` (the closed list, validated at the import and named on display; the calculation and the screen each name their own effects and do not derive from it; adding an effect is a code change with its test, never a data change):

| Code | Nature | Parameters | What `calculer` does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `vitesse` | modification | `valeur` | `vitesse` += `valeur` × q (floor 0; `null` stays `null` when the hull states none) |
| `armure` | modification | `valeur` | same on `armure` |
| `echange` | modification | `points_max`, `sens`, `echange_puissance`, `echange_masse` | with `points` p chosen (X-4): `puissance_vers_masse`: free power −= p × `echange_puissance`, free mass += p × `echange_masse`; `masse_vers_puissance`: the reverse |
| `soutes_doublees` | modification | — | targets a fitting line stating `soute`: that line's `soute` × q counts twice |
| `magasins_doubles` | modification | — | targets a weapon line: its munitions cost and its magazines' mass count twice (`munitions_ligne` × 2, `masse_magasins` × 2) |
| `reparation_min` | modification | `valeur` | the design's repair skill (X-4) must be at least the greatest `valeur` of its lines, else the watch alert `reparation_insuffisante` |
| `moteur_systeme` | amenagement | `rend_puissance`, `rend_masse` | free power += `rend_puissance[classe − 1]` × q, free mass += `rend_masse[classe − 1]` × q: the hull's own engine is replaced, so what it took is given back |

*Rejected*: an expression language or formula string in the data (a second interpreter, a security surface, AD-130 forbids it); one free `effets` JSON of key/value pairs (nothing for the import to check per field, K-1 could not compare it by column); an effect list per catalogue declared by the data (the same thing as code in the data); effects on other natures (no such rule is asked).

**X-3 — The calculation (amends V-2 and F-3).** A modification line costs `cout_ligne + round(coque.cout × cout_pct / 100) × q` with the percentage rounded **half up to the credit, in integers**: `floor((coque.cout × cout_pct + 50) / 100)` (hand case: hull 300 000, 10 % → 30 000; hull 333 335, 10 % → 33 334). The percentage is never multiplied by a class factor (`x_cout` concerns `cout` only). `Resultat.lignes[]` gains, **only on a line whose element states `cout_pct`**, `cout` (the line's total cost); on a line carrying an effect it gains `points` and `echange: { puissance, masse }` (echange; the signed deltas of the line, e.g. −3 and +1.5, computed by the server) and `vise: { nature, cle }` (a doubling). `Resultat` gains `soutes` (Σ `soute` × q, ×2 on a targeted line; `null` when no chosen element states `soute`) and `reparation: { choisie, min } | null` (`null` when no line asks for a skill and the design's skill is 0). `vitesse` and `armure` are those of F-3 plus the effects. A catalogue that states none of the fields gives a result **equal key for key** to the delivered one (a test compares); a stored result without the new keys reads as `null`. Order of application: the sums of V-2, then `moteur_systeme`, then `echange`, then the alerts.

**X-4 — The design's two new inputs, stored with it (as F-2).** `swn_conceptions.reparation` (integer 0–20, default 0; `POST calcul` and the save take `reparation?`, else `invalide`, `champ: 'reparation'`) — chosen in E-22, **saved with the design, not a catalogue value**. On a choice line of the body: `points?` (integer 0 to the element's `points_max`, default 0; **one above `points_max` is refused**, `invalide`, `champ: 'points'`, the maximum itself passes) and `vise?` (`{ nature, cle }`). `points` on an element without `echange`, or `vise` on one without a doubling, is `invalide` (`champ` = the field). Both live in `swn_conceptions.choix`, so a replay reproduces the result.

**X-5 — Alerts (added to V-2 and F-3).** *Blocking*, after `classe_inadaptee` and before `element_retire`: `moteur_systeme_multiple` (« Un seul moteur système par vaisseau. », more than one unit of a `moteur_systeme` element, whatever the lines), `cible_invalide` (« « Cale d'essai » : choisissez l'élément visé. » when `vise` is absent, or « … : l'élément visé n'est pas dans cette conception. » when the pair is not one of the design's lines, or « … : cet élément ne peut pas être visé. » when it is not a fitting stating `soute` / not a weapon; `ligne` = the modification's index). *Watch*, after `equipage_excessif`: `reparation_insuffisante` (« « Blindage d'essai » : réparation 2 au moins (1 choisie). », one per line asking more than the skill, **exactly at the bound**: equal passes). Reasoning as F-3: a missing target makes the modification meaningless (blocking), a skill short of the book's minimum is a table choice (watch, never makes a saved design unsaveable).

**X-6 — The text version and the rule sheets (amends V-6 and R-3).** V-6's table gains the row `Soutes` after `Équipage`, only when the value is not `null`; a list item prints `(→ <nom visé>)` and `(<n> points)` after the name when present, and a percentage line `— <cout> cr`. R-3's modifications table gains `Coût %`, `Effet` and `Valeur / points max` columns, the fittings table `Effet` and `Soute`, **only when a row states the value** (the fiches rule); `Effet` prints the French label of the code (`Vitesse`, `Armure`, `Échange de points`, `Soutes doublées`, `Magasins doublés`, `Réparation minimale`, `Moteur système`, from the one list). The version `c=` moves with any added or modified element (K-3); a catalogue stating none leaves the sheets as they were.

**X-7 — The assistant and the browser.** `swn_vaisseau_calculer` (V-9) takes `reparation?` and, on a `choix` entry, `points?` and `vise?` (by **name**, resolved like the entries, `'Introuvable.'` otherwise); it returns the result as it is. The browser (`frontend/src/outils/swn/vaisseaux/`) gains: the repair-skill stepper (the fiches slice's stepper component, 0–20), a points stepper under a chosen line whose element has `echange`, an « Élément visé » select under a line with a doubling, the rows `Soutes`, and the captions of the lines; **nothing is computed there** (V-5): the select lists the design's lines whose catalogue row states `soute` (fittings) or that are weapons — a display filter on fields the server sends, not a rule; whether the chosen target is valid is the server's alert (`GET catalogue` rows carry X-1's fields when stated: `effet`, `cout_pct`, `valeur`, `points_max`, `sens`, `echange_puissance`, `echange_masse`, `rend_puissance`, `rend_masse`, `soute`), the consequence of the points is the line's `echange`, the figures and alerts are the server's.

**X-8 — Rights and trial data.** Reads are unchanged (same rows, same K-2 guards, a masked element stays resolvable for a design that carries it, V-3). The trial files are **not changed** (V-11: the tables and counts of `retrait` stay true); the tests write their catalogue in memory, all names and numbers invented: hull `frégate-r` (cost 300 000, class 2, speed 3, armour 5), `blindage` (10 %, `armure` +2, `reparation_min` 2), `cale` (`soutes_doublees`, `limite` 1) on `soute-r` (`soute` 10), `reacteur` (`echange`, `puissance_vers_masse`, `points_max` 5, 1 power for 0.5 mass), `munitions-x` (`magasins_doubles`) on a weapon of V-2's synthetic case, `moteur-sys` (`moteur_systeme`, power 2/3/4/5, mass 1/1.5/2/2.5). Hand-calculated: +30 000 cr and armour 7; soutes 10 → 20; 3 points → −3 power, +1.5 mass, 5 passes, 6 is `invalide` (`champ: 'points'`); repair skill 1 raises the alert, 2 does not; `moteur-sys` on the class-2 hull gives back 3 power and 1.5 mass (4 and 2 on class 3); the synthetic weapon of V-2 with two extra magazines goes from 6 000 to 12 000 cr of munitions and from 2 to 4 of magazine mass. **Nothing of Éneria** (AD-131).

*Rejected overall*: an effect on a line chosen by position (indexes move when a line is removed; the pair `nature/cle` is unique per design); several effects per element (the book's modifications carry one; a second would need an order); repair skill as an alert-only catalogue value (it is a fact about the crew, chosen by the player).

### Detail of `kanevas-outils-format-vaisseaux-ecarts` (B-85)

Three gaps of the format, found when the real data were converted (decision of Monsieur, 2026-11-10; B-79 holds: none is accepted). **Everything is optional**: a catalogue that says none of it gives a result equal key for key to the delivered one (a test compares). The `Y-` identifiers belong to this slice. It amends F-1 (the speed-bonus range), X-1 to X-3, X-5, X-6, V-2, V-6, V-10 and R-3, on top of the fiches and effects slices. No book value is in the repository (AD-131): the figures below are invented.

**Y-1 — The fields (amends F-1, X-1 and V-10; all optional, strict).**

| Nature | Field | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `defenses` | `bonus_vitesse` | integer **−9 to 9** (was 0–9); 0 stays valid and does nothing |
| `modifications` | `reparation_min` | integer 1–20: the repair skill the modification asks for, **whatever its effect** (or none) |
| `amenagements` | `soute_par_classe` | array of **four** integers 0–1 000 000, one per hull class 1 to 4 |
| `amenagements` | `reduction_cout_pct` | integer 1–100: percentage taken off the cost of the fitting; **only with `effet` `moteur_systeme`**, else refused with `champ: 'reduction_cout_pct'` and the reason « réduction du coût réservée au moteur système » |

Cross rules, the import's (as K-3 and X-1): a modification stating **both** the field `reparation_min` and the effect `reparation_min` is refused, `champ: 'reparation_min'`, reason « déjà dit par l'effet » (two sources of one number; the effect and its `valeur` stay valid, X-2 unchanged, so no delivered catalogue changes meaning); `soute` and `soute_par_classe` **may both be stated**, the per-class one wins (B-85: it replaces the single cargo « quand elle est dite »; refusing the pair later would be cheaper than loosening it, but a file that says both is not wrong, so it is accepted). A malformed value (wrong length, a decimal, below 0 or above 1 000 000, 101 %, a string, a `null`) is `invalide` with `champ` = the field and the other rows pass (K-3). `bonus_vitesse` −10 and 10 are refused, −9 and 9 pass. On replay a file that drops a field modifies the element (as F-1). Per-class values are not multiplied by any hull factor (as `soute`).

**Y-2 — Storage.** One migration, number at merge (AD-51; `0034` at the time of writing). `ADD COLUMN` for the three new columns (`swn_catalogue_modifications.reparation_min`, nullable `CHECK` 1–20; `swn_catalogue_amenagements.soute_par_classe`, JSON text of four integers, the `CHECK` of `rend_puissance` plus `json_valid`/`json_array_length = 4`; `reduction_cout_pct`, nullable `CHECK` 1–100). **`bonus_vitesse` cannot be widened by `ALTER`** (SQLite does not change a `CHECK`): `swn_catalogue_defenses` is **rebuilt** with the procedure of `0026-swn-masses.sql` (first line `-- migrate: sans-cles-etrangeres`, new table, `INSERT … SELECT` of every column, drop, rename, both unique indexes recreated), the only change being `BETWEEN -9 AND 9`; the same migration carries the three `ADD COLUMN` (they are two statement groups of one file, one transaction). A migration test checks that the rows of a trial universe survive with their values and that `foreign_key_check` is empty. No row is rewritten otherwise and the catalogue version does not move at migration. The shared column set of V-10 stays « one statement set per nature, built once from the declared columns »: the three fields are declared there, not copied.

**Y-3 — The calculation (amends V-2, F-3, X-2, X-3 and X-5).** `calculer` stays pure; resolved elements gain `soute_par_classe?`, `reduction_cout_pct?`, `reparation_min?` (numbers or arrays, absent = not stated).
```
vitesse     = max(0, coque.vitesse + Σ bonus_vitesse × q + effets vitesse)   one floor, on the total, after every addition (as X-2 and the code)
soute(e)    = e.soute_par_classe[coque.classe − 1] ?? e.soute                 stated when either is
cout_ligne  = e.cout × (e.x_cout ? facteur_cout : 1) × q  −  reduction       reduction = floor((cout_brut × e.reduction_cout_pct + 50) / 100)  when the effect is moteur_systeme
exigence(e) = e.reparation_min ?? (e.effet = 'reparation_min' ? e.valeur : none)
```
The system engine's « cost of its effect » is **the cost of the fitting that carries it** (the effect has no cost of its own in the format: its only cost is the line's `cout_ligne`); the reduction is rounded **half up to the credit, in integers, on the reduction**, the same rule as X-3's percentage (so the cost never has a fractional part). It is part of `cout_ligne`, before the sums of V-2, hence in `prix`, `chantierJours` and `entretien`; `Resultat.lignes[].cout` is also given on a line whose element states `reduction_cout_pct` (the cost **after** the reduction). A line costs at least 0. `moteur_systeme_multiple` makes `q` > 1 a blocking alert, so a reduction never multiplies. A negative bonus on a hull that states no speed gives nothing (`null` stays `null`, F-3). `soutes` = Σ `soute(e)` × q (×2 on a targeted line), `null` only when no chosen element states either field; a class value 0 states a cargo of 0. A doubling (`soutes_doublees`) may target any fitting line **stating a cargo, by either field** (X-5 `cible_invalide` and the E-22 select both read « states a cargo »). `Resultat.reparation.min` = the greatest `exigence` of the lines; `reparation_insuffisante` (X-5) is raised for **every line** whose `exigence` exceeds the design's skill, **exactly at the bound** (equal passes), the text and `ligne` unchanged (a modification with the effect alone behaves as before).

**Y-4 — Fixed values (the tasks' criteria read them here).** All invented, hand-calculated, in-memory catalogue written by the tests (as X-8): hull `frégate-r` (class 2, cost 300 000, speed 3, armour 5). A second hull `frégate-r3` (class 3, cost 400 000, speed 3, armour 5) serves the cargo per class only.
- *Speed bonus*: defence `rotors-lourds` bonus −2 → speed 1; bonus −5 → 0 (floor); bonus −10 and 10 refused (`champ: 'bonus_vitesse'`), −9 and 9 pass; `+2` on the same defence still gives 5.
- *Repair apart from the effect*: modification `blindage-r` (X-8: `armure` +2, 10 %) with `reparation_min` 2: armour 7 and +30 000 cr as before; skill 1 → alert `reparation_insuffisante` « « Blindage d'essai » : réparation 2 au moins (1 choisie). », skill 2 and 3 → none; `Resultat.reparation` = `{ choisie: 1, min: 2 }`; `reparation_min` 0 and 21 refused; effect `reparation_min` **and** field → refused; a modification with the effect alone or the field alone behaves as before; two lines asking 2 and 3 with skill 2 → one alert (the 3), `min` 3.
- *Cargo per class*: fitting `soute-r2` with `soute_par_classe` [10, 20, 40, 80] (`soute` 5 also stated: ignored): hull `frégate-r` (class 2) → `soutes` 20, `frégate-r3` (class 3) → 40; with `cale` (doubling) targeting it on class 2 → 40; `[10, 20, 40]` (three), `[10, 20, 40, 1000001]`, `[10, 20, 40, 1.5]`, `[10, 20, 40, -1]` refused (`champ: 'soute_par_classe'`); `[0, 0, 0, 0]` → `soutes` 0 (not `null`); a fitting with `soute` 10 alone gives 10 on every class as before.
- *System engine reduction*: `moteur-sys` (X-8, `rend_puissance` 2/3/4/5, `rend_masse` 1/1.5/2/2.5), `cout` 40 000, `x_cout` false, `reduction_cout_pct` 10: line cost **before** 40 000, **after** 36 000, `prix` 336 000 (hull 300 000 + 36 000) with the 3 power / 1.5 mass given back unchanged; **rounding**: `cout` 12 345 → reduction 1 235 (1 234.5 half up), line 11 110; `cout` 12 344 → reduction 1 234, line 11 110; 100 % → line 0; 0 % and 101 % refused (`champ: 'reduction_cout_pct'`); the field on a fitting with no `effet`, or on a modification, is refused (reason of Y-1).

**Y-5 — Text and sheets (amends V-6, X-6 and R-3).** V-6's list item prints `— <cout> cr` on a line whose element states `reduction_cout_pct` (as for a percentage line); `Soutes` unchanged. R-3: the modifications table gains `Réparation min.` and the fittings table `Soute par classe` and `Réduction du coût`, each **only when a row states it** (the fiches rule; a row without it prints `—`), after the columns of X-6; `Soute par classe` prints `10 / 20 / 40 / 80`, `Réduction du coût` prints `10 %`, `Réparation min.` an integer; **the `Bonus de vitesse` column prints a negative bonus with U+2212 (`−2`)**, and a positive or zero one **as before, without a « + »** (`2`, `0`: the column already existed, so a catalogue with no negative bonus keeps its sheets byte for byte; R-3's « U+2212 never appears » is amended for negative bonuses only; V-6's `Vitesse` row can only show the floor of 0 and stays an unsigned integer). Escaping as F-1. The version `c=` moves with any added or modified element (K-3): the sheets' update is proposed (R-7); a catalogue stating none leaves them as they were (a test compares).

**Y-6 — Assistant, browser, rights.** `swn_vaisseau_calculer` (V-9) takes nothing new; it returns the result as it is. `GET catalogue` rows carry the new fields when stated (`bonus_vitesse` possibly negative, `reparation_min`, `soute_par_classe` as an array of four numbers, `reduction_cout_pct`). The browser changes **captions only**, no control and no computation (V-5): see `docs/ecrans.md`, E-22. Reads, guards, masks and the trial files are unchanged (the tests write their catalogue in memory, absent from the seed, so the counts of `retrait` stay true, V-11); the new fields are columns of the same rows under the same K-2 guards, and nothing of Éneria is in the repository.

*Rejected*: replacing the effect `reparation_min` by the field (it would change the meaning of delivered catalogues and force a second migration of data; both stay, the pair is refused); one `reduction` generic parameter for every fitting (the book asks it of the system engine only; the effect list is closed, X-2); a `soute` of the shape `{ classe_1: … }` or one column per class (the four-number array is the shape of `rend_*`, K-1 compares by column and one validator serves both); applying the reduction to `prix` instead of the line (the line's cost, the sheet and the total would disagree); refusing `soute` and `soute_par_classe` together (a file stating both says nothing false); an unsigned bonus plus a separate « malus » field (two fields for one number).

### Detail of `kanevas-outils-format-vaisseaux-suite` (B-86)

The two gaps the replay of the reference cases on the real data left open (decision of Monsieur, 2026-11-11; B-79 and M-11 hold: none is accepted). **Everything is optional**: a catalogue that says none of it gives a result equal key for key to the delivered one (a test compares). The `Z-` identifiers belong to this slice. It amends X-1 to X-6, Y-1 to Y-3 and Y-5, V-2, V-6, V-10 and R-3, on top of the effects and gaps slices; no new `AD` (it amends AD-130, the closed list). No book value is in the repository (AD-131): the figures below are invented. *The rules of the four new effects are derived from the labels the book's modifications carry, not from a source table the repository could quote: if the replay of the reference cases on the real data shows a different rule, that is a new gap, named and decided by Monsieur, never a silent adjustment of the code.*

**Z-1 — The fields (amends X-1, Y-1 and V-10; all optional, strict).**

| Nature | Field | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `amenagements` | `reduction_base` | `coque` or `amenagement`: what the percentage `reduction_cout_pct` is taken **of**. Absent = `amenagement` (Y-3 as delivered). Only with `reduction_cout_pct`, else refused `champ: 'reduction_base'`, reason « base réservée à la réduction du coût » |
| `modifications` | `effet` | four more codes, Z-2 |
| `modifications` | `effet_pct` | integer: percentage parameter of `soutes_optimisees` (1–100) and `surcharge_arme` (1–1 000) |
| `modifications` | `armure_par_classe` | array of **four** integers 0–9, one per hull class 1 to 4, parameter of `armure_par_classe` |
| `modifications` | `echange_arrondi` | `installation`: parameter of `echange` only (Z-3) |

`montage_specialise` reuses the column `valeur` (integer −9 to 9 except 0, as `vitesse`). Each parameter is required with its effect and refused without it (`invalide`, `champ` = the parameter, as X-1); an `effet_pct` outside the range of its effect, an array of the wrong length, a decimal, a string or a `null` is refused with `champ` = the field, the other rows pass (K-3). `soutes_optimisees`, `montage_specialise` and `surcharge_arme` are **targeted** like the doublings and need `limite` 1 in the file (`champ: 'limite'`, « limite 1 attendue »). `echange` keeps its `limite` 1 **except** when it states `echange_arrondi`: a rounded exchange is computed per installation, so it may be taken several times (Z-3). On replay a file that drops a field modifies the element (as F-1).

**Z-2 — The closed list, extended (amends X-2).** Four codes join the seven of `EFFETS`, all modifications (the list stays a module constant: adding an effect is a code change with its test, never a data change):

| Code | Parameters | Target | What `calculer` does (Z-3) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `soutes_optimisees` | `effet_pct` 1–100 | a fitting line stating a cargo | the mass that line occupies is reduced by `effet_pct` % (the cargo itself is unchanged) |
| `armure_par_classe` | `armure_par_classe` | none | `armure` += `armure_par_classe[classe − 1]` × q (as the effect `armure`, with a value per hull class) |
| `montage_specialise` | `valeur` | a weapon line | the emports that line takes change by `valeur` (negative = fewer), the line taking at least 0 |
| `surcharge_arme` | `effet_pct` 1–1 000 | a weapon line | the power that line draws grows by `effet_pct` % |

*Rejected*: one generic `effet` with free `cible` and `parametre` fields (K-1 compares by column, the import checks per field, and the list must stay closed); a cargo reduction applied to the hull's **free** mass (a percentage of a figure that moves with every other line would depend on the order of the lines); signed percentages (the sign is the code's: `soutes_optimisees` only lowers, `surcharge_arme` only raises); several effects per element (X-2).

**Z-3 — The calculation (amends V-2, X-3 and Y-3; all integers and halves).** `masse_ligne(cible)`, `puissance_ligne(cible)` and `emport_ligne` are the **totals of the target's line, quantity q included** (q × the element's figure, as V-2's sums), so an adjustment applies once to the line whatever its quantity (two lasers with a mounting of −1 take 1 emport, not 0).
```
base de la réduction du moteur système   coque : floor((coque.cout × pct + 50) / 100)     amenagement : floor((cout_brut × pct + 50) / 100)   (Y-3)
cout_ligne  = max(0, cout_brut − reduction)                                                (the line costs at least 0, Y-3)
ajuste masse    (soutes_optimisees)  = − floor((masse_ligne(cible) × 2 × effet_pct + 50) / 100) / 2         (half up to the half unit)
ajuste puissance (surcharge_arme)    = + floor((puissance_ligne(cible) × effet_pct + 50) / 100)            (half up to the unit)
ajuste emport   (montage_specialise) = valeur   (the line's emports become max(0, emport_ligne + Σ valeurs))
echange arrondi (installation)       masse échangée par installation = floor(points × echange_masse)       (integer, the power side is already one)
```
Order of application: for each targeted modification, the adjustment of its target line (each one computed on the target's value **before any adjustment**, the adjustments summed, the line's mass, power and emports never below 0); then the sums of V-2; then `moteur_systeme` (its reduction, on the base it states); then `echange`; then the alerts. `armure_par_classe` adds to `armure` like `armure`. **Base `coque`**: the reduction is taken of the hull's cost alone (never multiplied by a class factor, as X-3's percentage), subtracted from the cost of the line that carries it, which cannot go below 0; the part that would take it under 0 is lost, never carried to another line. `Resultat.lignes[]` of a targeted modification carries `vise` (as a doubling) and `ajuste: { masse?, puissance?, emports? }`, the signed deltas actually applied, computed by the server (a target already at 0 gives 0, so the caption never promises more than the result). **Rounded exchange**: with `echange_arrondi` and `points` p chosen **per installation** (0 to `points_max`), q installations exchange q × p × `echange_puissance` of power and q × floor(p × `echange_masse`) of mass, the sense of the exchange deciding which side is given and which is taken (`puissance_vers_masse`: power −, mass +; `masse_vers_puissance`: mass −, power +); without `echange_arrondi` the exchange is X-3's (p × the figures, no rounding, `limite` 1) and `echange` rounds nothing. A catalogue that states none of these fields gives a result equal key for key to the delivered one.

**Z-4 — Alerts and the design's inputs (amends X-4, X-5).** No input is added: a targeted modification takes `vise` (X-4) and a rounded exchange takes `points` per installation (stored in `swn_conceptions.choix` as before). `cible_invalide` (X-5, blocking, same three texts) reads: for `soutes_optimisees` a fitting stating a cargo by either field, for `montage_specialise` and `surcharge_arme` a weapon. `points` on an element without `echange`, and `vise` on one without a targeted effect, stay `invalide` (`champ` = the field). No new alert code.

**Z-5 — Fixed values (the tasks' criteria read them here).** All invented, hand-calculated, in-memory catalogue written by the tests (as X-8 and Y-4): hull `frégate-r` of Y-4 (class 2, cost 300 000, speed 3, armour 5) with power 20, mass 20, emports 3, factors 5 / 2 / 3; `frégate-r3` (class 3, cost 400 000, armour 5) for the per-class armour.
- *Base of the reduction.* `moteur-sys` (X-8, cost 40 000, `x_cout` false, 10 %): base absent or `amenagement` → line 36 000, price 336 000 (Y-4); base `coque` → reduction 30 000, line **10 000**, price **310 000**, power 3 and mass 1,5 given back as before; a hull of cost 333 335 → reduction 33 334 (33 333,5 half up), line 6 666, price 340 001; a fitting of cost 20 000 on the first hull → reduction 30 000, line **0**, price 300 000 (the line floors at 0); 100 % → line 0; `reduction_base` without `reduction_cout_pct`, or any value other than `coque` / `amenagement`, refused with `champ: 'reduction_base'`.
- *Cargo mass.* `soute-z` (fitting, `soute` 10, mass 8, no factor) with `optimise-z` (`soutes_optimisees`, `limite` 1): 10 % → mass 8 becomes 7 (reduction 1), free mass 12 → 13, `ajuste.masse` −1, cargo still 10; mass 5 at 10 % → 4,5 (reduction 0,5; 5 × 2 × 10 + 50 = 150, floor 1); mass 1 at 10 % → no change (0); mass 8 at 25 % → 6 (2); at 100 % → 0; 0 % and 101 % refused (`champ: 'effet_pct'`); a target that does not state a cargo → `cible_invalide`; no target → `cible_invalide`.
- *Armour per class.* `blindage-z` (`armure_par_classe` [1, 2, 3, 4], cost 0): armour 7 on `frégate-r` (class 2), 8 on `frégate-r3` (class 3), 6 on a class-1 hull of armour 5, with q = 2 on `frégate-r` 9; `[0, 0, 0, 0]` passes and adds 0; `[1, 2, 3]`, `[1, 2, 3, 10]`, `[1, 2, 3, −1]` refused (`champ: 'armure_par_classe'`).
- *Specialised mounting.* `laser-z` (weapon, emport 1, power 10, mass 1, `munitions` 0) with `montage-z` (`montage_specialise`): `valeur` −1 → emports used 0 (`ajuste.emports` −1); `valeur` +1 → 2; `valeur` −2 on emport 1 → 0 and `ajuste.emports` −1 (floor); with two lasers (q = 2) and −1 → 1; `valeur` 0 and 10 refused.
- *Weapon overload.* `laser-z` with `surcharge-z` (`surcharge_arme`): 50 % → power 10 becomes 15 (`ajuste.puissance` +5), free power 20 − 15 = 5 on the bare hull; power 3 at 50 % → 5 (1,5 half up to 2), at 10 % → 3 (0,3 → 0); 0 % and 1 001 % refused (`champ: 'effet_pct'`); two overloads of 50 % and 20 % on the same weapon → power 10 + 5 + 2 = 17.
- *Rounded exchange.* `reacteur-i` (`echange`, `puissance_vers_masse`, `points_max` 5, 1 power for 0,5 mass, `echange_arrondi` `installation`): 1 point → −1 power, +0 mass; 3 points → −3 power, **+1 mass** (1,5 rounded down; `reacteur` without rounding gives +1,5, X-8); 4 points → −4, +2; 5 points → −5, +2; two installations of 3 points → −6 power, +2 mass; three of 1 point → −3, 0; 6 points refused (`champ: 'points'`); `masse_vers_puissance` (same figures) with 3 points → −1 mass, +3 power. A rounded exchange with `limite` 3 is accepted, an unrounded one with `limite` 3 is refused (`champ: 'limite'`). `echange_arrondi` other than `installation`, or on another effect, refused (`champ: 'echange_arrondi'`).
- *Compatibility.* The catalogue of Y-4 and X-8 gives the same results as before, key for key.

**Z-6 — Storage (amends X-1 and Y-2).** One migration, number at merge (AD-51; `0035` at the time of writing). The `CHECK` of `effet` lists the codes and **cannot be widened by `ALTER`**: `swn_catalogue_modifications` is **rebuilt** with the procedure of `0026-swn-masses.sql` and `0034` (first line `-- migrate: sans-cles-etrangeres`, new table carrying every column and the four new ones — `effet_pct` nullable `CHECK` 1–1 000, `armure_par_classe` JSON text of four integers 0–9 with `json_valid` / `json_array_length = 4`, `echange_arrondi` nullable `CHECK IN ('installation')` —, `INSERT … SELECT` of every column, drop, rename, the unique indexes recreated), and `swn_catalogue_amenagements` gains `reduction_base` by `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN` (nullable, `CHECK IN ('coque', 'amenagement')`), in the same file and transaction. A migration test checks that the rows of a trial universe survive with their values and that `foreign_key_check` is empty. No row is rewritten and the catalogue version does not move at migration. The columns are declared once in V-10's column set, never copied.

**Z-7 — Text, sheets, assistant, browser, rights (amends V-6, X-6, X-7, Y-5, Y-6).** V-6's list item prints `(→ <nom visé>)` for the new targeted effects as for a doubling, `(<n> points)` as before; no new measure row. R-3: the modifications table gains `Pourcentage` (`10 %`), `Armure par classe` (`1 / 2 / 3 / 4`) and `Arrondi de l'échange` (`par installation`), the fittings table `Base de la réduction` (`Coque`), each **only when a row states it**, a row without it printing `—`, after the columns of Y-5; `Effet` prints `Soutes optimisées`, `Armure par classe`, `Montage spécialisé`, `Surcharge d'arme`, from the one list. The version `c=` moves with any added or modified element (K-3); a catalogue stating none leaves the sheets as they were. `swn_vaisseau_calculer` takes nothing new; `GET catalogue` rows carry the new fields when stated. The browser changes **captions and one display filter only**, no control and no computation (V-5): see `docs/ecrans.md`, E-22. Reads, guards, masks and the trial files are unchanged (the tests write their catalogue in memory, absent from the seed, so the counts of `retrait` stay true, V-11); the new fields are columns of the same rows under the same K-2 guards, and nothing of Éneria is in the repository.

*Rejected overall*: a formula, an expression or a table of values in the data (AD-130); folding the base of the reduction into the percentage field as a string such as `10% coque` (two things in one column, K-1 cannot compare it, the import cannot check it); changing the default base (it would change delivered catalogues); a second migration for the rounded exchange (one file, one transaction, as Y-2).

## Security

| Threat | Response |
|---|---|
| A player learns that something exists (sheet, section, map, title) | It is absent everywhere; its address answers like an unknown address (AD-22, AD-38). |
| A secret file retrieved by guessing its address | Random disk name; no static route; guard rechecked for every byte served (AD-7, AD-35, AD-36). |
| The agent as a backdoor | Same functions and guards as the routes (AD-2); catalogue selected by the server (AD-26). |
| A player writes in a section that the GM's agent will read (prompt injection) | The GM's agent can modify sections: each write is listed in the thread with a link (B-26), and a world update is never applied by the agent (AD-48). **Accepted risk** in V1, no per-write confirmation. A proposal based on a session report written by a player is read by the GM, alongside the current content, before any action (AD-48, AD-81). |
| An illustration stays in a browser's cache after it loses the right to see the sheet | **Accepted risk** (AD-93): `private` cache of the sole browser that already saw it; the sheet disappears from its lists, nothing requests it again, and the server rechecks on every request. *Since `kanevas-import`, the illustration is a media library file: the same risk applies to any file whose visibility changes to « Réservé au MJ » (AD-111); it is no longer “never secret”.* |
| Codex runs without a sandbox, in a root pod; its token is on the backed-up volume | **Accepted risk** (Monsieur's engine decision): the pod is the sandbox, the token is 0600, one process per request, a disposable directory. |
| *(kanevas-import)* A stolen API token (Mac, terminal history, script repository) | Scope of one universe, the import API alone, a GM's permissions; expiration (30 days by default); revocation by any GM; database keeps only the fingerprint; « dernier usage » visible on E-18 (AD-103). **Accepted risk**: while valid, the token writes and deletes universe sheets like the GM. |
| *(kanevas-import)* A file closed to a player seen through a sheet, or a file seen outside its permissions | Double guard in the per-reader view (container and file), file route guarded by its visibility, « Utilisé dans » limited to readable sheets (AD-111). **Accepted risk** (Monsieur's decision): the mention « Image réservée au MJ. » / « Fichier réservé au MJ. » reveals that a file is there; a file added from a sheet takes the visibility of the place where it is added (GM block or section not readable by all players: `mj`), which closes the case of a file added while writing; the media library still leaves open a `joueurs` file **chosen** later in GM-only content (the warning covers only the reverse), or sent as `joueurs` by an import — the API contract requires the script to mark as `mj` what the source uses only in GM content; whoever added a file always sees it (a player uploading a file into their restricted section keeps access to it). Cache: AD-93's, same accepted risk. |
| *(kanevas-import)* Code executing in a reader's browser, from text or an import (HTML, `javascript:`, external image) | `html: false`, bounded link schemes, images and file links limited to the universe's media library, rendering through a single server function (AD-95, AD-111); a suite of XSS vectors in that function's tests. |
| *(kanevas-import)* A secret leaks outside the sheet: full-text search, link, incoming link, image, assistant, breadcrumb, tree | A single per-reader view (AD-96); `contenu_public` for the non-GM index (AD-98, AD-101); two guards for links and incoming links (AD-97, AD-100); unreadable ancestors and children skipped (AD-99). Each path has its criterion in `docs/ecrans.md`. |
| *(kanevas-import)* A player erases a GM block by rewriting the section | A section with a GM block can only be written by a GM; a non-GM cannot write one (AD-98). |
| *(kanevas-import)* A stray click deletes a universe | GM only, exact name entry; the volume backup remains the only way back (AD-105). **Accepted risk**: any GM of a universe can delete it (GMs are equal). |
| Identity spoofing | Only Authelia authenticates; no homemade user database. Sole exception: stub mode, outside production (next row). |
| Stub mode enabled in production (anyone can choose their account) | Kanevas refuses to start in stub mode as soon as an `OIDC_*` variable is set; the variable is never set in `k8s-home-lab`; a visible banner on every page (AD-55). |

## Deferred

- ~~Import from Kanka — separate epic.~~ Scoped by `kanevas-import` (the import API; the script stays outside the product).
- *(kanevas-import)* Structured creatures and ships — reopened when sorting or filtering by characteristic is needed.
- *(kanevas-import)* Trash, batch undo — reopened at the first regretted deletion.
- Trash and deletion undo, history, export — to reopen through use.
- Dedicated sheet types with structured characteristics.

# Specialized tools (`kanevas-outils`, target)

> Scoping proposed on 2026-10-16 (`epic/kanevas-outils`). Decisions AD-121 to AD-137. They add to those
> above; none amends one unless stated. Monsieur's decisions from the epic's discussion are
> **[ADOPTED]**: they are written to be binding, not reopened for discussion.

## Target repositories

| Repository | State | What it carries in this epic | Merge order |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AntorFr/kanevas` (public) | existing | module framework, configuration, API, SWN module (code, rules, format, **invented trial catalogue**), documentation in English | 1 |
| `AntorFr/eneria` (**private**) | existing | only the `_outils/kanevas/` directory: conversion script, converted data, frozen reference values, tests on real data; never `base-de-connaissance/`, the current tools' sources, or the rest | 2 (after the code reading the format) |
| `AntorFr/k8s-home-lab` | existing | a **prepared** PR (image tag and chart version), opened, which Monsieur merges; no new secret or volume | 3 (always last) |

`kanevas` CI runs on **synthetic data** and has no access to `AntorFr/eneria` (Monsieur's decision, 2026-10-16); tests on real data run in pods, which read `eneria` with their existing access. No secret, key or token is added to CI.

## Components and state

```
browser ──▶ Fastify ─┬─ module framework (registry, activation, settings, tool permissions, import, common routes)
                      │      └─▶ module `swn`: functions ─▶ swn_* tables (SQLite, same database)
                      ├─ routes `/api/univers/:id/outils/<module>/…` and `/api/systemes/:id/outils/<module>/…`
                      ├─ assistant tools (neutral) composed by a role's catalogue
                      └─ core service functions (permissions, sections, search) called by modules
conversion script (private repository) ──tool-scoped token──▶ extended import API
```

Durable state lives in the database (core + module tables); **ephemeral** state consists of the cache of generated map blocks (memory, lost on
restart without harm) and journey calculation. No state lives in the browser.

## Decisions

**AD-121 — A module registers through a single declaration.** [ADOPTED, decision (e)]
Binds: all modules, B-57 to B-70. Prevents: a module hooking into the router, sidebar, assistant and settings screen in its own way for each.
Rule: a module declares, in a single place, its name, its common settings (stored under the module's name alone), the tools it provides (each: type, scope — one game system or all —, dependencies, default
visibility, uses), its settings and their shape, the shape of its catalogues, its routes, its assistant tools and its interface entry point (loaded on opening,
AD-133). The core knows no module by name. A module groups one or more tools (the `swn` module carries `galaxie` and `vaisseaux`, which share the
jump range setting); activation and visibility are configured **per tool**. *Rejected*: a configuration file per tool — sheet types have none and
the declaration must be testable; *hot loading of third-party code* — Monsieur's decision (5).

**AD-122 — One interface contract per tool type.** [ADOPTED, decision (3)]
Binds: B-59, B-66, B-71, B-75. Prevents: a generator keeping its data separately, a map writing into sections.
Rule: three types, each with a contract tested by a trial module: **dynamic map** (rendered by its module in the « Cartes » section, separate data; the module lists its maps — title, visibility — and E-10 adds them to those in `cartes`, whose shapes do not change), **generator** (the
result is a sheet's managed section, construction choices are tool data attached to the sheet, with the version of the rules and settings), **game
aid** (its own data and entry point; none in this epic). Each contract says how it handles a changed setting (AD-128). A type is added when a second
need resembles the first, not before. *Rejected*: a single generic engine — it would carry SWN's abstractions (decision (1)).

**AD-123 — Tool data lives in the database, in its module's tables, at two levels.** [ADOPTED, decisions (4), (d), point 6]
Binds: every module. Prevents: a file or generic JSON table losing links and queries; access from outside the module.
Rule: prefixed tables, created by the module's migrations, a row attached to a universe (or to a system for its catalogue), sheet references by identifier,
no SQL outside the module's functions; storage organization (typed tables, documents with a declared shape, or both) is the module's choice. The **system** level (the game) and the
**universe** level (the campaign): a universe sees both, universe data with the same key replaces system data, and a universe **removal** (a system key
marked absent for that universe) erases it from its view without touching the system. A system catalogue has no hidden items; a universe item carries
`masque` (GM only), set and cleared by a GM of the universe, and reads filter it for a player like a secret system (B-62, Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-17).
Resolution is **by key, once**: the universe item if it exists (hidden or not), otherwise the system item if not removed; a hidden replacement
never lets the system item reappear, and computation uses only the resolved item.
*Rejected*: hiding a system item (the system is the game, shared by its universes: to make it absent from a campaign, remove it). The contract requires: key, version, attachment, shape declared
and checked on import. *Rejected*: files on the volume; generic JSON table.

**AD-124 — A common API framework, routes specific to each module.** [ADOPTED, decision (e)]
Binds: B-58, B-65, B-61. Prevents: different error and activation conventions per module.
Rule: a module's routes live under `/api/univers/:id/outils/<module>/…` and `/api/systemes/:id/outils/<module>/…`; **activation is checked on entry** (tool
disabled or invisible to the role = unknown address, same response as an absent route); same identities (session for the screen, tool-scoped token for scripts), same
conventions (error `code` + field, pagination, version against concurrent writes, update by key); the module receives the actor, their role and player mode. Two routes
are common to every module: the tool's **description** (settings and their shape, dependencies, activation: E-20 reads it) and **catalogue import** (preview then application,
added / modified / refused report). Screen, API and assistant go through the **same functions** of the module (thin routes, AD-2).

**AD-125 — Activation, visibility and settings resolve system → universe.** [ADOPTED, decisions (6), point 3, point 10]
Binds: B-57 to B-60. Prevents: three places deciding who sees a tool, and settings read in different places.
Rule: a single function resolves, for (universe, tool), the *enabled* state, visibility by role and each setting's value: the universe row if there is one, otherwise the system row,
otherwise the declared default. The system's **responsible** (creator by default, transferable role) alone writes at system level; any GM writes at their universe's level; the instance
admin has no access. Disabling at system level hides the tool from universes following it; a universe that forced it keeps access, including system data. *Rejected*: “any GM of an attached
universe configures the system” (the action affects all universes); *the instance admin as responsible* — they have no content access (AD-9). Templates keep their current rule.

**AD-126 — Tool permissions have two levels, enforced in services.** [ADOPTED, decision point 3]
Binds: B-59, B-73. Prevents: a tool leaking a secret in player view (search, route, zone, menu).
Rule: (1) the core decides whether the tool is **visible** (enabled, visible to the role); otherwise it is not found. (2) The tool declares its **uses** and enforces its internal permissions down to the row,
