with Kanevas's permission functions and vocabulary (GM only, players, author, Player mode). “What the account cannot read does not exist for it” applies to the map (item by item),
search, journeys, block generation (AD-132), the assistant and links. The actor never has more permissions than the account; a GM's Player mode reduces them to a player's.

**AD-127 — A section “managed by a tool” is a content lock, separate from the author.** [ADOPTED, decisions (g), (h), (i), (j)]
Binds: B-66, B-67, B-74, B-77. Prevents: two writers of the same section; a player writing beyond their permissions; a section unreadable without the tool.
Rule: the section carries `gere_par`; only that tool's write function, which **goes through the existing section write path** (`ecrireContenu`, with the read version; AD-48), changes its content;
the editor and section API refuse it to anyone else. The tool checks the use and the account's author role (“the author writes”) and writes **on behalf of the account**. Each tool section has **structured content**
in the declared format, a display component supplied by the module (loaded only when a sheet contains one), and a **mandatory text version (Markdown)**, produced with the content and placed in
`contenu`: this is what search, the assistant, export and the sheet read when the tool is disabled. Same audience switches as any section. Kanevas has no sheet owner:
authorship is per section. *Rejected*: the sheet as the generated unit (a sheet mixes managed and free sections).

**AD-128 — A source change rewrites nothing silently.** [ADOPTED, decision (7)]
Binds: B-67, every generator. Prevents: a ship whose numbers change under the player after a setting changes.
Rule: a computed result keeps the version of the catalogue and settings that produced it; when they differ from the current ones, it is marked **stale** (state computed on read, never stored) and is
recalculated on an explicit action, or on a proposal (same mechanism as the update proposal, AD-79), according to what the tool declares. A validated turn (later) is frozen.

**AD-129 — Editable tool data is protected like a section.** [ADOPTED, decision point 11]
Binds: B-64. Prevents: a silent overwrite between two GMs.
Rule: all editable tool data carries `version`; a write supplies the read version; a stale version is refused with the same response as a section (`conflit`, code `donnee_modifiee`), the screen
explains and offers to reload. A managed section is already protected. No lock or live editing.

**AD-130 — A catalogue is a transport JSON file; the database stores it.** [ADOPTED, decision (b)]
Binds: B-61, B-65, B-79. Prevents: an import overwriting, duplicating or deleting without saying so.
Rule: one file per module, one section per item kind, each item a **key**; the file is checked against the module's schema **before** being applied (invalid item refused with its field, the
rest goes through); reimport = update by key without duplicates; an item removed from the file is not deleted; the catalogue has a version, which a result keeps (AD-128). Two paths, same functions: the « Outils » tab
(preview then application) and the extended import API with a **tool-scoped** token (batch report by kind). Like B-48, an import does not overwrite what a human has since modified in Kanevas. A token belongs to a universe: it writes the **system's** catalogue only if its account is its maintainer at that moment (the role is reread on every use, like an import token's GM role).

**AD-131 — Nothing from Éneria enters the public repository, including tests.** [ADOPTED, mandate letter]
Binds: B-78, B-79. Prevents: a catalogue from the book, a translated rules sheet, a value computed from real data, a screenshot, entering `kanevas` or its public logs.
Rule: the public repository contains only the **format**, the code reading it and a small **invented** trial catalogue (with its own expected values, computed by hand or by the module's rules); the attested
suite and `kanevas` CI run on this synthetic data alone (Monsieur's decision, 2026-10-16). Tests on real data, their reference values (computed once with Monsieur's current tools, frozen) and the conversion script live in `AntorFr/eneria`, directory
`_outils/kanevas/`, and run **in the pods**, which read `eneria` with their existing access: they exercise `kanevas` code at a pinned revision, outside the attested suite. A discrepancy is a porting bug or a decided and recorded rule correction. *Rejected*: a `kanevas` “tests” workflow with a private repository secret — Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-16: CI is public, as are its logs; *anonymized real data* — the test's value lies in the exact data.

**AD-132 — The map is infinite: anonymous systems are computed server-side, in blocks.** [ADOPTED, point 9]
Binds: B-71, B-73. Prevents: a client receiving the whole map; a secret territory inferred from the generated density.
Rule: a block of 5×5 hexagons is generated from coordinates and a seed (same location, same result, tested) **server-side**, under the caller's permissions: density takes into account
only territories this account can read; the result is cached (memory). Only defined systems, routes, zones and **promoted** anonymous systems are stored (they keep their location and become
universe data with its permissions). The **seed** (specific to each galaxy, protected): changing it requires confirmation explaining that all undefined systems change location. The view receives only the blocks it displays. *Outside scope*: the offline map.
*Resolved by `kanevas-outils-galaxie-lire` (G-4)*: the key carries the reading level (`mj` / `joueur`, Player mode being `joueur`) and a fingerprint of the readable zones; the cache holds generated positions only.

**AD-133 — Tool code loads when used.** [ADOPTED, point 9]
Binds: B-70. Prevents: a bundle carrying the map and designer for all accounts; a data.js sent in full.
Rule: a module's entry point and a section's component are loaded through a dynamic import, on opening the screen or when a sheet contains the section; an account without access never loads it; data
follows the view (AD-132). A component respects the existing conventions (tokens in `frontend/src/ui/tokens.css`, `docs/charte.md`) and is exercised at 360 px width. Each tool's acceptance criterion: phone and tablet.

**AD-134 — A shareable link leads to a stored resource.** [ADOPTED, point 8]
Binds: B-68. Prevents: content encoded in the address bypassing permissions and the catalogue version.
Rule: the address of a map system or ship sheet is a stored identifier; it is read according to the opener's permissions (unreadable = not found). No content is encoded in the address.
Printing is a later core function for any sheet (outside the epic).

**AD-135 — Disabling hides; deleting is a separate action.** [ADOPTED, point 6]
Binds: B-63. Prevents: data lost by clicking a toggle.
Rule: disabling a tool touches no row; it hides the entry point, addresses, data and the view of system data for the universe; its managed sections remain readable in text form with an
« outil désactivé » banner, without regeneration or modification. Deleting data is a separate, restricted function (the responsible for the system, GM for the universe), whose confirmation counts what goes and, for the system,
what still uses it (universes, local items, designs). Deleting a universe erases its tools' data (one transaction, including the module's tables).

**AD-136 — A tool's capabilities for the assistant are neutral module tools.** [ADOPTED, model (8)]
Binds: B-69. Prevents: an assistant more permissive than the screen; a tool visible to a role that does not have it.
Rule: a module declares assistant tools in `catalogue`'s shape (`{nom, description, schéma, executer}`), composing only module functions (AD-27); `catalogueDe` adds them when the tool is enabled and visible
to the role read from the database; account, universe and role are closed over, never a parameter; a refusal returns `{ok:false}` without an event.

**AD-137 — Two modules talk through their functions, on a declared dependency.** [ADOPTED, decision (e)]
Binds: every module. Prevents: a module reading another's tables or calling it over HTTP.
Rule: a dependency is declared in the declaration (AD-121), the activation screen enforces it (B-58); the call goes through the other module's exported function, under the same permissions. In this epic, there is no dependency between tools: `vaisseaux` reads the jump range configured at `swn` module level, not the map. The mechanism is tested with the trial module.

### Detail of `kanevas-outils-retrait` (guarding and erasing a tool's data)

Decisions of this slice, taken inside AD-124, AD-126, AD-135 and AD-121. Marked **D-n**. It builds on `cadre` (declaration, `exigerResponsable`, `exigerMJ`, `resoudreOutil`), `catalogue` (K-1: the `StockageCatalogue` port), `sections-gerees` (S-1 to S-4: the lock) and the data of `galaxie-tenir` and `vaisseaux(-regles)`. **Disabling needs nothing new**: the toggle of `cadre` touches no row (B-63, AD-135) and the banner of `sections-gerees` already reads « Outil désactivé »; this slice adds a test that proves it (D-6) and the erasing.

**D-1 — A module says what its data are; the core counts and erases.** `DeclarationOutil` gains an optional `donnees` (a tool with neither `donnees` nor `catalogue` has nothing to erase, and the card shows no « Supprimer les données de l'outil… »):

```
DonneesOutil  { compter(db, portee): LigneDonnees[]            // read only, SQL through the module's own services
                effacer(db, portee): void                      // inside the core's transaction; leaves the sheets and the catalogue to the core
                utilisations?(db, systemeId): LigneDonnees[] } // system level only: what still uses the system's data (below)
Portee        { niveau: 'univers', universId } | { niveau: 'systeme', systemeId }
LigneDonnees  { cle, singulier, pluriel, nombre }              // « conception » / « conceptions », 2
```

`StockageCatalogue` (K-1) gains `compter(db, cible): { nature, nombre }[]` and `effacer(db, cible): void` (the rows of **one level**, universe withdrawals included for a universe; `cible` as in K-1). The **core** adds the catalogue line itself (« élément local de catalogue » at universe level, « élément du catalogue du système » at system level, the sum of the natures **and of the universe's withdrawals** — one line, one count, no separate line for withdrawals; no line when 0) and sets the level's `version_catalogue` to null; activation, visibility and settings are **configuration, not data**: they stay (a deleted tool is still enabled or not, and `Revenir à la valeur du système` remains the way to drop a setting). A line whose `nombre` is 0 is dropped from the summary.
- `swn/galaxie`: universe level counts `systemes` / `routes` / `zones` (the same recount as T-6) and erases `swn_zones`, `swn_routes`, `swn_systemes`, `swn_galaxies` of the universe through the body of `supprimerGalaxie` **extracted** into a function without guard or `vu` (`effacerGalaxie`, called by both; one place erases a galaxy). System level: no data (the tool has no catalogue and its jump range is a setting).
- `swn/vaisseaux`: universe level counts `conceptions` (`swn_conceptions`) and erases them with `swn_fiches_generees`; its catalogue (seven natures, `swn_catalogue_*`, `swn_catalogue_retraits`) goes through the port. System level: only the system's catalogue rows. `utilisations(systemeId)` returns one `conceptions` line: the designs of **all** the universes attached to the system where the designer is effectively enabled, counted as a whole (it does not test which design reads a system row, and it does not list the universes' local elements). It is a deliberate overestimate: the dialog says who may lose their catalogue, and the designs to recalculate are at most those. `swn/vaisseaux` also counts, at universe level, its generated rule sheets (`fiches-generees`, « fiche de règles générée ») as a line of its own, when above 0.
- the trial module: `essai/g` declares the catalogue only (its two `essai_*` tables through the port, so the generic test of the core runs without SWN code); the contract of `donnees` itself is tested with a module injected by the test into the registry (a counter in memory, a `effacer` that can be made to throw).

**D-2 — Core service `src/services/outils-donnees.ts`, the only caller of `donnees` and of the port's `compter` / `effacer`.**
- `resumeSuppression(db, acteur, portee, outilCle)` → `{ outil: { cle, libelle }, niveau, efface: LigneDonnees[], total, sectionsGerees: number, encoreUtilise: null | { univers: { id, nom }[], lignes: LigneDonnees[] } }`. `encoreUtilise` exists at **system level only**: `univers` = the attached universes in which the tool is **effectively enabled** (`resoudreOutil`) — « Univers voisin », which forced it, and any that follows an enabled system; `lignes` = `utilisations` of D-1, **one list for all the listed universes together** (the dialog prints it after the universes, as « Ensemble : … »), not one list per universe. `efface` at system level is the system catalogue and nothing else. `sectionsGerees` is the number of the universe's sections whose `gere_par` starts with `<module>/<outil>/` (read through `services/sections-gerees.ts`, which owns the lock); always 0 at system level (D-3: nothing is released there). It is what makes the dialog show the reassurance line of D-3, and only when it is above 0.
- `supprimerDonneesOutil(db, acteur, portee, outilCle, vu)` with `vu = { cle, nombre }[]` the lines the user read: the function recounts **in its transaction** and, if the lines differ (a key, a count), answers `conflit` (`detail: 'contenu_change'`) and erases nothing — the server holds the confirmation, not only the screen (AD-119, T-6). Same transaction, in order: `effacer` of the module, the port's `effacer` of the catalogue, `version_catalogue` set to null, and, at universe level, the release of D-3. Returns the lines erased. Nothing is erased when `vu` is empty and the recount is not (`conflit`); an empty recount with an empty `vu` is a successful no-op (idempotent: a second call erases zero).
- **Guards, in this order**: unknown tool, or tool unknown to the code, or a tool with nothing to erase **at this level** (universe level: neither `donnees` nor `catalogue`; system level: no `catalogue`, so the galaxy tool has none) → `introuvable`; universe level → `exigerMJ` of the universe (no role or a player: `introuvable`; a GM in Player mode: `refuse`); system level → `exigerResponsable` (a GM of an attached universe who is not the responsible: `refuse`; player, foreigner, instance admin: `introuvable`); tool **not applicable** to the system of the universe (C-1 `portee`) → `introuvable`. The tool may be **disabled, or invisible to the GM's role**: erasing is a GM/responsible gesture on configuration (AD-124 hides the tool's addresses, not its management). Both functions are the only door: the summary is a read of the same guards.

**D-3 — Erasing a universe's data releases its managed sections, and never deletes a sheet.** Sheets, their sections and their text are the core's: erasing a tool's data touches none of them (`swn_conceptions` and `swn_fiches_generees` → `fiches` / `sections` are `ON DELETE CASCADE` *towards the module row*, not the reverse). But a section left `gere_par = swn/vaisseaux/caracteristiques` with no design row behind it could be neither edited (the lock) nor recalculated (no `charge`): it would be an orphan nobody can fix. `services/sections-gerees.ts` (the only writer of the lock) gains `libererSections(db, universId, outilCle)`: for the universe's sections whose `gere_par` starts with `<module>/<outil>/`, `gere_par` and `source_version` become null **together**; `contenu`, `contenu_public`, `version`, `modifie_le`, links and search are not touched (AD-114 only forbids writing `contenu`); the pending source proposals (`propositions.source_version` not null) of those sections are deleted (they would apply to a text nobody maintains). The sheet then shows an ordinary free section, no banner (the tool no longer manages it). *Why not leave them managed*: the lock would outlive the only thing that could open it; *why not delete the sections*: a text a player already read is not the tool's data to destroy (B-63 says « les fiches restent »). The confirmation says it (E-20, « Les fiches et leur texte sont conservés. »). *Cheapest to undo*: a section freed is edited like any other; re-importing the design recreates a managed one only by a new design.
At **system level** the managed sections of the attached universes are not released: their source version (`catalogue`) moves with the null `version_catalogue`, so they read « Calculée avec d'anciens réglages » (stale, S-4), not silently rewritten (B-67).

**D-4 — Deleting a universe erases its tools' data in the same transaction (AD-135).** `supprimerUnivers` (`services/univers.ts`, AD-119 (5)) calls `effacerDonneesUniversComplet(db, universId)` of `outils-donnees.ts` right after the tokens and batches (`jetons_outils` goes with the tokens, K-6) and **before** the scenarios, sheets and sections, because the module rows reference `fiches` and `sections` with `RESTRICT`. The function runs, for **every** tool of the registry (enabled or not, applicable or not: the rows are the criterion, not the activation), the `effacer` of D-1 with `{ niveau: 'univers', universId }`, the port's `effacer`, then deletes the universe's `outils_univers` rows. No release (D-3): the sections go with the universe. A failure of a module's `effacer` rolls the whole deletion back (nothing is half deleted). The universe's own confirmation dialog (AD-119) is unchanged; its summary does not count tool data (the dialog of E-20 does, on its own).
*Test*: after `supprimerUnivers(« Éneria d'essai »)` every `swn_*` and `essai_*` table has zero row with `univers_id` of that universe, `outils_univers` has none, the rows of « Univers voisin » are intact, and `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` is empty.

**D-5 — Routes (session scope, AD-15; never the import scope).** `GET /api/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/suppression` and `POST …/suppression` (body `{ vu: [{ cle, nombre }] }`), and the same under `/api/systemes/:id/outils/:module/:outil/suppression`. They are the core's routes, **registered beside — not behind — `garde(...)`**: they answer for a disabled tool. `GET /api/univers/:id/outils` and `/api/systemes/:id/outils` (C-5) gain per tool `supprimable` (boolean: the tool has something to erase **at that level**, the rule of D-2's guard — at system level, `catalogue` only, so the galaxy tool carries no `supprimable` on `/api/systemes/:id/outils` and its `…/suppression` answers 404) — **present only for who may delete**, absent for a non-responsible GM (the hidden action leaves no trace in the body). The button is never dimmed on a count: an empty tool opens the dialog's empty state. Codes: `conflit` → 409 `contenu_change`; `refuse` → 403; `introuvable` → 404 (same body as an unknown address). A token `kvs_…` opens neither.

**D-6 — Disabling is proved, not rebuilt.** A cross-table test (`src/services/outils-retrait.test.ts`) takes a row count of every `swn_*` / `essai_*` table and of `sections` / `fiches` of the universe, disables the tool at universe level and at system level through `poserActivation`, and counts again: equal; re-enabling gives back the same designs and the same managed sections (state `a_jour` or `perime` as before, not `desactive`). The scan is **generic** (the list of tables comes from the schema, `sqlite_master`, names starting with `swn_` / `essai_`), so a later module's table is covered by the same test.

**D-7 — Assistant and import.** No assistant tool is added (deleting is not offered to the assistant: AD-136, and the catalogue of tools is read-only on this point; the test of the catalogue lists no `supprimer_*` tool). The import API has no deletion route for tool data (the sheet deletion route of AD-119 stays the only one).

**D-8 — Browser.** `frontend/src/ecrans/outils/suppression/` (a dialog component mounted by `PanneauOutils` in the card's foot, level read from the panel's `niveau`), behind no lazy registry: it is the core's. It reads the summary on opening (never from the list), shows the lines and, at system level, the universes and their lines; « Supprimer les données » sends `vu` back; on `contenu_change` it re-reads and shows the new lines under « Les données ont changé depuis que vous avez ouvert cette fenêtre. » with a button « Recharger ». After success it emits `kanevas:outils-modifies` (C-6).

**Security.** The summary is a read of data, hence under the same guard as the deletion; at system level it names universes (AD-135 asks for it) to the responsible only. A player and a non-member get the same 404 as for an unknown address, a non-responsible GM a 403 (`{ message, code: 'refuse' }`, no data in the body). Erasing logs nothing of the data (counts only).

### Detail of `kanevas-outils-cadre` (the framework slice)

Decisions of this slice, taken inside AD-121 to AD-126, AD-133, AD-136 and AD-137 (they amend none). Marked **C-n**.

**A numbering collision, noted.** `AD-121` is used twice in this file: by `kanevas-import-reprise` (campaigns, scenarios, session reports and relations through the import API, in the code at `services/campagnes.ts` and `scenarios.ts`) and by the tools framing (« A module registers through a single declaration »). The framing was validated with its numbers and renumbering it would rewrite the decision Monsieur read; this slice therefore always writes **« AD-121 (module declaration) »** and its tasks cite it that way; `git grep 'AD-121 (module'` finds it. Cheapest to undo: renumber the tools decision to the next free number in a later pass.

**C-6 — The sidebar refresh (AD-133).** E-20 and the bar are two tasks; their contract is one browser event: after every successful write of `PanneauOutils` (activation, visibility, settings, transfer) it dispatches `window.dispatchEvent(new Event('kanevas:outils-modifies'))`; the bar listens and re-asks `…/outils/entrees`. The event name is the whole interface. The bar's task tests it by dispatching the event itself after a `PUT`; the tab's task tests that each write dispatches it.

**Files.** `src/outils/declaration.ts` (the types below and their zod check), `src/outils/registre.ts` (`modulesActifs()`: the list of modules; the `essai` module is in it only under `KANEVAS_STUB=1` or `NODE_ENV=test`, read in `config/env.ts` like the other switches), `src/outils/essai/` (the trial module: `index.ts` declares it, one file per tool), `src/services/outils.ts` (**the only reader and writer of `outils_systeme` and `outils_univers`**, and of `systemes_jeu.responsable_id` for the transfer), `src/routes/outils.ts` (thin routes, AD-2). Frontend: `frontend/src/outils/registre.ts` maps `module/outil` to a lazy import of its screen; `frontend/src/ecrans/outils/` holds E-20. One migration of the core, numbered when the slice merges (AD-51): the two tables of `docs/donnees.md` and `systemes_jeu.responsable_id` (nullable, no backfill).

The core implementation uses migration `0019-outils.sql`. Its three write services
take `(db, acteur, cible, cle, valeur)`, where `acteur` is an account id or an `Acteur` and `cible` is
`{ niveau: 'systeme' | 'univers', id }`; settings take `{ version, valeurs, confirme? }` as their value.
`resoudreOutilSysteme` exposes the base resolution for the system settings screen; `resoudreOutil`
exposes the universe resolution. Both return common settings as `communs: { reglages, versionReglages }`.
`responsableEffectif` computes ownership without changing the stored account; `exigerOutil`,
`exigerUsage` and `listerCartesOutil` compose activation, role and item visibility checks for module callers.

**The declaration (AD-121).**

```
DeclarationModule { nom, libelle, reglagesCommuns: Reglages, outils: DeclarationOutil[], routes?(app, garde) }
DeclarationOutil  { cle: 'module/outil', libelle, description,
                    type: 'carte' | 'generateur' | 'aide',
                    portee: 'tous' | { noms: string[] },      // game systems it applies to (C-1)
                    dependances: string[],                    // keys of other tools of the same module
                    visibiliteParDefaut: { mj: boolean, joueur: boolean },
                    usages: { nom, mjSeul: boolean }[],
                    reglages: Reglages,
                    outilsAssistant?(ctx): OutilAssistant[],  // AD-136
                    ecran: { libelle, icone }  }              // sidebar entry, AD-133
Reglage           { libelle, aide?, niveau: 'systeme+univers' | 'univers',
                    forme: booleen | nombre{min,max,entier?} | texte{max} | choix{valeurs}, defaut, protege?: boolean }
```

`verifierDeclaration` runs at startup and in the unit tests: unique keys, dependencies that exist in the module and form no cycle, a default inside its own shape, at most one tool of a key. A malformed declaration stops the server (a developer's error, never a user's). The core imports no module by name: it reads `modulesActifs()`.

**C-1 — Which systems a tool applies to.** `portee: 'tous'` (every game system) or `{ noms }`, names compared after trimming and case-folding. The system has no stable key (`systemes_jeu` carries a name); the SWN tranches choose between the two. *Cheapest to undo*: a key column on `systemes_jeu` replaces `noms` later without touching any stored row. The trial module uses `'tous'`; the unit test covers `noms`.

**Resolution (AD-125).** `resoudreOutil(db, universId, cle)` is the one function; it returns `{ active, visibilite: { mj, joueur }, reglages: { [nom]: { valeur, origine: 'defaut' | 'systeme' | 'univers' } }, versionReglages: { systeme, univers } }`. For each field: the universe row if it is non-null, else the system row, else the declared default (a tool is *disabled* by default). A setting of `niveau: 'univers'` ignores the system row. The common settings of the module (row `outil = '<module>'`) resolve the same way and are returned apart, under `communs`. `accessible(db, acteur, universId, cle)` answers whether the tool is **enabled and visible to the actor**: the role read from `membres` at each call, `visibilite.mj` for a GM outside Player mode, `visibilite.joueur` for a player and for a GM in Player mode; no role = no. **A tool the actor cannot use is `introuvable`** (same response as an unknown address). The tool's own check `peutUsage(acteur, cle, usage)` then applies its declared `usages` (an `mjSeul` usage is refused to a player with `refuse`).

**C-2 — Who writes where (B-57).** `exigerResponsable(db, compteId, systemeId)`: the account is `responsable_id`; when it is null, any GM of a universe attached to the system. Anyone else: a GM of an attached universe who is not the responsible gets `refuse` on a write and reads; an account that is no GM of an attached universe (player, foreigner, instance admin as such) gets `introuvable`. Universe-level writes: `exigerMJ` of the universe. A GM in Player mode has no write (like every service of the product). `creerSysteme` and `creerEtRattacherSysteme` set `responsable_id` to the creator. `transfererResponsabilite(db, compteId, systemeId, versCompteId)`: the caller is the responsible (or any GM when there is none); the target must be a GM of an attached universe, else `invalide` with `champ: 'compte'`; the former responsible keeps their GM role in their universes and loses the system level only. If the responsible account is deleted, `ON DELETE SET NULL` brings the system back to the rule without owner.

**C-3 — Writes.** Three separate service functions per level, so that none rewrites the others: `poserActivation(…, active)` (system: `true`/`false`; universe: `true`/`false`/`null` = follow), `poserVisibilite(…, { mj?, joueur? })` (system: booleans; universe: booleans or `null` = follow), `poserReglages(…, { version, valeurs })` (a value `null` removes the universe override; a system value is never null). Only the settings write bumps `version_reglages` of its row (AD-128: activation never makes a design stale); a stale `version` is `conflit` with `detail: 'version'`. A value outside its shape is `invalide` with `champ` = the setting's name; an unknown setting or a setting of another level is `invalide`. A protected setting (`protege`) is written through the same function but the route requires `confirme: true` in the body, else `invalide` with `code: 'confirmation_requise'` (the screen of the tool shows the confirmation; the trial module has none).

**C-4 — Dependencies (AD-137, B-58).** Activating a tool *at a level* requires every dependency to be effectively active **at that level** after the write (system: system rows; universe: the resolved value for the universe); otherwise `conflit`, `detail: 'dependance'`, message « <Outil> demande <autre outil> : activez-le d'abord. ». The reverse is guarded too: turning a tool off while a dependent is active at that same level is refused with « <Autre outil> demande <Outil> : désactivez-le d'abord. » (`detail: 'dependant'`) — cheaper than a tool left active over a missing one; undone by dropping the check. A dependency never reads another module (AD-137): the trial test only checks the enforcement.

**C-5 — Common API (AD-124).** All under the session guard; errors `code` + `champ` as in `kanevas-import`.

| Route | Who | Gives |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/univers/:id/outils/entrees` | any member | the sidebar's entries: `[{ cle, libelle, icone, chemin }]`, only tools enabled **and** visible to the actor (the client hides an entry whose screen it does not know, as `items.ts` does); never a count of hidden ones |
| `GET /api/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/description` | an actor who can use the tool | label, type, description, settings and their shape, dependencies, usages, effective values; `introuvable` otherwise |
| `GET /api/univers/:id/outils` | GM | E-20, universe tab: every applicable tool, even disabled, with the system level and the universe level side by side, the common settings, dependencies, `version_reglages`; players: `introuvable` |
| `PUT /api/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/activation` · `/visibilite` · `/reglages` | GM | the three writes of C-3 at universe level |
| `PUT /api/univers/:id/outils/:module/reglages` · `PUT /api/systemes/:id/outils/:module/reglages` | GM (universe) · the responsible (system) | the common settings of a module: same body as a tool's `/reglages`, the module key standing where `module/outil` would (no artificial tool key) |
| bodies | | `activation`: `{ "active": true \| false \| null }` (`null` = follow, universe only); `visibilite`: `{ mj?, joueur? }` (booleans, `null` = follow at universe level); `reglages`: `{ version, valeurs, confirme? }`; all answer 204 |
| `GET /api/systemes/:id/outils` | GM of an attached universe | E-20, system tab, with `responsable` (`{ id, nom, vousEtes }`); a non-responsible GM gets the same body with `ecriture: false` |
| `PUT /api/systemes/:id/outils/:module/:outil/activation` · `/visibilite` · `/reglages` | the responsible (C-2) | the same writes at system level |
| `GET /api/systemes/:id/responsable` · `GET /api/systemes/:id/responsable/candidats` | GM of an attached universe | the head of E-15 (`{ id, nom, vousEtes }`) · the GMs the responsibility can go to |
| `POST /api/systemes/:id/responsable` | the responsible | `{ compteId }`, C-2 |
| `/api/univers/:id/outils/:module/…` and `/api/systemes/:id/outils/:module/…` (module routes) | per tool | registered by `DeclarationModule.routes(app, garde)`; `garde('module/outil')` is a pre-handler that runs `accessible` and puts `{ acteur, role, modeJoueur, resolution }` on the request, or answers 404 exactly like an unregistered path |

The `mode=joueur` query of a GM is read by the common pre-handler as everywhere else. The routes are in the guarded scope of `registerSessionRoutes` (AD-15); a tool token (`kvs_…`) opens none of them in this slice (the import scope is `kanevas-outils-catalogue`).

**Assistant (AD-136).** `catalogueDe(db, compteId, universId)` appends, for each tool of `modulesActifs()` with `accessible` true for the role it reads, the tools of `outilsAssistant({ db, compteId, universId, role })`; they follow the `{nom, description, schema, executer}` shape, names prefixed by the module (`essai_…`), and close over account, universe and role. The trial tool `essai_a_decrire` (read-only, no event) returns the tool's label and the effective value of « Pas de charges de carburant ». The catalogue is built at each request, so disabling or hiding takes effect on the next message.

**Interface (AD-133).** The bar asks `…/outils/entrees` when a universe opens and again after any E-20 write in the same session (a refresh, no polling) and builds an « Outils » section (heading, one item per entry, icon from `ecran.icone`), between « Cartes » and « Lore » as the mockup shows; an entry whose `module/outil` is absent from `frontend/src/outils/registre.ts` is not shown. Its address is `/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil`; the generic screen `OutilEcran` first calls the `description` route (404: « Page introuvable. », and **no code of the tool is requested**), then `import()`s the tool's component, shows « Chargement de l'outil… » meanwhile. The trial tools' components are a title, the tool's description and the effective settings (nothing else): they exist to prove the contracts, not as a product screen.

**The three contracts (AD-122), proven by the trial module.** A contract is a TypeScript interface the declaration's `type` selects, with a test that registers one tool of each type by declaration alone and checks what the core does with it: *carte* — `cartes?(db, acteur, universId)` lists `{ titre, visible }` for E-10 (the core does not call it before `galaxie-lire`; the test calls it directly, and a player gets only visible maps); *generateur* — `usages` include « concevoir » and the tool is a candidate for managed sections (the mechanism arrives with `kanevas-outils-sections-gerees`); *aide* — an own entry point, none beyond the sidebar entry. Each contract states in its comment how a changed setting is handled (AD-128): *carte* recomputes on read, *generateur* marks stale (`version_reglages`), *aide* reads live.

**The trial module `essai` and the seed.** Tools: `essai/a` « Essai A » (*carte*, `portee: 'tous'`, setting « Pas de charges de carburant », boolean, `systeme+univers`, default `false`; one assistant tool), `essai/b` « Essai B » (*aide*, depends on `essai/a`), `essai/g` « Essai G » (*generateur*, usage « concevoir » `mjSeul`, setting « Marge d'essai », number 0–100, `niveau: 'univers'`, default 10); the module declares one common setting « Portée d'essai » (number 1–6, `systeme+univers`, default 1) which E-20 shows in its « Réglages communs » card. Default visibility of the three: GM and players. Stub seed (`bouchon/depart.ts`, through the services): accounts `roxane` added; « Système d'essai » (created by Antor, hence responsible) attached to « Éneria d'essai » (Antor GM; Mira GM; Léa and Roxane players) and to « Univers voisin » (Antor GM alone); « Système ancien » with `responsable_id` null, attached to « Univers ancien » (Mira GM). No tool is enabled at seed: the acceptance starts from « Désactivé ». The seed runs only when `essai` is registered.

**Tests.** Service tests on a temporary database: resolution table (system only, universe only, forced, followed, null), C-2 matrix (responsible, other GM, no-owner system, player, foreigner, instance admin, transfer, target not a GM), C-3 (shape, version conflict, protected), C-4 both ways, `accessible` per role and Player mode, the declaration check. Route tests: the common table above, the uniform 404 of a hidden tool compared byte for byte with an unregistered path, and a module route behind `garde`. An assistant test (the tool present for Antor when enabled and visible, absent for Léa when GM-only, absent when disabled). A front test of `OutilEcran` that the module is not imported when the description answers 404.

## Interfaces

`kanevas-import` conventions retained (errors `code` + field, pagination, version). Catalogue item identifiers are stable text **keys**, never row identifiers. Dates are ISO 8601 UTC. A setting has a
declared shape with a schema (zod on the server).

### Detail of `kanevas-outils-catalogue` (the catalogue slice)

Decisions of this slice, taken inside AD-123, AD-124, AD-129, AD-130 and B-61 to B-65 (they amend none). Marked **K-n**. It builds on the cadre's declaration, resolution (`resoudreOutil`, C-1 to C-5) and `services/outils.ts`.

**K-1 — A catalogue belongs to a tool, and a tool declares it.** AD-130 says « one file per module »; B-65 and the token scope say per tool. In this epic they coincide (only `vaisseaux` has a catalogue, `galaxie` has none), and the finer grain is the cheaper to undo: the declaration of a tool (`DeclarationOutil`) gains an optional `catalogue`:

```
DeclarationCatalogue { natures: { nom, libelle, libellePluriel, schema: ZodStrictObject /* fields without cle */,
                                  resume(donnees): string }[],
                       stockage: StockageCatalogue }
StockageCatalogue    { lister(db, cible, nature?, page?): LigneCatalogue[]            // raw rows of ONE level
                       ecrire(db, cible, nature, { cle, donnees, versionCatalogue }): 'cree' | 'modifie' | 'inchange'
                       lirePar(db, cible, nature, cle): LigneCatalogue | null
                       poserMasque(db, universId, nature, cle, masque, versionLue): LigneCatalogue
                       retraits(db, universId): { nature, cle }[]
                       poserRetrait(db, universId, nature, cle, retire): void }
LigneCatalogue       { nature, cle, nom, donnees, masque, version }      // cible = { systemeId } | { universId }
```

The module owns its tables and every SQL statement on them (AD-123); the core owns the algorithm. `verifierDeclaration` also checks: natures with unique names; no nature named like a reserved key (`catalogue`, `version`). A tool without `catalogue` answers `introuvable` on every catalogue route, like an absent route.

**K-2 — The core resolves, once (AD-123).** `src/services/catalogues.ts` is the one place that knows the two levels. `resoudreElements(db, cible: universId, outil, nature)` returns, for a key: the **universe row** if it exists (masked or not, even if the system key is withdrawn), else the **system row** unless a withdrawal of that key exists for this universe. Origins: `systeme`, `univers` (no system row with that key), `remplace` (universe row over a system key), `retire` (system row withdrawn, no universe row; listed for the GM only, with « Rétablir »). A module's computation reads the catalogue **only** through `resoudreElements` / `lireElement`; a player's reading (`acteur` outside GM, or a GM in Player mode) drops every `masque` row and every `retire` entry and carries **no field that says something was dropped** (no count, no gap). `lireCatalogue` is the one read (screen, API, later the assistant): GM → `{ nature, cle, nom, resume, origine, masque, version }`; player → `{ nature, cle, nom, resume, donnees }`. Pagination: 50 per page, `?nature=&curseur=` (the next cursor is the last `cle`, alphabetical by `nom` then `cle`).

**K-3 — Import: parse, preview, apply, over the same function.** `previsualiserCatalogue(db, acteur, cible, outil, fichier)` and `appliquerCatalogue(db, acteur, cible, outil, fichier)` share one pass (`importCatalogue`); the first never writes. The token path, `appliquerCatalogueParJeton` (`services/import-catalogue.ts`), wraps the apply with the batch: it checks the open batch, then counts into it (`noterDansLot`) in the same transaction. File (transport JSON, AD-130):

```
{ "catalogue": "essai/g", "version": "2026-11-essai", "coques": [ { "cle": "…", "nom": "…", … } ], "armes": [ … ] }
```

The file is invalid as a whole — `invalide`, message « Ce fichier n'est pas un catalogue valide. », code `catalogue_invalide`, plus a `cause` (and a `champ` when it names one) that the dialog shows in small text: `pas_un_objet` (not a JSON object), `mauvais_outil` (`catalogue` is not the tool's key; the body carries `attendu` and `recu`: « Ce catalogue est pour « Essai G », pas pour « Essai A ». »), `version` (not a text of 1 to 40 characters), `section_inconnue` (a top-level key that is neither `catalogue`, `version` nor a declared nature; `champ` = the key: « Section inconnue : `canons`. »), `section_non_liste` (a nature that is not an array; `champ`), and `catalogue_trop_grand` beyond 5 000 elements in total (code of its own: « Ce catalogue dépasse 5 000 éléments. »). Above 8 MiB the body limit answers 413 (`fichier_trop_gros`, « Ce fichier est trop volumineux (8 Mio au plus). ») on **all** the routes that take a file — the four session routes of K-7 and the two token routes of K-6 (the import scope already caps its JSON bodies at 8 MiB; the Fastify 413 is mapped to this code in both places). Otherwise each element is checked alone: `cle` (`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$`) then the nature's schema; an element that fails is **refused with its key and its field** (`{ nature, cle, champ, raison }`) and the rest goes through; a repeated key in the file refuses the second occurrence (`champ: 'cle'`, « clé en double dans le fichier »). The reason is built by the core from the schema issue: `nombre attendu` / `texte attendu` / `oui ou non attendu` (also for a missing field), `entier attendu`, `au moins N`, `au plus N`, `valeur attendue : a, b`, `champ inconnu`. For each valid element, `lirePar` on the **target level** decides `ajoute` (no row at that level), `modifie` (canonical JSON of `donnees` or `nom` differs) or `inchange`. The result:

```
{ version, apercu: boolean, natures: { [nature]: { ajoutes, modifies, inchanges, refuses } },
  remplacements,   // universe import only: added elements whose key exists in the system catalogue
  absents,         // rows of this level whose key is not in the file: kept, only counted (AD-130)
  refus: [ { nature, cle, champ, raison } ],   // at most 1 000, `refusTotal` says the true count
  versionCatalogue }                           // the one now in force
```

**Application is one transaction**: the whole file or nothing (the screen's « rien n'est appliqué à moitié »). A failure inside — a storage adapter throwing — rolls back every write, including the `version_catalogue`, and answers 500 `erreur_serveur`; the test injects the failure on the Nth write. **The catalogue version changes only when at least one element was added or modified** (an identical replay stays « 0 ajouté, 0 modifié »; a changed label alone must not make every design stale, AD-128). It is written through `poserVersionCatalogue` of `services/outils.ts` (the only writer of `outils_systeme` / `outils_univers`, cadre): it creates the row when missing **as resolution reads a missing row** (system: inactive, the declared visibility, no settings; universe: every field null) and never touches another column. **A human gesture is never overwritten (B-48)**: an import does not write `masque` and does not touch withdrawals; `ecrire` updates `nom`, `donnees`, `version_catalogue` and bumps the element `version` only when something changed.

**K-4 — Two catalogues, two guards.** System level: `exigerResponsable` (cadre, C-2) — a GM of an attached universe who is not the responsible gets `refuse`, a player or a foreigner `introuvable`. Universe level: `exigerMJ` of the universe, not in Player mode, and the tool must **apply** to the universe's game system (`portee`, C-1) — it need not be enabled: a GM prepares a catalogue before enabling. An universe without a game system, or a tool that does not apply: `introuvable`. Reading the catalogue (K-2) needs the tool `accessible` (cadre): a hidden or disabled tool is `introuvable`, a GM configuring on E-20 reads through the GM list of the same function with the applicability guard instead (`lireCatalogueGestion`), so that « Voir les éléments » works on a disabled tool.

**K-5 — Gestures on an element, and versions (AD-129, B-64).** `masquerElement(db, acteur, universId, outil, nature, cle, { masque, version })`: only a **universe** row (origin `univers` or `remplace`); a system-origin or `retire` key is `invalide` (`champ: 'cle'`, code `element_systeme`) — **no gesture hides a system element**. The `version` is the element's, read with the list; a stale one is `conflit`, code `donnee_modifiee`, « Cet élément a changé depuis que vous l'avez ouvert. »; a successful change bumps the version. `retirerElement(…, { retire })`: only a **system** key with no universe row of that key (`invalide`, code `element_remplace`, otherwise); idempotent by design (withdrawing a withdrawn key, or restoring a present one, succeeds and changes nothing) — a withdrawal has no `version` to compare, the cheapest honest rule; undone by adding one. A withdrawal changes nothing for a design already carrying the element (that is the designer's rule, `kanevas-outils-vaisseaux`); this slice only guarantees that **no read** returns it.

**K-6 — Tool-scoped token (B-65, AD-130).** Table `jetons_outils (jeton_id, outil)` (donnees.md). **A token with no row is the existing content token**: it opens the existing routes and **no** tool route. **A token with rows opens only the routes of those tools** and no content route: two tokens when a script writes both (least privilege, no new column, nothing to migrate for existing tokens). `Jeton` gains `outils: string[]`. In `routes/import.ts` each route declares `config: { portee: 'contenu' | 'outil' }`; the `onRequest` hook, after `verifierJeton`, answers the usual 401 `jeton_invalide` (no batch, nothing else said) when a content route meets a tool token, when a tool route meets a token without that tool, or when the tool key is unknown; the universe check stays as is (404 for another universe). The route is `PUT /api/import/v1/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/catalogue?niveau=univers|systeme&lot=` (the file as JSON body, default `niveau=univers`) and `POST …/catalogue/apercu` (same body, never writes). `niveau=systeme` needs the token's account to be the responsible **at that moment** (read on every use, like the GM role of `verifierJeton`); a GM who is not: 403 `refuse`; an account no longer a GM: already a 401. `POST /lots?outil=module/outil` opens a tool batch (`lots_import.outil`; mandatory for a tool token, forbidden for a content token); `noterDansLot` accepts, for a tool batch, the **natures declared by the tool** (otherwise it throws): counters are per nature and issue like the others (`creees`, `mises_a_jour`, `inchangees`, `refusees`) and each refusal enters the report (`objet` = the nature, `cle`, `raison`, `champ` in the reason text). A closed batch answers 409 `lot_clos`, as for sheets. Without `?lot=` an import leaves no trace but its response. A token is created with its scope in `creerJeton` (`outils?: string[]`, each must be a tool with a catalogue applicable to the universe's system, else `invalide`, `champ: 'outils'`). **E-18's session routes** (the guard of the existing `/api/univers/:id/jetons` routes, GM) follow: `POST …/jetons` accepts `outils`; `GET …/jetons` returns each token with its `outils` (`[{ cle, libelle }]`; a key the registry no longer knows keeps its key as label); a new `GET …/jetons/outils` returns the tools that can be offered, `[{ cle, libelle, module }]` — the tools with a catalogue that apply to the universe's game system, **an empty list for a universe without one** (the screen then disables the choice); `GET …/lots` returns, for a tool batch, `outil: { cle, libelle }` and `natures: [{ nom, libelle }]` (the tool's declared natures, in order, so that the screen labels the counters without knowing the tool).

**K-7 — Routes (common, AD-124).** Session guard; the screen goes through the same functions as the API.

| Route | Who | Gives |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /api/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/catalogue/apercu` · `…/application` | GM (K-4) | the report of K-3; body = the file; limit 8 MiB |
| `POST /api/systemes/:id/outils/:module/:outil/catalogue/apercu` · `…/application` | the responsible | same, system level |
| `GET /api/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/catalogue?nature=&curseur=` | any member, tool accessible | K-2 list (player shape or GM shape) |
| `GET /api/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/catalogue/gestion?nature=&curseur=` | GM | the GM list on a tool that may be disabled |
| `GET /api/systemes/:id/outils/:module/:outil/catalogue?nature=&curseur=` | GM of an attached universe | the system's own rows, read only |
| `PUT /api/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/catalogue/:nature/:cle/masque` | GM | K-5 `{ masque, version }` |
| `PUT /api/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/catalogue/:nature/:cle/retrait` | GM | K-5 `{ retire }` |

The list of tools (`GET …/outils`, cadre C-5) gains, **for a tool that declares a catalogue**, `catalogue: { natures: [{ nom, libelle }], systeme: { version, comptes }, univers: { version, comptes } }` (`comptes`: rows per nature at that level; `version` null when none was imported). The trial route tests compare an unreadable catalogue's response byte for byte with an unregistered path, like the cadre.

**K-8 — The trial catalogue.** `essai/g` declares two natures — `coques` (`nom`, `masse` number ≥ 0, `cout` integer ≥ 0) and `armes` (`nom`, `masse` number ≥ 0, `cout` integer ≥ 0, `portee` choice `courte`/`longue`) — stored in `essai_catalogue_elements (niveau, systeme_id | univers_id, nature, cle, nom, masse, cout, portee, masque, version, version_catalogue)` and `essai_catalogue_retraits (univers_id, nature, cle)` (docs/donnees.md). The migration creates them even where the module is not registered (two small empty tables): the cheapest to undo is dropping them, and a module's tables arriving only in stub would make the migration depend on a switch. The **invented** file `src/outils/essai/exemples/catalogue-essai.json` carries 12 valid elements, version `2026-11-essai` — `coques`: `corvette`, `cargo`, `fregate`, `patrouilleur`; `armes`: `laser`, `laser-lourd`, `canon-leger`, `mitrailleuse`, `torpille`, `missile`, `brouilleur`, `harpon` — and **fixes these keys for every later test** (a task may add other files, never change this one); the tests derive the faulty variant (an arme without `masse`, key `laser-lourd`) from it; nothing of Éneria or of the book is in it (AD-131). `src/outils/essai/catalogue.ts` is the adapter.

**Files.** `src/services/catalogues.ts` (K-2, K-3, K-5; the only reader of a module's `StockageCatalogue`), `src/routes/outils.ts` (the session routes, extended), `src/routes/import.ts` (the tool route and the scope check), `src/services/jetons.ts` and `lots.ts` (K-6), `src/outils/essai/catalogue.ts`, frontend `frontend/src/ecrans/outils/catalogue/` (the import dialog and the element list, mounted by `PanneauOutils` in the card) and `frontend/src/ecrans/acces-api/` (E-18 scope and tool batches). One core migration (numbered at the merge, AD-51): `jetons_outils`, `lots_import.outil`, and the two `essai_*` tables.

**Tests.** Services on a temporary database: the K-3 file matrix (valid, each invalid-file cause, each element refusal with its field and wording, duplicate key, replay = unchanged, version unchanged on no change, removed-from-file kept and counted in `absents`, atomic rollback by injected failure), the K-2 resolution table (system only, universe only, replacement, replacement masked, withdrawal, replacement of a withdrawn key, player vs GM vs Player mode, no trace in the player shape), K-5 (masking a system key refused, stale version, import after masking leaves it masked, idempotent withdrawal). Routes: guards of K-4 per role, K-6 per token (content token on a tool route, tool token on a content route and on another tool, system level by a non-responsible, an account turned player), byte-identical 404, body size. A front test of the dialog (preview before any write, truncation to 50) and of the list (actions per role).
### Detail of `kanevas-outils-sections-gerees` (managed sections)

Decisions of this slice, taken inside AD-127, AD-128, AD-79 and AD-122 (they amend none). Marked **S-n**. It builds on the framework slice (`accessible`, `peutUsage`, the module registry) and writes no SWN calculation: its generator is the trial tool `essai/g`.

**S-1 — Data (one core migration, number taken at merge, AD-51).** `sections` gains `gere_par TEXT` (`module/outil/type`, null: free) and `source_version TEXT`, both nullable, no default, no backfill; a column `CHECK` keeps `gere_par` of the form `a/b/c`. That `gere_par` and `source_version` are null together or set together is held by the service (SQLite cannot add a table `CHECK` to an existing table; the cheapest to undo). `propositions` is rebuilt (the same procedure as migration 0009, `-- migrate: sans-cles-etrangeres`): `cr_id` becomes nullable, a `source_version TEXT` column is added, and `CHECK ((cr_id IS NULL) = (source_version IS NOT NULL))` says a proposal comes either from a session report or from a recalculation of a managed section; the unique index of pending proposals per (requester, section) is kept. No table is added.

**S-2 — The lock, in one place.** `ecrireContenu` (`services/sections.ts`) gets an optional last parameter `geree?: { outil: string; sourceVersion: string }`. Without it, a section whose `gere_par` is set is refused with `refuse` (`detail: 'section_geree'`, « Cette section est gérée par un outil. ») — after the read check, so a caller who cannot read the section still gets `introuvable`. With it, the write is accepted only if `geree.outil` is the section's `gere_par` prefix; it then runs the same path as any write (audience, `exigerSansBloc`, version, `preparerContenu`, `poserContenu`) and sets `source_version` in the same transaction. **Only `services/sections-gerees.ts` passes that parameter**: a source-scan test fails otherwise. `insererSection` gains optional `gerePar` and `sourceVersion` for creation (still the only `INSERT INTO sections`). Every other writer inherits the lock without a line: the section API `PUT …/contenu`, the assistant's section-writing tool (its refusal carries the same words), proposals applied from a session report (`creerProposition` refuses a managed section with `refuse`), and the import (`assurerFiche` refuses a file section whose key is the key of a managed section of the sheet, `invalide`, `champ: sections[i]`, `code: 'section_geree'`, validated before the transaction). The GM's other gestures stay: rename, reorder, audience, « Retirer la section » (which forgets the tool's data row if the tool references the section with `ON DELETE`; that is the tool slice's choice). The text written goes through the audience rules, so **a text with a `:::mj` block written for a non-GM account is refused** (`exigerSansBloc`): a generator that writes for a player produces no GM block; one that writes for a GM may, and `contenu_public` strips it.

**S-3 — The generator side of the contract (AD-122).** `DeclarationOutil` of type `generateur` may declare `sections: SectionGeree[]`:

```
SectionGeree { type,                        // third part of gere_par
               libelle,
               usage: string,               // the declared usage (AD-126) the writer needs, e.g. 'concevoir'
               recalcul: 'geste' | 'proposition',
               versionSource(ctx): string,  // the current version of the sources (catalogue, settings), cheap, read-only
               produire(ctx): { contenu: string; sourceVersion: string } }  // the text version and the version it was made with
ctx = { db, acteur, universId, ficheId, sectionId | null }
```

`verifierDeclaration` also checks: `sections` only on a `generateur`, unique `type` per tool, and — in the unit test of each module — that `produire(ctx).sourceVersion === versionSource(ctx)` just after. The structured content and the display component belong to the module (its tables, its screen); the section only holds the text version and the lock. `produire` reads what the actor may read (AD-126) and never writes. Trial tool `essai/g` declares two sections: `caracteristiques` (`recalcul: 'geste'`) and `notes` (`recalcul: 'proposition'`), both `usage: 'concevoir'`; its text is « Marge d'essai : <valeur> % » (resp. « Notes — marge <valeur> % »), its source version the string `reglages:<module univers>.<module systeme>/<outil univers>.<outil systeme>` built from the `version_reglages` of the four rows (AD-128: the activation never moves it).

**S-4 — `services/sections-gerees.ts`, the only caller of the lock.** *Writes*: `poserSectionGeree(db, compteId, universId, e)` with `e = { ficheId, outil, type, titre?, sectionId?, audience?, contenu, sourceVersion, version? }`, where `audience` is `{ joueursLisent?, joueursEcrivent?, auteurId?, auteurLit?, auteurEcrit? }` — the columns `insererSection` already takes, read only at creation (an update never changes the audience: that stays the GM's gesture on E-9). It resolves the tool in the registry and refuses with `introuvable` when `accessible` is false (tool disabled, invisible to the role, unknown to the code — the same answer as an absent address, AD-124), with `refuse` when the account lacks the declared `usage` (`peutUsage`), then writes **on behalf of the account**: creation (no `sectionId`) is a GM gesture like `ajouterSection`; an update needs the right to write the section (the GM, or the author with « L'auteur l'écrit », or players when « Les joueurs l'écrivent ») and the read `version`. The account therefore never gets more than it has (AD-126): the generator for Léa without the usage is refused, and the generator for Antor on a section whose author is Léa succeeds because Antor is a GM. A GM in Player mode has no write (routes read `?mode=joueur`). An empty text is `invalide`. *Reading*: `etatGere(db, acteur, universId, section)` returns the `geree` object of the section view, `null` for a free section:

```
geree: { outil: 'module/outil' | null, libelle: string | null, type, lien: string | null,
         etat: 'a_jour' | 'perime' | 'desactive', recalcul: 'geste' | 'proposition' | null,
         peutRecalculer: boolean, peutOuvrir: boolean, propositionId: number | null, composant: boolean }
```

`desactive` when the tool is disabled **or** unknown to the code; then `outil`, `libelle` and `lien` are null and nothing else is offered — the banner has no name, so a hidden tool's name leaks to nobody. A tool enabled but not visible to the reader's role also gives `libelle: null` (« Générée par un outil »), no link, no state. Otherwise `etat` is `perime` iff `versionSource(ctx) !== source_version` — **computed at each read, never stored** (AD-128). `peutRecalculer` is true for an account with the usage and the right to write the section, outside Player mode; `peutOuvrir` for the GM outside Player mode and the section's author; `lien` is the tool's sidebar path (`ecran`, C-5 `chemin`) with `?fiche=<ficheId>`, which a tool may ignore; `composant` is true when the tool has a display component for the type (the client then loads it). A view of a managed section **never carries `contenu` (the source)** — nobody edits it here — only `html` rendered from the text version; `peutEcrire` is false; the search, `lireSection` in `'markdown'` form for the assistant and the export read `contenu`.

**S-5 — Recalculating.** `recalculerSection(db, compteId, universId, ficheId, sectionId, version)` (`recalcul: 'geste'`): checks as above, calls `produire`, writes through the lock; if the section is already up to date it answers the unchanged view without moving `version`. On a `'proposition'` type it is `invalide` (`code: 'recalcul_sur_proposition'`). `proposerRecalcul(db, compteId, universId, sectionId)` (GM, `'proposition'` type, section stale, else `conflit`/`a_jour`): files a proposal (`cr_id` null, `source_version` = `produire`'s, `version_origine` = the section's `version`), replacing the requester's pending one (AD-79). `appliquerProposition` branches on `source_version`: set, it calls the managed write with that `sourceVersion` (the same checks: usage, tool enabled; a section changed since is `section_modifiee`); abandoning is unchanged. A proposal is read, applied and abandoned only by its requester (AD-79 unchanged). **Who files them without a gesture**: `signalerSourceModifiee(db, compteId, universId, cle)` — called by `poserReglages` at **universe** level inside its transaction, after the write, with the GM who wrote — files a proposal for that GM on every `'proposition'` section of that universe whose tool belongs to `cle` (a module or a tool) and is stale. The `'geste'` sections only become stale on read. A **system**-level write files nothing (it would write in universes on behalf of GMs who did nothing): the stale state shows in each attached universe, and its GM files the proposal with « Proposer un recalcul ». The catalogue slice calls the same function when an import changes a catalogue (the token's account is the GM). *Cheapest to undo*: drop the call; the sections then only show « Calculée avec d'anciens réglages » and offer the gesture.

**S-6 — Routes (thin, AD-2, in the guarded scope).** `POST /api/univers/:id/fiches/:fid/sections/:sid/recalcul` `{ version }` → the section view; `POST …/sections/:sid/recalcul/proposition` → the proposal view (201). The section view of `GET …/fiches/:fid` and of the existing section routes carries `geree`. `PUT …/sections/:sid/contenu` on a managed section answers 403 `refuse` with `code: 'section_geree'`. The trial module's own route, behind `garde('essai/g')`: `POST /api/univers/:id/outils/essai/g/sections` `{ ficheId, type, sectionId?, auteurId?, version? }` creates or rewrites the trial section through `poserSectionGeree` (it exists only where the `essai` module does: stub or test, AD-121 (module declaration)). Refusal `code`s: `section_geree`, `recalcul_sur_proposition`, `a_jour`; the others are those of the section and proposal services.

**S-7 — The browser.** `frontend/src/outils/registre.ts` (framework slice) gains a second map, `module/outil/type` → lazy `import()` of a display component `({ universId, ficheId, sectionId, html, etat }) => JSX`. E-9 requests the component **only for a managed section whose view says `composant: true`** (so a sheet with no managed section, and a disabled tool, load nothing); the component fetches its structured data by its module's routes, and when it fails or is absent, E-9 shows the text version (`html`) with « Mise en forme indisponible — version texte affichée. ». The banner, its actions and its states are in `docs/ecrans.md` (« Screen details for `kanevas-outils-sections-gerees` »). The trial tool has a tiny component for `caracteristiques` (a framed rendering of the text, `data-testid="composant-essai"`) so that the lazy loading is proven; `notes` has none.

**S-8 — Tests.** Services, on a temporary database with the trial module: the lock (the section API, the assistant tool, a proposal from a report, the import — each refused; the free section untouched), the source-scan test, the write on behalf (Antor GM writes a section authored by Léa; Léa without the usage refused; a player author with the usage and « L'auteur l'écrit » succeeds; a `:::mj` text for her refused), the stale state (settings write → `perime`, content and `version` unchanged; `recalculerSection` → up to date with the new `source_version`), the disabled tool (`desactive`, text kept, write `introuvable`), the hidden tool (name withheld), the `'proposition'` section (proposal filed by the setting write for the GM who wrote it, none on a system-level write, ignored leaves the text, applied updates it and its `source_version`, a second setting write replaces the pending one), search finding the text, a GM-only managed section invisible to Léa, `produire`/`versionSource` agreement. Routes: the 403 and its code, the view's `geree` per role. Front: the lazy component is not requested before a sheet shows it; states of the banner; 360 px.

## Deployment and operations

No new service, secret or volume. Module migrations run at startup through the existing runner (AD-14). The CI-published image (semver tag) is pinned in `k8s-home-lab` through a prepared PR, which Monsieur merges.
We know it works through `/healthz` (version) and acceptance in stub mode. Backup: the existing database carries everything; tool data is part of it.

### Detail of `kanevas-outils-galaxie-lire` (the galaxy map, read side)

Decisions of this slice, taken inside AD-122, AD-123, AD-126, AD-132, AD-133, AD-134 and AD-136 (they amend none). Marked **G-n**. It builds on the framework of `kanevas-outils-cadre` (declaration, `garde`, `accessible`, `resoudreOutil`, the `carte` contract); where it needs one more field of that contract it says so (G-8).

**G-1 — Files and layers.** SQL and guards live in `src/services/swn/` (AD-2: only `services/` reads or writes tables): `galaxies.ts` (galaxies, reading level, the row filters of G-2, listing for E-10), `lecture-carte.ts` (view by blocks, system, search), `trajet.ts` (journey), `cache-blocs.ts`. The rules are **pure functions without SQL or clock** in `src/outils/swn/regles/`: `hex.ts` (axial coordinates, distance, hexagon to pixel and back, polygon test), `generation.ts` (blocks), `chemin.ts` (shortest path and duration), `format.ts` (« 4,5 jours »). `src/outils/swn/index.ts` is the module declaration (`nom: 'swn'`, `portee: 'tous'`: `systemes_jeu` has no key, so the module applies to every game system and, in stub mode, to « Système d'essai »; one tool `swn/galaxie`, type `carte`, `visibiliteParDefaut {mj: true, joueur: true}`), `src/outils/swn/routes.ts` the module routes, `src/outils/swn/assistant.ts` the assistant tool. Frontend: `frontend/src/outils/swn/galaxie/` behind the registry of `frontend/src/outils/registre.ts` (dynamic import, AD-133). The migration takes its number at merge (AD-51) and creates the four tables of `docs/donnees.md` **complete** (including the columns `kanevas-outils-galaxie-tenir` writes: `fiche_id`, `section_id`, `version`), so that slice adds no migration for them.

**G-2 — Reading levels.** `niveauLecture(db, acteur, universId)` is the one place a role becomes a level: `'mj'` (a GM outside Player mode), `'joueur'` (a player, and a GM in Player mode, `?mode=joueur` read by the common pre-handler), `null` (no role, tool not `accessible`: everything answers `introuvable`). A galaxy that is not `visible` is `introuvable` at level `'joueur'` for a player; for a GM in Player mode it is `refuse` with `detail: 'mode_joueur'` (same convention as `lireCarte`). Row filters, written once in `galaxies.ts` and used by **every** read (view, system, search, journey, assistant, cache): level `'joueur'` sees systems `secret = 0`, zones `secret = 0`, routes `publique = 1` **whose two ends are visible systems**; level `'mj'` sees all. No function returns a count of what is filtered out.

**G-3 — Generation of a block (AD-132).** Pure: `bloc(graine, bx, by, champDensite) → {q, r}[]`. Block `(bx, by)` holds the hexagons `5·bx ≤ q ≤ 5·bx+4`, `5·by ≤ r ≤ 5·by+4` (`bx = floor(q / 5)`, so negative coordinates work). For each hexagon, `u = first 6 bytes of SHA-256("<graine>:<q>:<r>") / 2^48` and a system exists when `u < densite(q, r)`; the same `u` for the same triple, on every machine and every restart (tested with literal expected positions for a fixed seed on the trial data). **Density** of a hexagon: among the zones *readable at the caller's level* that contain its centre (polygon test), the **lowest** `densite` (a zone with `densite` null is ignored for this purpose); none: the galaxy's `densite_defaut`. The *defined* systems are **not** consulted: a defined system replaces the generated one on its hexagon **at read time**, only if the caller can read it. So a secret system's hexagon shows, for a player, exactly what it would show without that system. An anonymous system has no name, no link and no stored identity until promoted (`kanevas-outils-galaxie-tenir`); the panel shows « Système non défini » and its coordinates. *Cheapest to undo*: the density rule (lowest wins) is one function.

**G-4 — The block cache key (resolves the [TO VERIFY] of AD-132).** The cache holds only the *generated* positions of a block, never defined data (read fresh at each request under G-2). Key: `(galaxie_id, graine, bx, by, niveau, empreinte)` where `niveau ∈ {'mj','joueur'}` is the reading level of G-2 and `empreinte` is the SHA-256 of the sorted `(cle, version)` of the zones readable at that level — computed at each request (a galaxy has dozens of zones), so editing, adding or removing a zone, or changing the seed, changes the key and a stale entry is simply never asked again. Player mode and ordinary players share `'joueur'`: there is **no per-player knowledge** (mandate decision), so the key cannot be finer without being useless, and cannot be coarser without leaking. Memory only, least-recently-used, 4 000 entries. `cache-blocs.ts` exports `viderCacheBlocs()` and a `compteurs()` (hits, generations) for tests. *Tests*: Antor warms a block, Léa reads it with zero extra generation of a `'mj'` entry and equal to her cold read; a block under a secret zone is identical for Léa with and without the zone (the zone added then removed in the test).

**G-5 — Journey (B-72).** Pure `chemin.ts`. Lengths come from the stored `longueur` of readable routes (undirected). With engine `n` (integer 1–6, chosen on the screen, default 2), the **range** of a leg is `p × (n + 1)` cases where `p` is the module's common setting `portee_saut` (cases per engine level, integer 1–20, default 5, `systeme+univers`: the book's `5 + 5n`). A route longer than the range **is not usable** (it is not cut in two). Shortest path: Dijkstra on total length over usable routes; ties by fewer legs, then by the lexicographic list of system `cle`s (deterministic). **Duration** = Σ over legs of `longueur / n + 1` days (one day of transit per leg: the rule of Monsieur's current tool, « Theryn → Éneria, 7 cases, engine 2 = 3,5 + 1 = 4,5 jours »). Shown rounded to the nearest tenth, half up, comma as separator, trailing « ,0 » dropped, « 1 jour » singular below 2: `« 7 cases · 4,5 jours »`. *Hexagon distance* (measurement, E-21) is `(|Δq| + |Δr| + |Δq+Δr|) / 2`. `routes` = `toutes` is honoured at level `'mj'` only; at `'joueur'` it behaves as `publiques` (no error, no signal). A departure or arrival that is not a readable system is `introuvable`; no usable path is `{ trouve: false }` with one fixed text « Aucun trajet avec ce moteur. », never saying whether a hidden route, or a stronger engine, would give one. *Rejected*: minimising duration instead of length (the book's tool minimises length; both agree on the reference cases, length is the decided behaviour).

**G-6 — Routes (AD-124, AD-126).** Module routes behind `garde('swn/galaxie')`, under `/api/univers/:id/outils/swn/galaxie/`, all `GET`, thin over the services:

| Route | Gives |
|---|---|
| `galaxies/:gid` | `{ id, titre, visible, version, niveau, porteeSaut, reference }` (`niveau` lets the screen show GM controls later; `visible` only for `'mj'`; `reference` is `{ cle, nom, q, r }` or null — the galaxy's first *readable* defined system by identifier, computed at the caller's level, so the screen can write « 7 cases depuis Port-Sel » whatever blocks are loaded; a secret system is never the reference of a player) |
| `galaxies/:gid/blocs?de=bx,by&a=bx,by` | the rectangle of blocks, at most 12 × 12 (`invalide` above): `{ blocs: [{bx, by, generes: [[q, r]…]}], systemes: [{id, cle, nom, q, r, taille, couleur}], routes: [{id, depuis: {cle, q, r}, vers: {cle, q, r}, longueur, publique?, type_lien?}], zones: [{id, nom, contour, couleur, secret?}] }`: systems inside the rectangle, routes with at least one end inside, zones whose bounding box meets it; `publique` and `secret` only at level `'mj'` |
| `galaxies/:gid/systemes/:cle` | the panel: `{ nom, q, r, taille, couleur, notes, routes: [{ « avec »: {cle, nom}, longueur }], lien }` (`lien` = the stable address, G-7); notes as plain text |
| `galaxies/:gid/recherche?q=` | among readable systems, name containing the text after folding case and accents, 20 at most, ordered by name; `q` under 2 characters: empty list; the answer is a bare array `[{cle, nom, q, r}]`, not an object |
| `galaxies/:gid/trajet?depart=<cle>&arrivee=<cle>&moteur=<n>&routes=toutes\|publiques` | `{ trouve, etapes: [{cle, nom, q, r}], troncons: [{depuis, vers, longueur, jours}], cases, jours, libelle }`; `routes` absent means `toutes` (a player never sees the private routes anyway), any other value is `invalide` |

Every unknown, hidden or foreign identifier is the same 404 as an unregistered path (compared byte for byte in the tests, as `cadre` does). The view carries no more than the requested rectangle; there is no « whole galaxy » route.

**G-7 — The address of a system (AD-134, B-68).** `/univers/:id/outils/swn/galaxie/:gid?systeme=<cle>`: the stored identifier is the system's `cle` (stable across a re-import: the import keys every element by `cle`). Opening it loads the galaxy, centres on the system and opens its panel; a `cle` the caller cannot read gives the same « Page introuvable. » as a missing one. The screen keeps `?systeme=` in sync with the selection (history `replace`), so the address bar always holds a link to read. Nothing but the two identifiers is in the address (no coordinates, no name).

**G-8 — E-10 lists the galaxies; the `carte` contract gains an `id`.** `cadre` declares `cartes?(db, acteur, universId) → { titre, visible }[]`; this slice makes it `{ id, titre, visible }[]` (and amends `cadre`'s contract test) so that the entry has an address. The core function `listerCartesOutils(db, acteur, universId)` (in `services/outils.ts`) walks the active modules, keeps those whose tool is `accessible` for the actor and returns `{ module, outil, id, titre, visible }`; a player gets only visible ones. The route that lists maps for E-10 appends them with `type: 'outil'` and the address `/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/:id`; the core knows no module by name (AD-121 (module declaration)). The tool's screen is `OutilEcran` of `cadre`, which resolves the path segment after the tool to the module's component props `{ galaxieId }`.

**G-9 — Assistant tool (B-69, AD-136).** `swn_galaxie_trajet({ galaxie?, depart, arrivee, moteur?, routes? })`: names, not identifiers; `galaxie` optional when the actor reads exactly one (none readable: `'Introuvable.'`; several: `{ ok: false, erreur }` with the message « Précisez la galaxie. », which lists nothing); a system name is matched after folding case and accents among **readable** systems; none or several: `{ ok: false, erreur: 'Introuvable.' }` / `'Plusieurs systèmes portent ce nom.'` (the second says nothing about hidden ones: ambiguity is computed on readable systems only). The result is the object of the route above, so that screen and assistant agree. Actor `{compteId}`, no Player mode (as every assistant tool); never an `evenement`. Declared by `outilsAssistant` and present in `catalogueDe` only when the tool is `accessible`.

**Data additions (see `docs/donnees.md`).** Two columns beyond the framing's table, both needed by G-3: `swn_galaxies.densite_defaut` (real, 0–1, default 0.2) and `swn_zones.densite` (real 0–1, null = no effect on density). Coordinates are integers with `CHECK (abs(q) <= 100000 AND abs(r) <= 100000)`. A zone's `contour` is a JSON array of `[q, r]` vertices of a simple polygon in axial coordinates (closed implicitly). The test is made **in axial coordinates**, the centre of a hexagon being its `(q, r)` itself: the axial-to-plane map is linear, so containment is the same in either frame and no conversion is needed; a centre exactly on an edge or a vertex counts as **inside** (tested: the secret system of the trial galaxy, on the edge of « Marches interdites »). *Cheapest to undo*: columns with defaults, no stored data derived from them.

**The trial galaxy and the stub seed.** « Galaxie d'essai » in « Éneria d'essai » (`visible`, seed `essai-1`, `densite_defaut` 0.2): systems « Port-Sel » (0, 0), « Vael » (7, 0), « Hesper » (1, 5) and « Station fantôme » (−4, 2, `secret`); routes Port-Sel–Vael (7 cases, public), Vael–Hesper (6, public), Port-Sel–Hesper (6, **private**); zone « Marches interdites » (secret, `densite` 0, contour `[[-12,2],[-4,2],[-4,8],[-12,8]]`). All invented. Expected by hand, written in the tests: Port-Sel → Vael, engine 2, public: `7 cases · 4,5 jours`; Port-Sel → Hesper, engine 2: Antor (all routes) 6 cases, `4 jours`; Léa (public) via Vael 13 cases, `8,5 jours`; with engine 1 (range 10) Léa's path is still 13 cases and `15 jours` (7 and 6 are within range); the « no path » case is tested by adding in the test a 12-case route as the only link and engine 1 (12 > 10).

**Interface (AD-133).** One SVG with pointy-top hexagons in axial coordinates; pan by pointer drag, zoom by wheel and pinch (Pointer Events, two pointers), hexagon size 10–60 px, the lowest zoom such that at most 60 × 60 hexagons (12 × 12 blocks) are visible. The view computes the blocks it shows, asks the missing ones (debounced 150 ms, at most 4 requests in flight), keeps them in memory keyed by `(bx, by, niveau)` and drops those far from the view; a change of Player mode empties the store. Labels collapse below a zoom threshold and are truncated at 24 characters on the map (the panel shows the full name, 80 at most). Hover (mouse) or a tap (touch) on a system shows its name and distance in hexagons from the reference system (the one chosen as departure, else the `reference` of the galaxy's answer, G-6 — chosen because the exit criterion asks for a distance before any departure is chosen). Touch targets are at least 44 px (the maquette's `.t`); below 760 px the panels are under the map. Colours come from `frontend/src/ui/tokens.css` only; a system's `couleur` is one of the palette's named colours, never a free value (the column holds the name; the write is `galaxie-tenir`).

**Tests.** Pure: hexagon distance and conversion (round trip), generation (literal positions for a fixed seed, same call twice, negative coordinates, density 0 and 1), density by lowest zone, journey (the by-hand cases above, tie-break, range, no path), duration formatting (4,5 / 4 / 8,5 / 1 jour). Services on a temporary database with the stub seed: G-2 matrix (Antor, Mira, Léa, Roxane, a foreigner, Antor in Player mode on a visible and on a hidden galaxy), search never finds « Station fantôme » for Léa, a route to a secret system added in the test is absent from her view, journey and panel, the generated block over the secret hexagon equal with and without it, the cache (G-4). Routes: the table of G-6, the 404 compared byte for byte, a tool disabled or invisible. Assistant: same result as the route, absent when the tool is invisible, `{ok:false}` without event. Screen: Chromium at 390 px (see `docs/ecrans.md`).

### Detail of `kanevas-outils-galaxie-tenir` (the galaxy map, write side)

Decisions of this slice, taken inside AD-126, AD-127, AD-128, AD-129, AD-130, AD-132 and AD-135 (they amend none), on top of G-1 to G-9. Marked **T-n**. Every write is a function of `src/services/swn/` (AD-2); the routes are thin; the pure rules are in `src/outils/swn/regles/`.
