**T-1 — One write guard.** `exigerEcritureGalaxie(db, acteur, universId, galaxieId)` is the only place a write is allowed: `niveauLecture` (G-2) `null`, or a galaxy the caller cannot read → `introuvable`; a GM in Player mode → `refuse` (`detail: 'mode_joueur'`); a player on a galaxy they read → `refuse`; only a GM outside Player mode passes. It returns the galaxy row. Creating a galaxy has no galaxy yet: `exigerMJOutil` (same levels, same words) guards it. The routes read `?mode=joueur` as for the reads; the assistant has **no** write tool on the galaxy (hors tranche: the read tool of G-9 stays alone). *Cheapest to undo*: one function, called first by every write.

**T-2 — Versions (AD-129).** Each galaxy, system, route and zone carries `version` (starts at 1, +1 on each write to that row). An update or a delete supplies the version it read; a stale one is `conflit` with `detail: 'donnee_modifiee'` (the route sends it as `code`, 409), the same body as a section. A write to a system, route or zone never touches the galaxy's `version`; rename, visibility, seed and the document link share the galaxy's `version`. Moving a system rewrites the `longueur` of its routes in the same transaction and adds 1 to their `version` (a route open on a screen is then stale, which is right: its length changed). Creation takes no version. The reads of level `'mj'` return `version` on every element (G-6 additions: `version` on systems, routes and zones of the view, and on the panel; plus `id`, `secret`, `promu` and the raw `notes` on the panel); level `'joueur'` gets none of them.

**T-3 — Systems.** `placerSysteme(db, compteId, universId, gid, s)` with `s = { nom, q, r, taille?, couleur?, notes?, secret? }`. `nom` 1 to 80 characters after trim, `taille ∈ {petite, moyenne, grande}` (default `moyenne`), stored as the integer 1, 2 or 3 and answered so (an integer from 1 to 5 is accepted as is; the import takes either form), `couleur` one of the six names of the palette (`accent`, `table`, `ambre`, `danger`, `neutre`, `clair`; default `accent`; the screen maps each to a token of `tokens.css`, never a free value), `notes` at most 2 000 characters of plain text, `secret` default false (what the GM places is what players see, unless he ticks it). `q` and `r` are integers within ±100 000 (`invalide`, `champ: 'q'`). The `cle` is the slug of `nom` (lower case, accents folded, `-` for separators), suffixed `-2`, `-3`… on a clash within the galaxy; it never changes afterwards (a rename keeps the link `?systeme=<cle>` valid, G-7). A hexagon already holding a defined system is `conflit` (`case_occupee`). **Placing and promoting are one function**: `promu` is set to true when, at level `'mj'`, a generated system exists at `(q, r)` (the block is generated under the GM's level, G-4) — so the promoted system keeps the location the GM tapped; otherwise it is false (a place where nothing was generated). `modifierSysteme(…, cle, { …champs, version })` changes any field except `cle` and `promu`, including `(q, r)` (the move; T-2 for the routes). `retirerSysteme(…, cle, version)` deletes it and, by `ON DELETE CASCADE`, its routes. Retiring a defined system leaves its hexagon showing what generation draws there (a promoted system's hexagon shows its generated system again): generation reads the seed and the zones, never the defined systems (G-3).

**T-4 — Routes.** `tracerRoute(…, { depuis, vers, publique? })` (the `cle`s of two systems of the galaxy; `publique` default true). The **length is computed** by the grid (`hex.distance`) and written; a body carrying `longueur` is `invalide` (`champ: 'longueur'`, « La longueur est calculée. »). The two ends are different (`invalide`, `champ: 'vers'`) and the pair is unique **whichever the direction** (`conflit`, `route_existante`; G-5 treats routes as undirected). An unknown end is `invalide` (`champ`), not `introuvable`: the GM reads every system. `modifierRoute(…, id, { publique?, type_lien?, version })` changes the visibility and the **type of link** (B-81, `kanevas-outils-format-evolue`) and nothing else: ends never change (remove and draw again). `type_lien` is optional and free text of 1 to 40 characters after trimming (otherwise `invalide`, `champ: 'type_lien'`, « Le type de lien fait 1 à 40 caractères. »; a blank string is not a label **at the service, the route and the import**: it is refused, never turned into « none »; only the form of E-21 turns an empty or blank field into `null` / an absent key before sending); `null` in `modifierRoute` removes it, absent in `tracerRoute` means none. There is no list and no colour per type. Every write that changes it bumps the route's `version` (T-2), so the GM document goes stale by the rule of T-7 with no new code. The view of a route (`vueRoute`, level `'mj'` and level `'joueur'`) carries `type_lien` — a private route is absent from the player's view as a whole, label included (B-73) — and nothing else reads it. `retirerRoute(…, id, version)`. A route is addressed by its `id` in the session API; the import addresses it by the pair of `cle`s (T-9).

**T-5 — Zones.** `dessinerZone(…, { nom, contour, couleur?, densite?, secret? })`, `modifierZone(…, cle, {…, version})`, `retirerZone(…, cle, version)`. `cle` as for systems. `contour` is a polygon or a set of hexagons (Z-2, B-84); a polygon is validated by the pure `contourValide` (`regles/zone.ts`): 3 to 64 vertices `[q, r]`, integers within ±100 000, no two equal consecutive vertices, a **simple** polygon (no edge crossing another, not even at a vertex; a zero area is refused); codes `contour_trop_court`, `contour_croise`, `contour_invalide` (not an array, a vertex that is not a pair, or more than 64 vertices) (`invalide`, `champ: 'contour'`). `densite` is a real from 0 to 1 or null (the screen shows 0 to 100 %). `secret` default false. Changing a zone (outline, density, secret flag) changes the fingerprint of G-4 and so the generated systems, with no cache to purge; a **promoted** system never moves.

**T-6 — The galaxy's life.** `creerGalaxie(db, compteId, universId, { titre })`: `titre` 1 to 80, `visible` false, `graine` drawn by `crypto.randomBytes(6).toString('hex')`, `densite_defaut` 0.2, `version` 1, a GM outside Player mode, tool `accessible`. `renommerGalaxie`, `rendreVisibleGalaxie(…, visible, version)` (the E-10 « Rendre visible » / « Cacher aux joueurs » call it too). `changerGraine(…, { graine, version, confirme })`: `graine` 1 to 64 characters; `confirme` must be exactly `true`, else `invalide` (`champ: 'confirme'`) — the server holds the confirmation, not only the screen; defined systems (placed or promoted) keep their place, every generated one moves (AD-132). `supprimerGalaxie(…, version, vu)` with `vu = { systemes, routes, zones }` the counts the GM read in the dialog: the function recounts in its transaction and, if they differ, answers `conflit` (`contenu_change`) with the new counts, deleting nothing. The count is what the confirmation sentence states; `contenuGalaxie(…)` gives it (and the title of the document sheet, if any). Deleting removes the galaxy and its elements (`ON DELETE CASCADE`) and, if it has a document, **retires its managed section** through `retirerSection` on behalf of the GM, in the same transaction (the sheet stays). It is a separate gesture from disabling the tool (AD-135). **Creation from E-10 (G-8 extended)**: the `carte` contract gains an optional `creation: { libelle: « Carte de la galaxie », creer(db, acteur, universId, titre) → { id }, rendreVisible(db, acteur, universId, id, visible, version) }`; `services/outils.ts` exposes `formesCreablesOutils(db, acteur, universId)` (the tools `accessible` that declare `creation`, for a GM outside Player mode, else none); the route that lists maps carries them as `formes` (`{ module, outil, libelle }`) and gives every listed tool map its `outil: { module, outil }` (null for the others), so that « Nouvelle carte » and « Rendre visible » address a tool without the core knowing a module by name. Two **core routes** (in `routes/`, behind `garde`, reading `?mode=joueur`) are their callers: `POST /api/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/cartes` `{ titre }` → `creation.creer` (201 `{ id }`) and `PATCH /api/univers/:id/outils/:module/:outil/cartes/:cid` `{ visible, version }` → `creation.rendreVisible`; an unknown tool, a tool without `creation` or an invisible one is the 404 of AD-124. For `swn/galaxie`, `creer` and `rendreVisible` are `creerGalaxie` and `rendreVisibleGalaxie`: the module routes of T-8 call the same functions.

**T-7 — The GM document is a managed section of the map tool.** S-3 allowed `sections` on a `generateur` only; this slice allows it on a `carte` too (`verifierDeclaration` and its unit test amended). `swn/galaxie` declares one section `{ type: 'document_mj', libelle: 'Document MJ', usage: 'maintenir', recalcul: 'proposition', versionSource, produire }`, `usage: 'maintenir'` being granted to GMs only by the module. `versionSource(ctx)` is the SHA-256 of the galaxy's `titre`, then the sorted `(cle, version)` of its systems and zones and the sorted `(id, version)` of its routes: computed on read from the rows, **nothing stored** (AD-128), cheap for a few hundred elements; a galaxy that no longer exists gives the fixed string `galaxie-supprimee` (it cannot occur once T-6 retires the section, and the state then reads `perime` with no gesture offered). `produire(ctx)` runs at level `'mj'` or throws `introuvable` (defence in depth: it never composes the document for a lower level) and returns `{ contenu, sourceVersion }` where `contenu` is **entirely inside one `:::mj` container**, so that even if the GM later opens the section to players on E-9, `contenu_public` is empty (AD-114: nothing of the document can reach a player). Every name, title and note is **flattened to one line** (line breaks and runs of white space become one space) before it enters the text, and each line of the document starts with `# `, `## ` or `- `: no line of the document can start with `:::`, so nothing typed by the GM can close the container early (AD-114). The text, from the pure `redigerDocumentMJ(vue)`: a title line, `## Systèmes` (one bullet per system by name: `**Port-Sel** (0, 0) · moyenne — notes`, with « (secret) » for a secret one), `## Routes` (`Port-Sel — Vael · 7 cases`, then ` · <type de lien>` when the route has one, then ` · privée` when it is private: `Port-Sel — Vael · 7 cases · Pirate · privée`; the type is flattened to one line like every name, so it cannot close the container), `## Zones` (name, density, « secrète »); ordered by name; capped so that the whole stays under `MAX_CONTENU_SECTION` (the lists are cut and end with « … 37 systèmes de plus non listés. »). `poserDocumentMJ(db, compteId, universId, gid, { ficheId } | { creer: { titre } }, version)`: with `creer`, `creerFiche` of type `savoir` then the section; the section is created with `audience: { joueursLisent: false }` (GM only, B-74) through `poserSectionGeree`, and `fiche_id` and `section_id` of the galaxy are set in the same transaction (the `CHECK` holds because both are set together). A galaxy that already has a document is `conflit` (`document_existant`): to change the sheet the GM removes the section on E-9 (`SET NULL`) and chooses again. After **each** write of T-3 to T-6 that changes the document's source, the write calls `signalerSourceModifiee(db, compteId, universId, 'swn/galaxie')` inside its transaction (S-5): the writing GM gets a proposal on every stale `proposition` section of the tool, replacing his pending one, and nothing is written to the section without his acceptance. *Rejected*: regenerating on every write (a GM would find a text rewritten under his hands, AD-128); `recalcul: 'geste'` (the exit asks for a proposal).

**T-8 — Routes (session, thin).** Under `/api/univers/:id/outils/swn/galaxie/` behind `garde('swn/galaxie')`, all writes reading `?mode=joueur`; an unreadable or foreign identifier is the same 404 as G-6, a refusal 403 `refuse`:

| Route | Does |
|---|---|
| `POST galaxies` `{ titre }` | T-6, 201 `{ id }` |
| `PATCH galaxies/:gid` `{ titre? , visible?, version }` | rename / visibility (one at a time) |
| `PUT galaxies/:gid/graine` `{ graine, version, confirme }` | T-6 |
| `GET galaxies/:gid/graine` | `{ graine }` (GM only, a write guard: the current seed the « Graine… » dialog shows) |
| `GET galaxies/:gid/contenu` | `{ systemes, routes, zones, document: { titre } \| null }` (GM only) |
| `DELETE galaxies/:gid?version=&systemes=&routes=&zones=` | T-6 |
| `POST galaxies/:gid/systemes`, `PATCH …/systemes/:cle`, `DELETE …/systemes/:cle?version=` | T-3 |
| `POST galaxies/:gid/routes` `{ depuis, vers, publique?, type_lien? }`, `PATCH …/routes/:rid` `{ publique?, type_lien?, version }`, `DELETE …/routes/:rid?version=` | T-4 |
| `POST galaxies/:gid/zones`, `PATCH …/zones/:cle`, `DELETE …/zones/:cle?version=` | T-5 |
| `GET galaxies/:gid/document` | `{ fiche: { id, titre } \| null, sectionId, etat, propositionId }` (the state is that of `etatGere`) |
| `POST galaxies/:gid/document` `{ ficheId } \| { creer: { titre } }`, `version` | T-7, 201 |

A write answers the element's new view at level `'mj'` (with its `version`). The recalculation proposal of the document is the common route of S-6 (`POST …/sections/:sid/recalcul/proposition`): no module route duplicates it. Refusal codes: `donnee_modifiee`, `case_occupee`, `route_existante`, `contenu_change`, `document_existant`, `contour_trop_court`, `contour_croise`, `contour_invalide`, and for a set of hexagons `hexagones_vides`, `hexagones_trop_nombreux`, `hexagones_invalide` (Z-2) (the body carries `code` and `champ` as the other tool routes do).

**T-9 — Import by the API (B-65, AD-130, K-6).** The tool `swn/galaxie` declares `importation: { natures: [systemes, routes, zones], appliquer }` (the `DeclarationOutil` gains this optional key beside `catalogue`; `verifierDeclaration` checks the natures are unique). A token's `outils` (K-6) may name any tool that declares a `catalogue` **or** an `importation`; `GET …/jetons/outils` lists both. Routes of `routes/import.ts` with `config: { portee: 'outil' }`: `GET /api/import/v1/univers/:id/outils/swn/galaxie/galaxies` (`[{ id, titre }]`, so a script finds its target) and `PUT /api/import/v1/univers/:id/outils/swn/galaxie/galaxies/:gid?lot=` with the body `{ systemes: [{ cle, nom, q, r, taille?, couleur?, notes?, secret? }], zones: [{ cle, nom, contour (polygon or `{ hexagones }`, Z-5), couleur?, densite?, secret? }], routes: [{ depuis, vers, publique?, type_lien? }] }` (each list optional; at most 2 000, 500 and 4 000 items, else the whole request is `invalide`). The target galaxy must exist (created in Kanevas, T-6): an unknown or foreign `gid` is 404. **Order and atomicity**: one transaction; systems, then zones, then routes; an invalid item is refused with its `champ` (`systemes[3].q`) and counted `refusees`, the others go through. **Key**: system and zone by `cle` (the `cle` of the file is stored as is, it must match `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,63}$`), route by the unordered pair of `cle`s. **Replay**: an item equal to the stored row is `inchangees` (no version bump); a differing one is `mises_a_jour`; a new one is `creees`. **B-48 for the map**: the migration adds a nullable `version_importee` to `swn_systemes`, `swn_routes` and `swn_zones`, written by the import only (the `version` it leaves); an item whose row has `version ≠ version_importee` (edited in Kanevas, or created by hand: `version_importee` null) is not overwritten and is counted `refusees` with the reason « Modifié dans Kanevas depuis le dernier import. » (the same counters and report as the other tool batches, capped at 1 000). Rules of the interactive path hold: a `longueur` in the file is `invalide`, a route to an unknown end (not in the galaxy after the file's systems are applied) is refused with `champ: 'routes[i].vers'` and the rest passes, a hexagon already held by another `cle` is refused (`case_occupee`), `promu` is written false (the import knows nothing of generation). Moving a system by import rewrites its routes' `longueur` like T-2, and a route that was in step (`version = version_importee`) before the move is put back in step (`version_importee` takes its new `version`), so that the next file does not refuse it; a route edited in Kanevas stays out of step. A route compares **the pair, `publique` and `type_lien`** (an absent `type_lien` is « none »: a file that drops a label the stored route carries is a *differing* item — `mises_a_jour` if the route is in step, refused « Modifié dans Kanevas depuis le dernier import. » if the GM edited it); a `type_lien` outside 1–40 characters is refused with `champ: 'routes[i].type_lien'`. An item equal to the file is `inchangees` whatever its `version_importee` (nothing to overwrite); only a *differing* item out of step is refused. Every row the import writes sets `version_importee` to the `version` it leaves. A closed batch answers 409 `lot_clos`; without `?lot=` the import leaves no trace but its response. The write goes through the same service functions as T-3 to T-5 (one rule, two doors), acting as the token's account, still a GM at that moment; `signalerSourceModifiee` is called once per request, not per item.

**Zones as sets of hexagons (`kanevas-outils-format-zones`, B-84, amends T-5 and T-9).**
- **Z-1 Storage.** `swn_zones.plages` (migration `0029-swn-zones-plages.sql`, nullable, no backfill): canonical ranges `[[r, q0, q1], …]` sorted by `r` then `q0`, disjoint and never adjacent. A set zone has `contour = '[]'` and `plages` non-null, a polygon zone the reverse: the service keeps exactly one shape. `regles/forme.ts` is the only reader of the format (`plagesDe`, `developper`, `compterPlages`, `contient`); only the map service reads `swn_zones`.
- **Z-2 Validation.** The pure `formeValide`: a polygon as before, or `{ hexagones: [[q, r], …] }` (no other key: an extra key is `hexagones_invalide`) of 1 to 5 000 pairs of integers within ±100 000; duplicates collapse. Codes `hexagones_vides`, `hexagones_trop_nombreux`, `hexagones_invalide` (`invalide`, `champ: 'contour'`). `dessinerZone` and `modifierZone` accept both shapes; a change of shape rewrites both columns.
- **Z-0 Vocabulary.** The screens and the refusal messages of the map say « territoire » where this document says zone (e.g. « Un territoire a au moins un hexagone… »).
- **Z-3 Reading.** `ZoneVue` of a set zone has `contour: []`, `plages` and `hexagones` (the count); a polygon zone is unchanged (neither key). A block request returns the whole zone when its bounding box meets the rectangle.
- **Z-4 Density.** `densiteHexagone` asks `contient(forme, q, r)` for both shapes (ranges indexed by row once per array); the fingerprint of G-4 still rests on `(cle, version)`.
- **Z-5 Import.** `contour` of a file zone is either shape. A replay compares the shape stored (`contour` text and canonical `plages`): equal is `inchangees`, a different set or a change of shape is `mises_a_jour` (or the B-48 refusal). All the `hexagones` of one request count together: more than 50 000 and the whole request is `invalide` (`champ: 'zones'`); the body limit stays 8 MiB.
- **Z-6 Drawing.** E-21 draws a set zone with `ZoneHexagones` (`frontend/src/outils/swn/galaxie/ZoneHexagones.tsx`): two `<path>` per zone, the fill by bands (one polygon per run of a row) and the outer edge alone, computed on the unit plane and memoised on `(id, version)` (a zone read without a version, as by a player, is keyed by its ranges); panning and zooming only change the group's transform. Rejected: one `<polygon>` per hexagon, a canvas layer. Classes, colours, hatching and label threshold are those of a polygon zone.
- **Z-7 GM document.** A set zone line is `- <nom> · <n> hexagones · densité …` (« 1 hexagone » for a single one); a polygon line is byte for byte as before.
- **Z-8 Secret.** `FILTRE_ZONES` hides a secret set zone from a player like any zone: it neither appears nor enters the density of a player's blocks (B-73).

**T-10 — Interface (AD-133).** Same component tree as G; the write controls exist only when the galaxy's answer carries `niveau: 'mj'`, and **disappear** (are not rendered) in Player mode. The screen's **Mode MJ / Mode Joueur** control is the product's existing toggle (the one that sets `?mode=joueur`), mounted in the header of the screen, which empties the block store on change. Gestures are modes of the SVG with a one-line instruction: *Placer* (tap an empty or generated hexagon: a generated one becomes « Promouvoir »), *« Tracer une route »* (tap two systems), *« Dessiner une zone »* (tap the vertices, « Terminer »). A form replaces the right column's panel (under the map below 760 px); « Annuler » or `Échap` leaves the mode. After a successful write the view reloads **only the blocks the element touches** (an element's blocks by its coordinates, a zone's by its bounding box), keeps the selection, and puts the new element in the panel. A 409 `donnee_modifiee` shows the conflict text inside the form with « Recharger » (reloads the element and replaces the fields). The two destructive gestures (« Graine… », « Supprimer la galaxie… ») are dialogs that state what goes, whose primary button carries the verb (« Changer la graine », « Effacer la galaxie ») and whose focus starts on « Annuler ». Touch: every control is at least 44 px; on a phone the controls fold into « Modifier la carte ▾ ». The component for the document is not needed (a managed section of E-9 renders as text, S-7).

**Tests of this slice.** Pure: `contourValide` (triangle ok; 2 vertices, a bow-tie, a repeated vertex, a vertex out of ±100 000 refused), `cleDe` (accents, clash suffix), `redigerDocumentMJ` (a literal expected text for the trial galaxy, entirely in one `:::mj`, the cut at the ceiling). Services on a temporary database with the stub seed: the write guard matrix (Antor, Mira, Léa, Roxane, Teo, a foreigner, Antor in Player mode on visible and hidden galaxies); every write once by a GM and refused otherwise; version conflicts per element; the move rewriting lengths; secret items placed never appearing in Léa's view, search, journey or panel; seed change keeping defined systems and moving generated ones; delete with a wrong count; the document (created with a new sheet, GM only, absent for Léa, `contenu_public` empty, proposal after a write, text unchanged until accepted); the source-scan test (no `INSERT`/`UPDATE` on `swn_*` outside `src/services/swn/`). Import: scope, replay, B-48 refusal, the refused route with its field, the batch report. Routes: the table of T-8 and the 404 compared byte for byte. Screen: Chromium at desktop and 390 px.

### Detail of `kanevas-outils-vaisseaux` (the ship designer, first version)

Decisions of this slice, taken inside AD-122, AD-123, AD-126, AD-127, AD-128, AD-129, AD-130, AD-133, AD-134 and AD-136. Marked **V-n**. It builds on `cadre` (declaration, `garde`, `accessible`, `resoudreOutil`, `peutUsage`), `catalogue` (K-1 to K-7: the `StockageCatalogue` port, `resoudreElements`, import, masking, withdrawal) and `sections-gerees` (S-3 to S-5: the generator contract, `poserSectionGeree`); it declares the tool `vaisseaux` in the module `swn` that `galaxie-lire` declared (G-1), and amends two of those contracts (V-3, V-4), each time by one optional field.

**V-1 — Files and layers.** SQL and guards live in `src/services/swn/`: `catalogue-vaisseaux.ts` (the `StockageCatalogue` adapter of the seven natures, the only SQL on `swn_catalogue_*`), `conceptions.ts` (create, read, list, save), `calcul-vaisseau.ts` (resolves the choices of a design through K-2, then calls the pure rules). The rules are **pure functions without SQL or clock** in `src/outils/swn/regles/vaisseau.ts` (`calculer`, V-2) and `regles/texte-vaisseau.ts` (the Markdown text version, V-6). `src/outils/swn/index.ts` gains the tool `vaisseaux` (type `generateur`, `portee: 'tous'`, `visibiliteParDefaut {mj: true, joueur: true}`, usages `concevoir` and `creer` (`mjSeul`), one section `caracteristiques` with `recalcul: 'geste'`, `usage: 'concevoir'`, a `catalogue` of seven natures); `routes-vaisseaux.ts` the routes of V-8; `assistant-vaisseaux.ts` the tool of V-9; `services/swn/section-vaisseaux.ts` the section hooks of V-4 and V-5 (`versionSource`, `produire`, `apresEcriture`). Frontend: `frontend/src/outils/swn/vaisseaux/` (E-22) and the display component `caracteristiques` of E-9, both behind the registry (AD-133). The migration (number taken at merge, AD-51) creates `swn_catalogue_{coques,moteurs,amenagements,defenses,armes,modifications,exemples}`, `swn_catalogue_retraits` and `swn_conceptions` as in `docs/donnees.md`; `swn_fiches_generees` is created by `kanevas-outils-vaisseaux-regles`.

**V-2 — The calculation (B-75, B-80).** `calculer(coque, lignes, reglages) → Resultat` is pure and takes **already resolved** elements (V-3): `coque` = `{ cle, nom, classe, cout, pv, ca, puissance, masse, emports, facteur_cout, facteur_puissance, facteur_masse }`, `lignes` = `{ nature, cle, nom, quantite, element }[]`, `reglages` = `{ porteeSaut }` (the module setting `portee_saut`, cases per engine level, G-5). For a line of quantity `q` and an element `e`:
```
cout_ligne      = e.cout      × (e.x_cout      ? coque.facteur_cout      : 1) × q
puissance_ligne = e.puissance × (e.x_puissance ? coque.facteur_puissance : 1) × q
masse_ligne     = e.masse     × (e.x_masse     ? coque.facteur_masse     : 1) × q
munitions_ligne = e.munitions × e.cout_munition × 2^max(0, coque.classe − e.classe_min) × (1 + l.magasins) × q   (weapons only; E-2, E-3)
masse_magasins  = 1 × l.magasins × q                         (weapons only; `MASSE_MAGASIN`, added to the mass used, never multiplied)
prix            = coque.cout + Σ cout_ligne + Σ munitions_ligne
pv              = coque.pv + Σ e.pv × q            ca = coque.ca + Σ e.ca × q
puissanceLibre  = coque.puissance − Σ puissance_ligne        masseLibre = coque.masse − Σ masse_ligne
emports         = { utilises: Σ e.emport × q (weapons), total: coque.emports }
chantierJours   = ceil(prix / 25 000)          entretien = round(prix × 5 %) per year (half rounds up; computed with a 1e-9 tolerance against floating-point error)
porteeSaut      = reglages.porteeSaut × (niveau du moteur + 1) cases, null without engine
```
Munitions are counted by hull class like the book does: they **double per hull class above the weapon's minimum class** (`classe_min`, integer 1–4, optional in a file and 1 when absent; an element handed to `calculer` without it is never multiplied). A launcher may carry **extra magazines**: the optional `magasins` (integer 1–9, weapons only) of a design line, each one adding 1 of mass and one more load of munitions; it is a field of the line (`choix`, example lines included), kept in `Resultat.lignes` only when present and printed `(+2 magasins)` by V-6. Hand-checked synthetic case: a frégate (cost 300 000, factors 5 / 2 / 3) with a weapon of `cout` 12 000 multiplied by the cost factor, `munitions` 10, `cout_munition` 100, `classe_min` 1 costs 300 000 + 60 000 + 10 × 100 × 2 = 362 000 cr (mass free 19), and 366 000 cr (mass free 17) with two extra magazines. The trial weapon `canon-leger` carries `classe_min` 3, so the reference table of V-11 is unchanged.

`25 000` (credits per day of construction) and `5 %` are two named constants of `regles/vaisseau.ts` (`CADENCE_CHANTIER`, `TAUX_ENTRETIEN`): book mechanics, not data, so that the catalogue carries only elements; the book's reference case (949 000 cr → 38 days, 954 000 → 39; 5 % of 949 000 → 47 450) is what `kanevas-outils-eneria` checks them against. The three class factors are **fields of the hull**, not a table in the code: no book value is in the public repository (AD-131). Amounts are integers; `puissance` and `masse` are integers too (schema, V-10), so no floating-point sum decides an alert. **Alerts** (`{ niveau: 'bloquante' | 'a_surveiller', code, texte, ligne? }`, blocking first, then in the order below): *blocking* — `puissance_insuffisante` (« Puissance insuffisante : −2 », when `puissanceLibre < 0`), `masse_insuffisante` (« Masse insuffisante : −3 »), `emports_insuffisants` (« Emports insuffisants : 4 sur 3 »), `limite_depassee` (« « Soute » : 1 au plus. », an element with `limite` above 0 chosen in a larger quantity), `moteur_multiple` (« Un seul moteur par vaisseau. », more than one engine unit), `element_retire` (V-3); *watch* — `moteur_absent` (« Aucun moteur de saut : ce vaisseau ne peut pas voyager. »), `reserve_nulle` (« Plus aucune réserve de puissance. » / « … de masse. », a free budget of exactly 0). A design with a blocking alert **cannot be saved**; the alerts are part of `Resultat`.

**V-3 — The catalogue of the design, read once (AD-123, K-2, B-62; amends K-2 by one flag).** `calcul-vaisseau.ts` resolves the hull and every line by `resoudreElements` / `lireElement` **at the actor's level**, with one flag added to K-2, `inclureMasques: true`, passed **only** for the keys the stored design already carries (`conceptions.ts` knows them; `lireCatalogue` and the choice lists never pass it). So: a masked universe element stays resolvable for the design that carries it (Monsieur's decision of 2026-10-17: the player already knows what is on her ship); a new line cannot be a masked element (`invalide`, `champ: 'choix'`, code `element_indisponible`: for a player the same refusal as an unknown key). A **withdrawn** system key (origin `retire`) or a key present nowhere resolves to nothing: the line keeps the **name stored at the last save** (`resultat.lignes[].nom`, `resultat.coque.nom`) and carries the blocking alert `element_retire` (« Élément retiré de cet univers : Éclaireur. Remplacez-le pour enregistrer. », `ligne` = its index, `-1` for the hull); `calculerDepuisChoix` then returns `{ resultat: null, alertes }` (no number is invented) and the screen shows the **stored** result beside the alert. Reading is `introuvable` when the actor cannot read the sheet or its section (V-7), whatever the catalogue says.

**V-4 — Amends S-3 and S-4: `charge` and `apresEcriture`.** A design is two rows that must never disagree: the managed section (text, `source_version`) and `swn_conceptions` (choices, result, versions). Every write of the section — the designer's save, the GM's creation, the core recalculation route and the application of an update proposal (S-5) — must therefore also write the row. `SectionGeree` gains `apresEcriture?(ctx, ecrit: { contenu, sourceVersion, charge }): void`, `produire` may return `charge` (an opaque value), and `poserSectionGeree`'s `e` gains `charge?`; the hook runs **in the same transaction, after the section write**, and its errors roll the write back. `recalculerSection` and `appliquerProposition` pass the `charge` of `produire` back. The trial tool `essai/g` gets an `apresEcriture` that records its calls, and the contract test is extended (the hook runs once per write; a throw rolls the section back). *Cheapest to undo*: one optional field; without it the designer would write the row beside the lock and the core route would let the two diverge.

**V-5 — The section of a design.** `versionSource(ctx)` = `vaisseaux:r=<module univers>.<module systeme>/<outil univers>.<outil systeme>;c=<catalogue systeme>.<catalogue univers>` (the four `version_reglages` of `resoudreOutil` and the two `version_catalogue` of the tool rows; a missing one is `-`). `produire(ctx)` reads the stored choices, recalculates them **now** against the current catalogue and settings (never writes) and returns `{ contenu: texte (V-6), sourceVersion: versionSource(ctx), charge: { coque, lignes, resultat, versionReglages, versionCatalogue } }`; when a line cannot be resolved it throws `invalide` with `code: 'element_retire'` (« Élément retiré de cet univers : <nom>. Remplacez-le dans le concepteur. »), so a recalculation never writes a partial number. `apresEcriture` writes `coque_cle`, `choix`, `resultat`, `version_reglages`, `version_catalogue`, bumps `version` and sets `section_id`. The staleness (`perime`, S-4) is computed by the core for E-9 as for any managed section; **the stale banner of E-22, its « Recalculer » button and its proof are `kanevas-outils-vaisseaux-regles`** (this slice's screen shows no stale state), while the consistency of the two rows is guaranteed here.

**V-6 — The text version (AD-127).** `texteVaisseau(resultat)` → Markdown, deterministic, placed in `contenu`, in this exact layout (a blank line between blocks, one final newline): `**<coque.nom>** — classe <n>`; a table whose header is `| Mesure | Valeur |` and whose rows are `Prix`, `PV`, `CA`, `Chantier`, `Entretien`, `Portée de saut`, `Puissance libre`, `Masse libre`, `Emports`, in that order; the line `**Choix**` then one list item `- <quantité> × <nom>` per line of the design (order: drives, fittings, defences, weapons, modifications, then by name; the hull is in the first line only); when there are alerts, the line `**Alertes**` then one item per alert, `- **Bloquante** : <texte>` or `- **À surveiller** : <texte>`, blocking first. Credits print as `700 000 cr` (U+00A0 between the digit groups and before `cr`), `Entretien` as `35 000 cr / an`, `Chantier` as `28 jours` or `1 jour`, `Portée de saut` as `15 cases` or `—`, `Emports` as `2 / 3`, a negative number with U+2212 (`−2`). A mass prints with a decimal comma and one decimal when it has one (`7`, `1,5`, `−0,5`: V-10, *Mass*). No `:::` block, no link. It is what search, the assistant, the export and a sheet with the tool disabled read (E-9: « Outil désactivé »).

**V-7 — Who does what (AD-126).** The tool is `accessible` (cadre) or everything is `introuvable`. **Reading** a design = reading its sheet *and* its section (`peutVoirFiche`, `peutLireSection`, the functions `lireSection` uses): the list returns the readable ones only, an unreadable design is the same 404 as an unregistered path; a GM in Player mode reads as a player. **Creating** (`creerConception`) is a GM gesture outside Player mode (usage `creer`, `mjSeul`), on a sheet of type `vaisseau` of the universe with no design yet (`conflit`, code `deja_concue`, otherwise); it creates the section « Caractéristiques » through `poserSectionGeree` (creation) with the audience `{ auteurId, auteurLit: true, auteurEcrit: true }` (the author is any member who reads the sheet, or none: then only GMs write) and the row with the hull alone. **Saving** (`enregistrerConception`) needs the usage `concevoir` and the right to write the section (the GM, or the author with « L'auteur l'écrit »), checked by `poserSectionGeree` **on behalf of the account**, after the read check (an account that cannot read gets `introuvable`, one that reads without writing `refuse`). The save takes `{ coque, choix: [{ nature, cle, quantite }], version }`, `version` being the design's (`swn_conceptions.version`): a stale one is `conflit` with `detail: 'donnee_modifiee'` (« Cette conception a changé depuis que vous l'avez ouverte. »); at most 100 lines, one per `(nature, cle)` (`invalide`, `champ: 'choix'`, code `choix_en_double`), `quantite` an integer 1–99; a blocking alert refuses the save: `invalide`, code `conception_bloquee`, the body carries `alertes` (the reason the screen shows). One transaction. A GM's gestures on the section (rename, audience, « Retirer la section ») stay those of E-9; removing the section deletes the row (`ON DELETE CASCADE`).

**V-8 — Routes (AD-124).** Under `/api/univers/:id/outils/swn/vaisseaux/`, behind `garde('swn/vaisseaux')`, thin over the services; the catalogue itself is read by the **common** route of K-7 (`GET …/catalogue?nature=&curseur=`), not by a module route.

| Route | Gives |
|---|---|
| `GET conceptions` | the readable designs `[{ ficheId, titre, coque: { cle, nom, classe }, prix, bloquantes, aSurveiller, auteur: { id, nom } \| null, peutEcrire }]` ordered by title; `peutCreer` (GM outside Player mode) and, for that GM, `fichesSansConception` (`{ ficheId, titre }[]`, sheets of type `vaisseau` for the dialog) ride along |
| `GET conceptions/:fid` | `{ ficheId, titre, coque, lignes: [{ nature, cle, nom, quantite }], resultat, alertes, version, peutEcrire, lienFiche }`; `alertes` = those stored with the result **plus** the `element_retire` ones of V-3 computed now |
| `POST calcul` | `{ coque, choix, ficheId? }` → `{ resultat, alertes }`, nothing written; `ficheId` lets the masked elements of that readable design through (V-3); any member for whom the tool is accessible |
| `POST conceptions` | GM: `{ ficheId, coque, auteurId? }` → the design view (201) |
| `PUT conceptions/:fid` | `{ coque, choix, version }` → the design view; 409 `conflit` / `donnee_modifiee`; 422 `conception_bloquee` with `alertes`; 403 `refuse` |

`POST calcul` is the only calculation the screen uses: the browser carries no rule (one set of numbers for the screen, the section and the assistant). Unknown, hidden or foreign identifiers are the same 404 as an unregistered path (compared byte for byte in the tests, as `cadre` does). **The address of a design** is `/univers/:id/outils/swn/vaisseaux?fiche=<ficheId>` (the `lien` of S-4's `etatGere`, kept in sync by history `replace`, nothing else in it, AD-134); an unreadable one is « Page introuvable. ». The list is `/univers/:id/outils/swn/vaisseaux`.

**V-9 — The assistant tool (B-69, AD-136).** `swn_vaisseau_calculer({ conception?, coque?, choix? })`: either `conception` (the title of a readable design: its stored result and current alerts, the object of `GET conceptions/:fid`) or `coque` and `choix: [{ nature, nom, quantite? }]` by **names**, matched after folding case and accents among the elements readable by the account (none: `{ ok: false, erreur: 'Introuvable.' }`; several: `'Plusieurs éléments portent ce nom.'`, saying nothing of hidden ones), the object of `POST calcul`; both or neither: `{ ok: false, erreur: 'Précisez une conception, ou une coque et des choix.' }`. An unreadable design is `'Introuvable.'`. Actor `{compteId}`, no Player mode, never an `evenement`, present in `catalogueDe` only when the tool is `accessible`; it calls `calculerDepuisChoix`, **the function behind `POST calcul`**, so that the screen and the assistant agree.

**V-10 — Catalogue format (AD-130, K-1).** Seven natures, key `swn/vaisseaux` (the `catalogue` of the file, verified at import). All fields required (the optional ones are marked below and in F-1: `kanevas-outils-format-vaisseaux-fiches` adds optional fields only, absent = as before), strict, no `cle` in the schema; booleans are « oui ou non » :

| Nature | Fields beyond `nom` |
|---|---|
| `coques` | `classe` integer 1–4, `cout`, `pv`, `ca`, `puissance`, `emports` integers ≥ 0, `masse` a **multiple of 0.5** ≥ 0 (*Mass* below), `facteur_cout`, `facteur_puissance`, `facteur_masse` integers ≥ 1 |
| `moteurs` | `niveau` integer 1–6, then the **element fields** |
| `amenagements`, `defenses`, `modifications` | the **element fields** |
| `armes` | `emport`, `munitions`, `cout_munition` integers ≥ 0, `classe_min` integer 1–4 (optional, 1 when absent), then the element fields |
| `exemples` | `coque` (a hull `cle`), `choix`: `{ nature, cle, quantite, magasins? }[]` (at most 100); each pair must resolve at import among the valid elements of the same file and the **catalogue visible at the target level** (the system's for a system import; the universe's own then the system's for a universe import), otherwise the example is refused with `champ: 'choix'` and the reason `élément inconnu : <nature>/<cle>` |

*Optional fields of this table* (hull speed, armour, crew bounds; element class range, condition, speed bonus; weapon damage, penetration, qualities, tech level; example crew): F-1 lists them with their ranges; none is required, none appears below.

*Element fields*: `cout`, `puissance`, `pv`, `ca` integers ≥ 0, `masse` a multiple of 0.5 ≥ 0; `x_cout`, `x_puissance`, `x_masse` booleans (multiplied by the hull's factor); `limite` integer ≥ 0 (greatest quantity per design, 0 = none). `description`, `source`, `nom_en` are the optional **foundation columns** of docs/donnees.md, stored and shown nowhere in this slice. The adapter writes **one statement set per nature**, built once from the declared columns (not seven copies); `masque` and the withdrawals go through `poserMasque` and `poserRetrait` of K-1 (`swn_catalogue_retraits`).

**Mass (B-80, `kanevas-outils-format-evolue`).** A `masse` is a number that is a multiple of 0.5 (`2 × masse` is an integer), 0 to 1 000 000 (so a sum never leaves the range where a binary float holds a multiple of 0.5 exactly): `0.3`, `1.25`, a string, `null` or a negative are `invalide` with `champ: 'masse'` (« nombre attendu » is the reason already shown for a missing or non-numeric mass; a fraction other than .5 reads « multiple de 0,5 attendu »). The columns `masse` of the six tables carrying it become `REAL` with `CHECK (typeof(masse) IN ('integer','real') AND masse >= 0 AND masse * 2 = CAST(masse * 2 AS INTEGER))` — a migration rebuilding the six tables (`-- migrate: sans-cles-etrangeres`, AD-120's procedure, copying every row and index; existing integer masses are copied as they are, nothing is rewritten, K-4's catalogue version does not move). **Exactness needs no decimal library**: a multiple of 0.5 times an integer factor and a quantity, and sums of such numbers, are exact in binary floating point, so `calculer` (V-2) keeps its formulas and `masseLibre` is exact; `masseLibre < 0` is the `masse_insuffisante` test and `masseLibre === 0` the `reserve_nulle` one, unchanged. A single pure function `texteMasse(n)` (`regles/`) prints a mass for V-6, R-3 and the alert texts; the browser has its own twin for E-22 (`frontend/src/outils/swn/vaisseaux/`), both pinned by the same table of cases (`7`→`7`, `1.5`→`1,5`, `−0.5`→`−0,5`). The import compares masses with `===` (the file's `1.5` and the stored `1.5` are the same number). The catalogue editor (E-20) shows the catalogue's own number with the same rule. *Rejected*: storing half-units as an integer (`masse2`): every reader and every file would have to know the doubling; a decimal library or fixed-point arithmetic: not needed for multiples of 0.5; any finer unit (out of the tranche).

**V-11 — The trial catalogue and the stub seed (AD-131).** The **invented** file `src/outils/swn/exemples/catalogue-essai.json` (version `2026-11-essai-vaisseaux`) fixes these keys and numbers for every later test (a task may add files, never change this one; nothing of the book or of Éneria is in it):

| Nature · key (name) | Values (the order of the V-10 tables; element fields: cout, puissance, masse, x_cout/x_puissance/x_masse, pv, ca, limite) |
|---|---|
| coques · `eclaireur` (Éclaireur) | classe 1, cout 20 000, pv 8, ca 14, puissance 10, masse 8, emports 1, factors 1 / 1 / 1 |
| coques · `fregate` (Frégate d'essai) | classe 2, cout 300 000, pv 30, ca 14, puissance 20, masse 20, emports 3, factors 5 / 2 / 3 |
| coques · `croiseur` (Croiseur d'essai) | classe 3, cout 1 000 000, pv 60, ca 15, puissance 50, masse 40, emports 5, factors 10 / 3 / 4 |
| moteurs · `moteur-1` (Moteur 1) | niveau 1 · 5 000, 2, 1, oui/non/non, 0, 0, 0 |
| moteurs · `moteur-2` (Moteur 2) | niveau 2 · 12 000, 3, 2, oui/oui/non, 0, 0, 0 |
| amenagements · `soute` (Soute) | 10 000, 0, 3, oui/non/oui, 0, 0, **limite 1** |
| amenagements · `cabines` (Cabines) | 8 000, 1, 2, oui/non/oui, 0, 0, 0 |
| defenses · `blindage` (Blindage) | 4 000, 0, 2, oui/non/oui, pv 5, ca 1, 0 |
| armes · `laser` (Laser) | emport 1, munitions 0, cout_munition 0 · 25 000, 2, 1, oui/oui/non, 0, 0, 0 |
| armes · `canon-leger` (Canon léger) | emport 1, munitions 10, cout_munition 100 · 12 000, 1, 1, oui/non/non, 0, 0, 0 |
| modifications · `renfort` (Renfort de coque) | 5 000, 0, 1, non/non/oui, pv 4, 0, 0 |
| exemples | the seven designs below, each `{ coque, choix }` |

The **universe** file `src/outils/swn/exemples/catalogue-essai-univers.json` holds one weapon, `lance-d-essai` (Lance d'essai): emport 1, munitions 0, cout_munition 0 · 30 000, 6, 1, oui/**non**/non, 0, 0, 0. Setting `portee_saut` = 5 (the default). **Reference designs**, calculated by hand from V-2 (the test writes the expected values literally; a mismatch is a bug of the code or of this table, never a reason to adjust the test):

| Example (hull · lines) | Prix | PV | CA | Puiss. libre | Masse libre | Emports | Chantier | Entretien | Portée | Alerts |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `eclaireur-nu` — eclaireur · moteur-1 | 25 000 | 8 | 14 | 8 | 7 | 0/1 | 1 j | 1 250 | 10 | — |
| `eclaireur-arme` — eclaireur · moteur-1, laser, blindage | 54 000 | 13 | 15 | 6 | 4 | 1/1 | 3 j | 2 700 | 10 | — |
| `fregate-de-ligne` — fregate · moteur-2, cabines, soute, laser ×2 | 700 000 | 30 | 14 | 5 | 1 | 2/3 | 28 j | 35 000 | 15 | — |
| `fregate-a-l-extreme` — fregate · moteur-2, cabines ×2, laser ×3 | 815 000 | 30 | 14 | 0 | 3 | 3/3 | 33 j | 40 750 | 15 | watch `reserve_nulle` (puissance) |
| `fregate-gourmande` — fregate · moteur-2, cabines ×3, laser ×3 | 855 000 | 30 | 14 | −1 | −3 | 3/3 | 35 j | 42 750 | 15 | blocking `puissance_insuffisante` (−1), `masse_insuffisante` (−3) |
| `croiseur-de-transport` — croiseur · moteur-2, soute, cabines, laser ×2, canon-leger, renfort, blindage | 1 966 000 | 69 | 16 | 27 | 3 | 3/5 | 79 j | 98 300 | 15 | — |
| `fregate-sans-moteur` — fregate · cabines, laser | 465 000 | 30 | 14 | 15 | 13 | 1/3 | 19 j | 23 250 | — | watch `moteur_absent` |

Named cases: a frégate with **two** soutes (`limite` 1) is refused with `limite_depassee`; « Vent d'Orak » plus a second `lance-d-essai` has `puissanceLibre = 4 − 6 = −2`. **The stub seed** (« Éneria d'essai ») enables the tool `vaisseaux` for the system (GMs and players), imports the system catalogue and the universe catalogue (the Lance), and creates three sheets of type `vaisseau`: « Vent d'Orak » (frégate · moteur-2, soute, laser, lance-d-essai: prix 685 000, 28 j, entretien 34 250, PV 30, CA 14, puissance libre 4, masse libre 7, portée 15; author Léa; section readable by Léa and the GMs only), « Étoile du soir » (eclaireur · moteur-1, laser: prix 50 000, 2 j, entretien 2 500, PV 8, CA 14, puissance libre 6, masse libre 6, portée 10; author Léa; section readable by players) and « Aube de Sel » (no design yet, for « Nouvelle conception »). So Roxane reads « Étoile du soir » and gets « Page introuvable. » on « Vent d'Orak ».

**V-12 — The browser (AD-133).** `frontend/src/outils/swn/vaisseaux/` holds the list, the workshop and the E-9 component; nothing loads unless the tool is `accessible` and the screen is opened (the sidebar item comes from `cadre`'s declaration). The workshop keeps its draft in memory (never in browser storage), asks `POST calcul` on every change (debounced 250 ms; the last answer wins, an older one is dropped) and shows the last good result while a calculation is in flight; « Enregistrer » is disabled while a blocking alert shows or a calculation is pending, and a refused save shows the server's reason. The choice lists are the common catalogue route (50 per page, loaded page after page per nature); the origin « Univers » and, for a GM, « Masqué » are shown. Colours only through `tokens.css`; at 390 px one column, the results panel whole in the flow above the choices — neither sticky nor height-capped, so figures, alerts, « Enregistrer » and the reason are all readable (arbitrated 2026-11-03: sticky, visible catalogue and readable alerts do not fit together on a phone); from 760 px the panel is the right-hand column, sticky under the frame bars (`top` = stub banner + 52 px). The E-9 « Caractéristiques » table fills the sheet's width (13 px text) and scrolls sideways inside its frame, with edge shadows (a `local`/`scroll` gradient pair) showing that columns are hidden.

**Tests (the proof is in the plan's tasks).** Pure: the seven reference designs literally, the named cases, the alert order, `chantierJours` on an exact multiple (28 exactly, not 29), the text version byte for byte. Services on a temporary database with the trial catalogue: every role on every read and write (Antor, Mira, Léa author, Roxane, Teo, a GM in Player mode, the stale version, the blocking save, the two-save conflict), masked / withdrawn / replaced elements on an existing design (V-3), the section and the row agreeing after each write (V-4), `produire` / `versionSource` agreement. Routes: the 404s byte for byte, the 403/409/422 bodies. Assistant: the same numbers as `POST calcul`, `{ok:false}` without event. Frontend: states, 390 px, the draft never in storage. Accuracy on Éneria's real values is **not** tested here: `kanevas-outils-eneria` does it in the pods (AD-131).

### Detail of `kanevas-outils-vaisseaux-regles`

Decisions of this slice, taken inside AD-79, AD-126, AD-127, AD-128 and AD-131. Marked **R-n**. It builds on `vaisseaux` (V-1 to V-12: the design's section, its hook `apresEcriture`, the catalogue adapter, the routes), on `sections-gerees` (S-3 to S-5: staleness computed at each read, `recalculerSection`, `proposerRecalcul`, `signalerSourceModifiee`, the banner of E-9) and on `catalogue` (K-2, K-5). Nothing here adds a core contract: the module declares more sections and one more usage, and calls three core functions that already exist.

**R-1 — Seven more managed sections, one per generated sheet.** The tool `vaisseaux` declares, besides `caracteristiques`, seven sections of `recalcul: 'proposition'` and `usage: 'generer'` whose `type` is the `cle` of `swn_fiches_generees`: `regles-coques`, `regles-moteurs`, `regles-amenagements`, `regles-defenses`, `regles-armes`, `regles-modifications` and `exemples` — built by one loop over the seven natures of V-10, not seven copies. The type carries the nature, so `produire(ctx)` needs no lookup (at creation `ctx.sectionId` is null and no row exists yet). The usage `generer` is a new declared usage with `mjSeul` (like `creer`): « Générer… » is a GM gesture, outside Player mode. The sheets are of type `regle`, titled « Règles — Coques », … « Règles — Modifications » and « Exemples de vaisseaux », each holding one section « Règles » (resp. « Exemples ») created with `joueursLisent: true`, no author, nobody but the generator writes it (S-2); the sheet itself is readable by every member (a player's reading is `introuvable` only when she has no role, as for any sheet).

**R-2 — Produced at a player's level, never at the actor's.** A rule sheet is one text read by every member, so `produire` reads the catalogue **as a player would** (K-2: the actor in Player mode, whoever the generating GM is): masked elements and withdrawn keys are absent, with no placeholder and no count (B-77, AD-126). An example appears only if its hull and **every** element of its `choix` resolve at that level, otherwise it is omitted whole; its numbers are those of `calculerDepuisChoix` (V-3) on the elements so read, i.e. the same pure rules as a design, never a second calculation. A GM who wants to see the masked elements reads the catalogue (E-20), not these sheets. *Rejected*: producing at the GM's level and letting the section audience hide it — the audience is a flag on the section, the text would still hold the secret in `contenu` and in the search index (AD-114's `contenu_public` only strips `:::mj` blocks).

**R-3 — The text of a rule sheet (pure, `regles/texte-regles.ts`).** `texteRegles(nature, elements)` → Markdown, deterministic, no `:::` block, no link: the line `**<libellé de la nature, pluriel>** — <n> éléments` (`1 élément`; `0`: the line is kept, then the sentence `Aucun élément dans le catalogue.` and no table), then **one table**, header `| Nom | <the nature's columns> |`, rows ordered by `nom` then `cle`, each cell an integer (U+2212 never appears: all values are ≥ 0), credits as in V-6 (`700 000 cr`), a boolean multiplied-by-the-hull column printed `× coque` or `—`, `limite` `—` when 0. Columns by nature — coques: Classe, Coût, PV, CA, Puissance, Masse, Emports, Facteur de coût, Facteur de puissance, Facteur de masse; moteurs: Niveau, then the element columns; armes: Emport, Munitions, « Coût d'une munition », then the element columns; others: the element columns — *element columns*: Coût, Puissance, Masse, PV, CA, Limite, and the three `× coque` flags as `Coût × coque`, `Puissance × coque`, `Masse × coque`. A local element is marked `(Univers)` after its name. `texteExemples(exemples)`: the line `**Exemples** — <n>` (`0`: the line is kept, then `Aucun exemple.` and nothing else), then for each example, ordered by `nom`, `### <nom>`, the line `<coque> — classe <n>`, the list of its choices as in V-6 and the same measures table as V-6 (the function reuses `texteVaisseau`'s blocks, so the layout cannot drift). No description, no source, no book text: the sheets hold the catalogue's **numbers and names**, which the GM imported (AD-131).

**R-4 — The version of a rule sheet (AD-128).** `versionSource(ctx)` = `regles:c=<catalogue systeme>.<catalogue univers>;m=<empreinte>` for the six rule types, with `;r=<reglages as V-5>` added for `exemples` only (a jump range is printed there and nowhere else). `empreinte` is the first 12 hex digits of the SHA-1 of the sorted list of `<nature>/<cle>` a player does **not** see in this universe (masked universe rows, withdrawn system keys) **of the sheet's own nature** — for `exemples`, of every nature, since an example disappears when any element it uses is hidden — `-` when there is none, so a mask touches only the sheet of its nature (and the examples): a mask or a withdrawal changes what a player reads without moving `version_catalogue` (K-5), so without it the sheet would stay « à jour » while holding a hidden element. Reading it is two small queries (`lister` of the masked rows and `retraits`), cheap and read-only as S-3 requires. `produire(ctx).sourceVersion === versionSource(ctx)` just after, for each of the seven types (the module's unit test).

**R-5 — `swn_fiches_generees` is written by the hook.** `produire` returns `charge: { cle }`; `apresEcriture` (V-4, same transaction) inserts the row at creation (`univers_id`, `cle`, `fiche_id`, `section_id`, `source_version`) and updates `source_version` at each later write, whoever wrote: the generation, an applied proposal (S-5) — so the row and the section never disagree. The hook (`src/services/swn/regles-hooks.ts`) writes that table and `src/services/swn/fiches-regles.ts` reads it (through `ligneGeneree`); no other code touches it. The migration (number taken at merge, AD-51) creates it as in `docs/donnees.md` plus `UNIQUE (section_id)`; no `CHECK` on `cle` (the code is the guard, as for `fiches.type`).

**R-6 — « Générer les fiches de règles… ».** `etatFichesRegles(db, compteId, universId)` → `[{ cle, libelle, ficheId \| null, titre, etat: 'absente' \| 'a_jour' \| 'perimee', propositionId \| null }]` (seven, in the order of R-1); `genererFichesRegles(db, compteId, universId)` → `{ creees, misesAJour, inchangees, fiches }`. Both are GM only (usage `generer`: `refuse` otherwise; `introuvable` when the tool is not `accessible`, the order of V-7). Generation is **one transaction** and goes through `poserSectionGeree` on behalf of the account: for each `cle`, the row of `swn_fiches_generees` and its sheet exist → the section is rewritten (`sectionId` + `version`) — **unless the text equals the stored one and `source_version` is current**, which counts « inchangée » and moves nothing (no `version` bump, no `modifie_le`); the row is missing → the sheet is created through `creerFiche` (type `regle`) and its section through `poserSectionGeree` (creation). A GM's click is the consent that B-67 requires, so an existing sheet is rewritten **directly** here, while the *automatic* path (a catalogue import, a setting) only files a proposal (R-7); a proposal pending on a section rewritten here becomes `perimee` by the rule of AD-79 (its `version_origine` moved). A deleted sheet is forgotten by the cascade and recreated by the next generation; a section removed by « Retirer la section » while the sheet stays is recreated **as a new sheet** (the old one stays the GM's, untitled-collision allowed): the cheapest to undo, noted on the screen. Routes behind `garde('swn/vaisseaux')`: `GET fiches-regles`, `POST fiches-regles` (200 with the report, no body).

**R-7 — Regeneration on proposal: the three triggers.** (1) A catalogue **import** at universe level: the catalogue slice already calls `signalerSourceModifiee` when `version_catalogue` moves (S-5); nothing to add. (2) A **mask or withdrawal** of an element (`masquerElement`, `retirerElement`, K-5) moves what a player reads but not `version_catalogue`: this slice adds the same call after those two writes, by the GM who made the gesture — one line in each, in the transaction. (3) A **setting** write at universe level: `poserReglages` already calls it. A system-level write files nothing (S-5): each attached universe shows « Calculée avec d'anciens réglages » on the stale sheets and its GM files the proposal with « Proposer un recalcul » (E-9). The proposal's content is `produire`'s text at the time it is filed; `appliquerProposition` re-checks the `source_version` through the managed write (S-5), so an element masked between the filing and the applying makes the proposal `perimee`, not wrong. **Known window, decided here**: between a mask and the acceptance of the proposal, the rule sheet still shows the element it was generated with — Monsieur's decision is that a sheet changes only on the GM's agreement (B-77, AD-128), and the GM's agreement is a click he can give at once: after a successful mask E-20 reads `GET fiches-regles` (R-6) and, when the sheet of the element's nature (or the examples) is `perimee` with a proposal, adds the line « Les fiches de règles montrent encore cet élément : acceptez la mise à jour proposée. » with a link to it; the core's mask answer carries nothing for it. Nothing is hidden from the GM, and he can close the window by « Générer… » as well. *Reversed if* Monsieur prefers that a mask rewrites the sheets at once.

**R-8 — Staleness and « Recalculer » of a design.** The state is the core's (S-4: `etatGere`, computed at each read); this slice adds no stored flag. `GET conceptions/:fid` gains `perime` (boolean), `peutRecalculer`, `sectionId` and `sectionVersion` (the **section's** version, which the core's recalculation route compares) — read from the same `etatGere` as E-9, so E-9 and E-22 cannot disagree; `GET conceptions` rows gain `perime` (the version of the sources is the same for every design of a universe, so it is computed once, not per row). « Recalculer » calls the **core** route `POST …/sections/:sid/recalcul { version: sectionVersion }` (S-6), which runs `produire`, writes through the lock and runs the hook of V-4: the text, `swn_conceptions` (choices untouched, result and both versions renewed) and `version` move together. `produire` throws `element_retire` when a line no longer resolves: the recalculation is refused with that reason and nothing moves (V-5). **A save also brings the design up to date** — it writes `versionSource(ctx)` of the moment (V-5) — and the screen says so before the click (E-22). The assistant's `swn_vaisseau_calculer` (V-9), reading a stored design, gains `perime` in the object it returns, so that a stale figure is never given as current (B-67); `POST calcul` is unchanged (it always computes now).

**R-9 — The browser.** `frontend/src/outils/swn/vaisseaux/` gains the stale banner and « Recalculer » of the workshop, the badge of the list, and the dialog « Générer les fiches de règles… » (loaded with the screen, AD-133). The workshop of a stale design opens on the **stored** result (the one the section holds); any edit replaces it by the live calculation (`POST calcul`), the banner staying. The tool disabled or invisible: the banner, the button and the dialog are absent because the screen itself is (V-12). Tests of the module use only the invented catalogue of V-11 (its seven examples, `lance-d-essai` in the universe file); no real name appears in a fixture (AD-131).

**Tests (the proof is in the plan's tasks).** Pure: `texteRegles` and `texteExemples` on the invented catalogue, literally, the order, the `0 élément` and `Aucun exemple.` cases. Services on a temporary database with the stub seed: a setting write makes « Vent d'Orak » `perime` with its text and `version` unchanged; a universe catalogue import does the same; `recalculerSection` renews the text and the row together; a design with a withdrawn element refuses to recalculate and keeps its text; a mask by Antor leaves Léa's design as it is and files a proposal on the rule sheet of the GM who masked; generation creates seven sheets, run twice creates nothing and moves nothing; a masked weapon is absent from « Règles — Armes » and from every example that uses it; Roxane reads the sheets and cannot write them; Teo (no role) gets `introuvable`; a player calling « Générer » gets `refuse`. Browser at 390 px: the banner, « Recalculer » and the dialog.


## Security

The threat: a GM secret (hidden system, private route, territory, hidden design) reaching a player through the map, search, a journey, generated density, a link, the API or the assistant. Countermeasures:
item-by-item permissions in module functions (AD-126), server-side generation under permissions (AD-132), invisible tool = not found (AD-124), tool-scoped token, locked managed section (AD-127). No Éneria secret in the public repository or its logs (AD-131).

## Stubs

No new dependency requiring a token, account or authentication: modules make no external call. The assistant keeps its stub (`KANEVAS_STUB=1`, AD-78), whose script can call modules' assistant tools.
Acceptance runs in stub mode, on the invented trial catalogue.

## Deferred

| Item | What will reopen it |
|---|---|
| Shared journal and dice | the faction turn epic (`kanevas-factions`), first user |
| “Game aid” tool type | the faction turn manager |
| Printing a sheet (rendering a tool section, otherwise its text version) | a core function, later |
| Offline map | a need for use without a network |
| Extracting common parts between modules | a second module (D&D) resembling the first |
| User JSON export / import of a design | not wanted (the import API covers transport) |
- *(kanevas-fiches-liees)* The attitude of a relation, the superior of a member, the pin — reopened
  with the first table that asks ; an assistant tool that links or searches by link — reopened
  when the assistant is asked « qui habite Port-Sel ? » ; natures for aventures — with the first
  need ; a side panel for « Fiches liées » on desktop — « on verra à l'usage » (Monsieur, 2026-10-25).
- *(kanevas-scenarios)* A calendar of sessions and a play log per scenario — reopened if the GM
  asks for the history of a campaign (B-59 says where, not when).
- *(kanevas-scenarios)* Status automation (a CR closes a scenario) — reopened at the first
  scenario the GM forgets to set « Terminée ».
- *(kanevas-scenarios)* An index on the status — reopened above ten thousand adventures.

## Scope of `kanevas-scenarios` (2026-10-25)

**Target repositories.** `AntorFr/kanevas` only (existing) : the type, the charge and its route, the sheet
blocks, the migration, the import and the assistant ; rank 1. No GitOps change : the image takes its
tag at the delivery (Renovate, then production).

**Components and state.** Unchanged : one server, one SQLite base, the frontend. The services `campagnes`
and `scenarios` disappeared (`kanevas-scenarios-suivi`) ; `preparation` and `comptes_rendus` read an adventure's sheet id ; the
type registry (`types-fiche`) carries the adventure and its charge schema ; the frontend gains a
registry of sheet blocks (AD-124) next to the section blocks and the header lines.

**Interfaces.** The routes table above (sheets with `type=aventure`, `PATCH {statut}`, `?statut=`,
`/fiches/:fid/taches`, comptes-rendus with an adventure id) ; the import contract (AD-125) ; the
assistant tool (AD-126). Errors keep the sheets' forms (`introuvable` identical to an unknown id for
what the caller does not read).

**Deployment and operations.** Unchanged (chart, Traefik, `/mnt/data/kanevas`) ; the migration
plays at start-up (AD-51; the backup of the file is a manual gesture before the deploy) ; the start-up log (`comptesDuPivot`, `app.ts`) says the counts converted.

**Security.** The threat is the sheets' : a player who reads what a GM closed. An adventure adds
nothing to it — its visibility is its sections' ; the status is public like PJ/PNJ and says nothing
of the content ; the preparation tasks are GM-only under AD-47. One point to watch : a scenario
that the import opens to the players by mistake — the import sets no audience the script does not
ask for, and a scenario's sections are born closed.

**Stubs.** None new in this slice : its stub seed carried no adventure (`kanevas-scenarios-import-assistant` seeded « La Couronne brisée » and « Acte II ») ; the import sample (`src/bouchon/echantillon`) carries two campaigns and two scenarios as `aventure` sheets. In this slice the assistant's stub kept no sentence about campaigns; `kanevas-scenarios-import-assistant` restored the two through `creer_fiche` (AD-126).

**Deferred.** See the list above.

## Detail of `kanevas-scenarios-import-assistant` (2026-10-25)

Third slice of the epic: it reopens, on the sheets, the two write paths that the pivot slice removed
with the tables. It adds no migration, no table and no route (AD-125, AD-126 hold as written). What it
fixes beyond them:

**Import of an adventure** (`src/services/import-fiches.ts`, `lireCorps` / `ecrire`).
- `TYPES_IMPORT` already derives from the type list, so `aventure` is accepted once the type exists.
  `statut` is read by `lireCorps`: `undefined` (says nothing), or one of the four values of AD-123.
  Any other value, or a `statut` on a type other than `aventure`, is a 400 `invalide` with
  `champ: statut` before any write (counted as « 1 refusée » with `?lot=`, no trace without it).
  The sentence names the four values.
- Creation: the charge is `{v: 1, statut: statut ?? 'en_preparation'}` for an adventure (the `pj` charge
  of a character is the only other one built here); the sheet is born through `creerFiche` like any
  other, `sousType` and `parent` by the code that already serves them. The import asks for no audience
  the script does not give: the sections follow `joueursLisent` / `joueursEcrivent` (closed by default),
  so a campaign pushed without them stays the GM's — the GM opens « Présentation » (decision of
  Monsieur, 2026-10-25).
- Replay: `MarqueFiche` gains `statut`; the comparison is the one of `sousType` (equal: mark it; equal
  to the last import's mark: write it with `changerStatutFiche`; else warn « statut modifié dans
  Kanevas : non écrasé »). An absent `statut` leaves the status alone. A type change towards
  `aventure` writes the charge with the wanted status (default `en_preparation`) and a change away from
  it drops `marque.statut`, as `marque.sousType` is dropped today.
- Compte-rendu: `campagne` is the origin key of an **adventure sheet of the token's universe**
  (`fiches.type = 'aventure'`, `cle_origine`); an unknown key, or a key of a sheet of another type, is
  the existing refusal (400 `invalide`, `champ: campagne`, « campagne inconnue : <clé> »); the id goes
  to `charge.campagne_id` through `creerCompteRendu` as before; the replay keeps the adventure and warns
  « campagne non modifiable, ignorée ». The lookup replaces whatever the pivot left; the sample orders
  the adventures before the reports.
- `GET /` : `types` already lists `aventure` with its activation ; `limites` loses `nomCampagne`,
  `titreScenario` and `contenuScenario` (the pivot removes them with the routes; this slice checks they
  are gone) and gains `statuts: ["en_preparation","prete","en_cours","terminee"]`, so the script knows
  the vocabulary without reading the code.
- The lot counts an adventure in `fiches` (no new nature): E-18 needs no change.

**Assistant tool** (`src/services/assistant/catalogue.ts`). `creer_fiche` is GM-only (the catalogue
chosen from the role, AD-26): `{type: z.enum(TYPES_FICHE minus compte_rendu), titre, sousType?,
parentId?}`. `compte_rendu` is left out because a report needs its adventure and has its own creation
(AD-61); `creerFiche` would refuse it anyway. One transaction: `creerFiche` (which refuses a disabled
type with `type_desactive`, said « Ce type est désactivé dans cet univers. »), then `deplacerFiche`
if `parentId` is given (the one writer of `parent_id`, AD-99) — a parent the GM cannot read, of
another universe or unknown answers « Introuvable. » and nothing is created. Success returns the
event `fiche_creee`, `libelle` « Fiche « <titre> » créée », `cible: {type: 'fiche', ficheId}`;
the event types `campagne_creee` and `scenario_cree`, and the targets `campagne` / `scenario`, leave
the server types, `frontend/src/ecrans/assistant/fil.ts` and `blocs/registre.ts` (the block
`Ecriture` serves `fiche_creee`, « Ouvrir » leads to `/univers/<id>/fiches/<id>`). A refusal returns
`{ok: false, erreur}` with no event (AD-76).

**Stub of the assistant** (`assistant/bouchon.ts`). The two sentences stay: « crée une campagne
« X » » → `creer_fiche {type: 'aventure', titre: X, sousType: 'Campagne'}`; « crée un scénario « X »
dans « Y » » → `chercher {mots: Y, type: 'aventure'}` for the parent (exact title, else « Introuvable. »),
then `creer_fiche {type: 'aventure', titre: X, sousType: 'Scénario', parentId}`. Answers keep their
wording (« Campagne « X » créée. », « Scénario « X » créé dans Y. »); in Léa's session the tool is
absent and the existing « absent » answer is given.

**Stub seed and sample.** `src/bouchon/depart.ts` seeds « La Couronne brisée » (Campagne, `en_cours`,
« Présentation » open to the players), « Acte II — Le sceau brisé » (Scénario, `prete`, under it,
sections closed), two preparation tasks and the report « Séance 12 » naming the campaign — the content
the pivot's browser proofs created by routes, now in the seed (its test in `depart.test.ts`).
`src/bouchon/echantillon/echantillon.json` drops `campagnes` and `scenarios`: its campaigns and
scenarios become entries of `fiches` (`type: aventure`, `sousType`, `statut`, scenarios with `parent`
= the key of the campaign), listed before the reports that name them; `client-import.ts` drops the two
routes and the two counters (the schema loses `campagnes` and `scenarios`) and the sample's README
says it. `npm run import:echantillon` on a fresh base ends with « Fiches » counting the adventures,
no refusal other than the planned ones of `refus.json`. The README (root) recipes that said « Crée une
campagne » and the import recipe follow.

**Security.** Nothing new is readable: the import and the tool write only; the event and the answers
name only what the GM may read; a scenario pushed by the import has closed sections unless the script
opens them, so no title or text reaches a player by this path.

**Contract note for Monsieur** (his script is outside the product): `PUT …/campagnes/:cle` and
`…/scenarios/:scle` are gone; a campaign is `PUT …/fiches/<cle>` with `type: "aventure"`, `sousType:
"Campagne"`, `statut`; a scenario is the same with `parent: "<campaign key>"`; a report keeps
`campagne: "<key of the adventure>"`; `GET …/univers/<id>` gives `statuts`. It is written to the
epic's discussion when the slice is delivered.

## Cadrage `kanevas-fiches-liees` (2026-10-25)

**Dépôts cibles.** `AntorFr/kanevas` only (existing), rank 1 ; the epic's branch comes from
`epic/kanevas-scenarios` (the sheet blocks of AD-124) and lands after it. No GitOps change.

**Organes et état.** Unchanged : one server, one SQLite base, the frontend. New : `services/natures.ts`
(the list), the route `…/fiches/:fid/liees`, the search conditions in `listerFiches`, a sheet block
« Fiches liées » in the registry of AD-124, the « gone » mark in the charte's components.

**Interfaces.** The routes table above ; the import contract (AD-134) ; the natures route.

**Déploiement et exploitation.** Unchanged ; the migration plays at start-up after backup (AD-51).

**Sécurité.** The threat is the sheets' : a player who learns what a GM closed — here through a
count, a group, a path in the tree, a search by link. Every count, line and result passes the two
guards of AD-64 (the section and the source sheet) ; the descent of the tree never names an
intermediate place ; a relation in a GM block or a closed section does not count for a player.
The « gone » mark is public like PJ/PNJ : it says nothing the sheet's title does not already say.

**Bouchons.** None new : the stub seed (`src/bouchon/depart.ts`) gains what the criteria play —
« Maître Aldric » « se situe à » Port-Sel (habite) from « Apparence » and « membre de » « La Guilde
des Brumes » (trésorier) ; « Le Prieur masqué » « se situe à » Port-Sel (se cache) from « Secret — MJ
seul », and dead (`disparu`) ; « Le Phare aux Sels » intact (the « Marquer détruit » criterion ruins it) ; « Suie » « se situe à » « Les Docks » (a sub-place of Port-Sel) ; « Dame Ombeline de
Val-Fortin » « membre de » « La Guilde des Brumes » (dirige) ; the family « Les Corvalis » with « Bran
Corvalis » (aîné, alive : the « Marquer mort » criterion kills him) and « Dame Ombeline de
Val-Fortin » (veuve) as members — every one of these relations from the character's « Apparence »,
read by the players, so that Léa's criteria (E-9, E-17) hold ; the Prieur's alone from « Secret — MJ
seul » ; the
three former free relations of Port-Sel keep their text as qualifier.

### Slice `kanevas-fiches-liees-bloc` — design notes (2026-10-26)

Detail of the slice's part of AD-130 and B-64 / B-69; nothing here adds a nature, a type, a screen or a column. Repository
facts read on `epic/kanevas-fiches-liees` and its voisines: `mentions.ts` (the model of a read-only backwards lookup under
the two guards), `descendantsDe` (point 3 of the `-recherche` notes), the sheet-block registry of AD-124 delivered by
`kanevas-scenarios-suivi` (`frontend/src/ecrans/fiche/blocs-fiche/`), the card thumbnail `vignette.tsx` and its
`MarqueDisparu` (`-disparu`).

1. **One service, read-only.** `liees(db, role, acteur, universId, ficheId, opts)` in `src/services/liees.ts`, beside
   `mentions.ts`; only `services/` reads `relations` (AD-2). `opts = {nature?, descendants?, apres?}`. The route
   `GET …/fiches/:fid/liees` (inside the guarded scope, `?mode=joueur` as the other reads) first checks that the reader reads
   `:fid` (`peutVoirFiche`; else `introuvable`, like `…/mentions`) and validates `nature` (a key of `natures.ts`),
   `descendants` (exactly `1`), `curseur` (`^\d{1,9}$`, an offset) — `invalide('Requête invalide.')` otherwise.
2. **The groups.** One group per nature of the list, **in the list's order**, whose target types include the sheet's type
   (a symmetric nature also when the sheet is a source type): `{nature, libelle, types, fiches, suivant}`. `libelle` is
   the nature read from the target (`libelleCible`; a symmetric nature's `libelleSource`). `types` are the types of the
   linked sheets in the order of the list of types, each `{type, total, totalAvecDescendants}`: `total` counts the sheets
   linked to the sheet itself, `totalAvecDescendants` those linked to it **or to one of its descendants**
   (`descendantsDe`, the set including the sheet). The descent is for a **place** only (AD-130): for any other sheet
   `totalAvecDescendants` equals `total` and `descendants=1` is accepted and changes nothing — no toggle, no « Rien à ce niveau »
   outside a place. The answer also carries `profondeurAtteinte` (boolean, top level): true when the walk met the 50-level bound
   (`descendantsDe` reports it; if the `-recherche` function does not yet, this slice adds the flag to its result, not a second walk) A sheet is counted **once** per group and type, however many
   relations bind it. Both totals are always sent (the screen decides from them, AD-130 (1)); a group with
   `totalAvecDescendants = 0` for every type is absent, and so is the whole list (`groupes: []`, the block absent) — an empty
   answer for a sheet that is nobody's target, hidden or not.
3. **The relation counted** is the one of `-recherche` point 2, read in the same words: a row of `relations` whose target is
   the sheet (or a descendant) and whose `nature` is the group's, **under the two guards of AD-64** — the section of the
   row readable by the reader (`sqlSectionLisible`), the source sheet readable (`sqlFicheLisible`), **and the target readable**
   (a relation to a hidden sub-place counts for nobody who cannot read that place). A symmetric nature is also read from the
   side where the sheet is the source (stored once, `-natures` point 3); the "other end" is then the line. The count and the
   lines come from the **same** query: a number never exceeds what the lines list (no count of a hidden sheet, no placeholder).
   The group's `libelle` and a type are shown only because at least one readable relation exists.
4. **The lines.** `fiches` of a group hold the first five lines (the screen's folding), each the E-8 line
   (`{id, titre, type, sousType, charge, illustration…}`, the same shape as `listerFiches` so that the screen reuses the card)
   plus `qualificatif` (the qualifiers of the readable relations that bind it, de-duplicated, joined by « · »; empty omitted)
   and `via?: {id, titre}` with the rule of `-recherche` point 1 (a direct link wins; the first descendant target by title then
   id; readable, so never a hidden or intermediate place). Order: title (nocase) then id. `descendants` absent: no `via`, and
   the counts are `total`. `suivant` is the next `curseur` or `null`. `?nature=` limits the answer to that group and returns
   up to **50** lines after `curseur` (the page behind « Afficher les N »); without `nature`, the five-line groups of all
   natures. The route sends the totals of both modes in every answer, whatever `descendants` says; `descendants=1` only
   changes which sheets the lines list.
5. **« Mentionnée dans » (B-69).** `mentionsDe` stops listing the **natured** relations (`nature <> ''` is dropped from the
   `relations` branch of `src`); the links and the free relations stay; the page counts follow the same filter. Natured relations
   appear in the block of the target, and, for a symmetric nature, of both ends.
6. **The block.** A sheet block (AD-124) `frontend/src/ecrans/fiche/blocs-fiche/liees.tsx`, served to the types that are the target
   of a nature (`lieu`, `faction`, `famille`, `objet`, `vaisseau`, plus `personnage` for the symmetric natures — the list is
   copied in `liees.tsx` from `natures.ts`: a new nature on another type needs it extended there) with `rang: 90, place: 'bas'`: « Mentionnée dans » is not a registry block (`fiche.tsx` draws it after the `bas` blocks), so the order comes from the slot, not from the rank; every role; the slot where the registry draws sheet blocks relative
   to the sections is AD-124 (1)'s and is not moved here, « before Mentionnée dans » holds in either slot. It loads its own
   answer (`GET …/liees`), not carried by `lireFiche`: states « Chargement… » (kept until its data are read **and** the sheet types have arrived, `blocEnAttente`, so a group title is never composed with the raw type; types in error: the block shows, falling back on the raw type), « Impossible de charger les fiches liées. » with
   « Réessayer » (`docs/ecrans.md`), the sheet readable meanwhile. `groupes: []` renders nothing (not even the heading).
